# MCP 2026-07-28 gap analysis (raw, machine-generated)

Generated from `mcp-2026-07-28-gap-analysis.json` (7 spec-area gap reports, 4 candidate plans, 2 judge reports). Severity: blocker = modern clients cannot work or a spec MUST is violated; major = MUST or important SHOULD; minor = SHOULD; optional = MAY feature. The curated plan is in `../mcp-2026-07-28-implementation-plan.md`.


## Base protocol, versioning, statelessness, server/discover, error codes, dual-era rules (JSON-RPC layer: MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor, MCPServer.CoreManager, MCPServer.Types, MCPServer.ManagerRegistry, and how MCPServer.IdHTTPServer / MCPServer.StdioTransport call ProcessRequest)

Requirements extracted: 30. Gaps: 22.


### GAP-BP-01 [blocker, effort L] No per-request _meta processing and no era detection; modern requests are silently served as legacy

- Requirements: BP-10, BP-09, BP-19, BP-13
- Current: TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:146-224) extracts only `id`, `method` and `params` (lines 166-188) and dispatches by method name (line 190). `params._meta` is never read; `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion` and `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities` are neither required nor validated; there is no notion of era. A 2026-07-28 client sending `tools/list` with `_meta` gets a legacy-shaped result (no resultType); a client sending an unknown version (e.g. `1900-01-01`) gets a normal result instead of -32022. Both transports pass the body straight through (IdHTTPServer.pas:488,522; StdioTransport.pas:64).
- Required: BP-10: required `_meta` fields "MUST be included on every request"; missing → -32602 (+HTTP 400). BP-09: unsupported `protocolVersion` → -32022 with `data.supported`/`data.requested` (+HTTP 400). BP-19: "A request carrying modern per-request `_meta` is served statelessly according to this revision. An `initialize` request selects legacy semantics."
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: none — legacy requests never carry `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion`, so they keep taking the legacy path unchanged. Library consumers: `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.Create(Registry)` and `ProcessRequest(Body, SessionID): string` keep their signatures; custom managers keep receiving `(Method, Params)` with `_meta` still present inside Params (as today).
- Suggested change: Add unit MCPServer.Protocol.Context (or extend MCPServer.Types): `TMCPProtocolEra = (peLegacy, peModern)`; `TMCPRequestContext` (record or class) with Era, ProtocolVersion, ClientCapabilities: TJSONObject (borrowed, may be nil), ClientInfo: TJSONObject, LogLevel: string, ProgressToken: TValue, Method, RequestID, plus transport hints (HeaderProtocolVersion, HeaderMethod, HeaderName). Constants in MCPServer.Types: `MCP_MODERN_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS: array[0..0] of string = ('2026-07-28')`, `MCP_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS = ('2025-03-26','2025-06-18','2025-11-25')`, `MCP_LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION = '2026-07-28'`, `MCP_LATEST_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSION = '2025-11-25'`, `MCP_META_PROTOCOL_VERSION = 'io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion'`, `MCP_META_CLIENT_CAPABILITIES`, `MCP_META_CLIENT_INFO`, `MCP_META_LOG_LEVEL`, `MCP_META_SERVER_INFO`. In ProcessRequest add `class function BuildRequestContext(Method, Params, Hints): TMCPRequestContext`: (1) Method = 'initialize' → peLegacy; (2) else if Params has `_meta` (TJSONObject) with a string `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion` → peModern: if value not in MCP_MODERN_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS → raise EMCPError.UnsupportedProtocolVersion(Requested, Supported); if `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities` absent or not TJSONObject → raise EMCPError.InvalidParams('Missing required _meta field io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities'); (3) else if Hints.HeaderProtocolVersion is a modern version (HTTP) → the body lacks required `_meta` → raise EMCPError.InvalidParams (-32602; transport maps to 400); (4) else → peLegacy. Keep the context for the duration of the call (pass to managers, see GAP-BP-13) and use it for result post-processing (GAP-BP-03/04) and status mapping (GAP-BP-05).

### GAP-BP-02 [blocker, effort M] server/discover not implemented

- Requirements: BP-14, BP-04, BP-12, BP-25
- Current: TMCPCoreManager.HandlesMethod (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:48-53) only handles `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`, `ping`; `server/discover` therefore falls to TMCPManagerRegistry.GetManagerForMethod → nil → "Method [server/discover] not found" → -32601 (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:121-123, 214-215). Capabilities are hard-coded in Initialize (CoreManager.pas:103-124) and cannot be reused.
- Required: BP-14: "Servers MUST implement it." Result must contain resultType:"complete", supportedVersions[], capabilities{}, ttlMs (>=0), cacheScope ("public"|"private"), SHOULD `_meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo`, MAY `instructions`.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients unaffected. Library consumers: TMCPCoreManager.Create(ASettings) stays; a new optional `ManagerRegistry` property/overloaded constructor is additive. Consumers who registered their own core manager must add `server/discover` themselves (document in README).
- Suggested change: In TMCPCoreManager: add 'server/discover' to HandlesMethod/ExecuteMethod; implement `function Discover(const Ctx: TMCPRequestContext): TValue` building `{"resultType":"complete","supportedVersions":[...MCP_MODERN_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS (+ optionally MCP_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS)],"capabilities":BuildServerCapabilities,"instructions":FSettings.Instructions (omit if ''),"ttlMs":FSettings.DiscoverTtlMs (default 3600000),"cacheScope":"public","_meta":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo":{"name":FSettings.ServerName,"version":FSettings.ServerVersion}}}`. Factor `BuildServerCapabilities: TJSONObject` out of Initialize so both `initialize` and `server/discover` share it (see GAP-BP-08 for deriving it from the registry). Add TMCPSettings.Instructions, DiscoverTtlMs, EmitServerInfo (ini section [Server]). Ensure `params` may be nil/only `_meta`.

### GAP-BP-03 [blocker, effort S] Results never carry `resultType` (required on every modern result)

- Requirements: BP-04, BP-03
- Current: ProcessRequest adds the manager's TJSONObject verbatim as `result` (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:192-200); no manager (CoreManager.pas:99-135, ToolsManager.pas:213-257, ResourcesManager.pas:93-198) emits `resultType`.
- Required: BP-04: "The `result` MUST include a `resultType` field"; "Servers implementing this protocol version MUST include this field."
- Compat impact: Legacy results stay byte-identical if injection is gated on Ctx.Era = peModern (legacy `Result` allows extra members, so injecting always would also be harmless). Library consumers' custom managers get `resultType` for free without code changes.
- Suggested change: In TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest, after ExecuteMethodCall and when Ctx.Era = peModern and the result is a TJSONObject: `if ResultObj.GetValue('resultType') = nil then ResultObj.AddPair('resultType', 'complete')`. Managers that later implement MRTR set `resultType: "input_required"` themselves and the injector leaves it alone. Also apply to the `{}` result produced for TValue.Empty (GAP-BP-11).

### GAP-BP-05 [blocker, effort M] HTTP status is always 200; modern error→status mapping (400 / 404) impossible with the current string-returning ProcessRequest

- Requirements: BP-08, BP-09, BP-10, BP-11, BP-15, BP-19
- Current: ProcessRequest returns only the JSON body (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:24,146). HandlePostRequestJSON sets `ResponseInfo.ResponseNo := HTTP_OK` unconditionally (IdHTTPServer.pas:553) and HandlePostRequestSSE likewise (IdHTTPServer.pas:509); the only non-200 paths are 202/204 for notifications (lines 346, 526), 404 for wrong path (189), 405 (202), 403 CORS (247). No 400 constant exists (lines 82-89).
- Required: Modern HTTP: -32602 for missing required `_meta` → 400 (BP-10); -32022 → 400 (BP-09); -32021 → 400 (BP-11); -32020 → 400 (BP-08); -32601 → 404 (BP-15). Legacy HTTP: JSON-RPC errors are carried with 200 (404 = session terminated, 400 = bad/unsupported MCP-Protocol-Version header per BP-22/BP-26).
- Compat impact: Keep `ProcessRequest(const RequestBody, SessionID: string): string` as a thin wrapper so existing library transports compile. Legacy clients keep receiving 200 + JSON-RPC error. Dual-era clients rely on the 400 body to distinguish modern errors from legacy servers, so the mapping must be exact.
- Suggested change: Add `TMCPProcessResult = record Body: string; HttpStatus: Integer; IsNotification: Boolean; Era: TMCPProtocolEra; ErrorCode: Integer; end;` and `TMCPTransportHints = record HeaderProtocolVersion, HeaderMethod, HeaderName: string; HasHeaders: Boolean; end;`. New `function ProcessRequestEx(const RequestBody: string; const Hints: TMCPTransportHints): TMCPProcessResult`; old ProcessRequest calls it and returns Body. Status policy inside the processor (single source of truth): Era=peModern: ErrorCode in [-32020, -32021, -32022] → 400; -32602 raised during context/meta validation → 400; -32601 → 404; other errors and all successes → 200. Era=peLegacy: always 200 (except transport-level header checks). Add HTTP_BAD_REQUEST = 400 to IdHTTPServer.pas and set `ResponseInfo.ResponseNo := Res.HttpStatus` in HandlePostRequestJSON/SSE (for SSE responses with an error status, prefer JSON body over SSE framing). Stdio ignores HttpStatus.

### GAP-BP-04 [major, effort S] `_meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo` not included in results

- Requirements: BP-12
- Current: No result carries `_meta`; serverInfo only appears in the legacy InitializeResult (CoreManager.pas:128-131). TMCPJsonRpcProcessor has no access to TMCPSettings (Create takes only the registry, JsonRpcProcessor.pas:38-42).
- Required: BP-12: "Servers SHOULD include ... `io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo` in every result's `_meta`, unless specifically configured not to do so" (Implementation requires `name` and `version`).
- Compat impact: Modern-only injection leaves legacy responses unchanged. Additive constructor overload keeps `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.Create(ManagerRegistry)` compiling for library consumers (they simply get no serverInfo unless they set the new property).
- Suggested change: Add `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.Create(ManagerRegistry; Settings: TMCPSettings)` overload and/or a `ServerInfo: TJSONObject`/`ServerName`/`ServerVersion` property plus `EmitServerInfo: Boolean` (default True). TMCPIdHTTPServer.Start (IdHTTPServer.pas:144) and TMCPStdioTransport.Create (StdioTransport.pas:34) pass their settings when available. In ProcessRequest, when Ctx.Era = peModern and EmitServerInfo: get-or-create `_meta` object on the result and add `io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo` = {name, version (+ title/description/websiteUrl when configured)} if not already present.

### GAP-BP-06 [major, effort M] Error responses cannot carry `data`, and error codes are inferred from exception message text

- Requirements: BP-05, BP-07, BP-09, BP-11, BP-15
- Current: CreateErrorResponse(RequestID, ErrorCode, ErrorMessage) has no `data` parameter (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:128-144). The code is chosen by `Pos('not found', E.Message) > 0 → -32601` else -32603 (lines 211-215): any exception whose text contains 'not found' (e.g. TMCPRegistry.CreateTool 'Tool not found: %s' at Registration.pas:76, 'SSL Certificate file not found' at IdHTTPServer.pas:370, a consumer's own messages) is reported as Method-not-found; managers cannot signal -32602 or any MCP code. Managers raise plain `Exception` (CoreManager.pas:67, ToolsManager.pas:76, ResourcesManager.pas:75).
- Required: BP-09/BP-11 need `error.data.supported`/`requested` and `error.data.requiredCapabilities`; BP-07 requires defined codes to be used only with their meanings; BP-15 -32601 only for unknown methods.
- Compat impact: Additive: plain exceptions from custom tools/managers still map to -32603 (and the 'not found' heuristic can be retained as a fallback for non-EMCPError exceptions so existing consumer behaviour is unchanged). New EMCPError class is opt-in for consumers.
- Suggested change: New unit MCPServer.Protocol.Errors (or in MCPServer.Types): `EMCPError = class(Exception) Code: Integer; Data: TJSONValue (owned); constructor Create(ACode: Integer; const AMessage: string; AData: TJSONValue = nil); class function MethodNotFound(const Method: string): EMCPError; class function InvalidParams(const Msg: string; Data: TJSONValue = nil): EMCPError; class function InvalidRequest(...): EMCPError; class function UnsupportedProtocolVersion(const Requested: string; const Supported: TArray<string>): EMCPError (data {supported:[...], requested}); class function MissingRequiredClientCapability(RequiredCapabilities: TJSONObject): EMCPError (data {requiredCapabilities}); class function HeaderMismatch(const Msg: string): EMCPError;`. Constants: MCP_ERROR_HEADER_MISMATCH = -32020, MCP_ERROR_MISSING_REQUIRED_CLIENT_CAPABILITY = -32021, MCP_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION = -32022 (keep JSONRPC_* names). In ProcessRequest: `on E: EMCPError do CreateErrorResponse(ID, E.Code, E.Message, E.Data.Clone)` before the generic handler; ExecuteMethodCall raises EMCPError.MethodNotFound; managers' `else raise` branches raise EMCPError.MethodNotFound. Add `Data: TJSONValue = nil` parameter to CreateErrorResponse.

### GAP-BP-07 [major, effort S] Legacy initialize ignores the client's requested protocolVersion and always answers 2025-06-18

- Requirements: BP-21, BP-22, BP-20
- Current: TMCPCoreManager.Initialize never reads `Params.protocolVersion`; it always writes `MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION` ('2025-06-18', Types.pas:11) into the result (CoreManager.pas:101). A client asking for 2025-11-25 or 2025-03-26 is answered 2025-06-18 even though the dual-era server is meant to serve those revisions.
- Required: BP-21: "If the server supports the requested protocol version, it MUST respond with the same version. Otherwise, the server MUST respond with another protocol version it supports. This SHOULD be the latest version supported by the server."
- Compat impact: Clients requesting 2025-06-18 see identical output. Clients requesting 2025-11-25 now get 2025-11-25 (they then may use 2025-11-25 features such as tasks/elicitation url mode that this server does not advertise — fine, capabilities gate them). `MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION` constant is public (also used by Resources/MCPServer.Resource.Project.pas:101): keep it (value unchanged, mark deprecated in a comment) and add the new constants so consumer code compiles.
- Suggested change: In Initialize: `Requested := Params.GetValue<string>('protocolVersion', '')`; `if MatchStr(Requested, MCP_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS) then Negotiated := Requested else Negotiated := MCP_LATEST_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSION` ('2025-11-25'); write Negotiated. A request naming a modern version (2026-07-28) or garbage also gets MCP_LATEST_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSION (initialize can only establish a legacy revision). Record `Negotiated` in the request context/response so the transport can (optionally) validate the legacy `MCP-Protocol-Version` header on later requests (BP-22).

### GAP-BP-09 [major, effort S] FSessionID is a single unsynchronised field shared by every client; session id echoed unchecked; sessions minted in the manager instead of the transport

- Requirements: BP-13, BP-26, BP-19
- Current: CoreManager.pas:17 `FSessionID: string` is overwritten on every initialize (:97) from whichever Indy worker thread handles the request (TIdHTTPServer is thread-per-connection; IsMultiThread set in MCPServer.dpr:170) — a data race on a Delphi string and semantically client B's initialize replaces client A's id. The value is never consulted for authorisation: IdHTTPServer echoes any incoming `Mcp-Session-Id` (:349, :486, :549) and never returns 404/400 for unknown/missing ids. In the modern era the header must be ignored entirely (BP-13).
- Required: BP-13 (modern): no protocol-level sessions, do not mint or echo session ids. BP-26 (legacy): minting is MAY; if minted, the server must be able to recognise it (404 after termination). Shared mutable state must be thread-safe under Indy.
- Compat impact: `TMCPCoreManager.SessionID` property stays (returns '' — document as deprecated). Legacy clients that never received an `Mcp-Session-Id` simply do not send one (spec-conformant). Consumers who relied on the echoed header get no header in modern era and (option A) none in legacy era either.
- Suggested change: Option A (recommended, stateless in both eras): delete the FSessionID write in Initialize, drop `sessionId` from the body, remove the Pos('"sessionId"') scraping (IdHTTPServer.pas:533-547) and stop echoing/minting `Mcp-Session-Id` (:349,:486,:549); for modern-era requests ignore the header per streamable-http.mdx § Earlier Streamable HTTP Revisions. Option B (if legacy sessions are wanted): move session minting into TMCPIdHTTPServer: `TMCPSessionStore` (TDictionary<string, TMCPSession> guarded by TMonitor or TCriticalSection; TMCPSession{ProtocolVersion, CreatedAt}), mint on legacy initialize (TGuid → visible ASCII), validate on legacy requests (unknown → 404, missing → 400 only if `RequireSession` setting), DELETE → remove; never touch it for modern requests. In both options CoreManager becomes state-free.

### GAP-BP-10 [major, effort S] `id: null` / non-string-non-integer ids are treated as notifications; `jsonrpc` and `method` are not validated (-32600 never emitted)

- Requirements: BP-02, BP-06, BP-01
- Current: ExtractRequestID returns TValue.Empty for TJSONNull, TJSONBool, objects, arrays (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:82-87) and ProcessRequest treats Empty as "notification" and returns nothing (:174-181), so `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"method":"tools/list"}` gets no response at all. Fractional numeric ids are truncated via AsInt64 (:83). `jsonrpc` is never checked; a missing/non-string `method` yields '' (:168-171) which is reported as -32601 rather than -32600. JSONRPC_INVALID_REQUEST is defined (:29) but never used.
- Required: BP-02: id "MUST NOT be null" and MUST be string or integer; schema InvalidRequestError (-32600) covers "missing required fields like `jsonrpc` or `method`, or using invalid types for these fields".
- Compat impact: Only malformed messages change behaviour (they now receive -32600 instead of silence/-32601). Legacy and modern clients unaffected; library consumers unaffected.
- Suggested change: Change ExtractRequestID to return a tri-state: `function ExtractRequestID(JSONRequest: TJSONObject; out RequestID: TValue): TMCPIdState` where TMCPIdState = (idAbsent, idValid, idInvalid). idAbsent → notification path; idInvalid (null, bool, object, array, non-integer number) → CreateErrorResponse(TValue.Empty → id null, JSONRPC_INVALID_REQUEST, 'Request id must be a string or integer'). Validate `jsonrpc = "2.0"` (TJSONString) and `method` is a non-empty TJSONString, else -32600 with the request's id when readable. Add `params` type check: present but not an object → -32602.

### GAP-BP-13 [major, effort M] Managers (built-in and consumer-provided) have no access to the request context (era, protocol version, client capabilities, logLevel)

- Requirements: BP-11, BP-17, BP-10, BP-13
- Current: IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod(const Method: string; const Params: TJSONObject) (Types.pas:39-44) is the only entry point; the processor passes only Method and Params (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:125). Managers cannot know whether to emit modern fields, cannot raise -32021 based on declared client capabilities, and cannot read logLevel/progressToken except by re-parsing `params._meta` themselves.
- Required: BP-11: "A server MUST NOT rely on capabilities the client has not declared" (requires the manager to see `clientCapabilities`); BP-17: logLevel gating; BP-10/BP-13: per-request metadata is the only context source.
- Compat impact: Purely additive: existing `IMCPCapabilityManager` implementations keep working (processor falls back to ExecuteMethod). Consumers opt in by implementing the new interface or reading the thread-local context.
- Suggested change: Add to MCPServer.Types: `IMCPCapabilityManagerEx = interface(IMCPCapabilityManager) ['{new GUID}'] function ExecuteMethodEx(const Context: TMCPRequestContext; const Method: string; const Params: TJSONObject): TValue; end;` Processor: `if Supports(Manager, IMCPCapabilityManagerEx, Ex) then Result := Ex.ExecuteMethodEx(Ctx, Method, Params) else Result := Manager.ExecuteMethod(Method, Params)`. Additionally expose `class function TMCPRequestContext.Current: PMCPRequestContext` backed by a `threadvar` set/cleared by the processor around the call, so unchanged managers/tools (TMCPToolBase<T>.Execute) can query Era/ClientCapabilities without signature changes. Provide helper `TMCPRequestContext.RequireClientCapability(const Path: string)` that raises EMCPError.MissingRequiredClientCapability (-32021) when the capability is absent from `ClientCapabilities`.

### GAP-BP-15 [major, effort M] No HeaderMismatch (-32020) support: MCP-Protocol-Version / Mcp-Method / Mcp-Name headers are never read or validated

- Requirements: BP-08, BP-22
- Current: HandlePostRequest reads only `Mcp-Session-Id` and `Accept` (IdHTTPServer.pas:333-337); CORS allow-list mentions `Mcp-Protocol-Version` (:260) but the value is never used. No -32020 constant exists (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:27-32, IdHTTPServer.pas:94-99). Legacy rule BP-22 ("invalid or unsupported MCP-Protocol-Version → 400") is also not enforced.
- Required: BP-08: header presence and header/body equality checks with 400 + -32020 for modern requests; BP-22: 400 for invalid/unsupported legacy header. Validation must be era-aware: legacy requests carry no Mcp-Method/Mcp-Name and must not be rejected for their absence.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: unaffected provided the checks are applied only to modern-era requests (header names a modern version or body carries modern `_meta`); missing header + no `_meta` continues to mean 2025-03-26/legacy as allowed by the MAY clause. Library consumers using TMCPIdHTTPServer inherit the behaviour; consumers with their own transport keep using ProcessRequest (no header checks). Detailed Mcp-Name Base64 sentinel decoding and Mcp-Param-* belong to the transport area.
- Suggested change: Populate TMCPTransportHints from RequestInfo.RawHeaders (`MCP-Protocol-Version`, `Mcp-Method`, `Mcp-Name`) in HandlePostRequest and pass to ProcessRequestEx. In the processor, after era detection, for peModern: (a) Hints.HasHeaders and HeaderProtocolVersion = '' → EMCPError.HeaderMismatch('Missing MCP-Protocol-Version header'); (b) HeaderProtocolVersion <> Ctx.ProtocolVersion → HeaderMismatch; (c) HeaderMethod = '' or <> Method → HeaderMismatch; (d) Method in ('tools/call','resources/read','prompts/get'): decode `=?base64?...?=` sentinel and compare with params.name / params.uri → HeaderMismatch. For peLegacy: if HeaderProtocolVersion <> '' and not in MCP_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS (and not modern) → 400 with -32600 body (BP-22). Order of checks: header presence/mismatch (-32020) before version support (-32022) so that a body/header disagreement is reported as mismatch.

### GAP-BP-08 [minor, effort M] Legacy capability object uses undefined keys, embeds a non-standard `sessionId`, and is hard-coded regardless of registered managers

- Requirements: BP-25, BP-20, BP-14
- Current: CoreManager.pas:106-114 emits `tools: {supportsProgress:false, supportsCancellation:false}` (not defined in any ServerCapabilities schema; the defined sub-key is `listChanged`); :116-124 emits `resources: {subscribe:false, listChanged:false}` (fine); :126 adds `sessionId` to the InitializeResult body (not a schema member; the HTTP layer then scrapes it with `Pos('"sessionId"', ResponseBody)` at IdHTTPServer.pas:533-547). Capabilities are static: a consumer registering only TMCPToolsManager still advertises `resources`; a consumer adding a prompts manager cannot advertise `prompts` (violates "Servers that support prompts MUST declare the prompts capability").
- Required: BP-25: capability sub-keys are `listChanged` / `subscribe`; capabilities MUST reflect what the server actually supports; session id belongs in the `Mcp-Session-Id` header only (BP-26).
- Compat impact: schema.json has no `additionalProperties:false` on these objects, so removal of the undefined keys cannot break conformant clients; no known client reads `result.sessionId`. Library consumers: additive interface; existing managers without it fall back to a name-based mapping so behaviour is preserved.
- Suggested change: Emit `tools: {"listChanged": false}` (or `{}`), `resources: {"subscribe": false, "listChanged": false}`; drop `sessionId` from the body. Derive capabilities from the registry: new optional interface in MCPServer.Types `IMCPCapabilityDeclaration = interface ['{...}'] function GetCapabilityDeclaration: TJSONObject; end;` implemented by TMCPToolsManager/TMCPResourcesManager; TMCPCoreManager gets `property ManagerRegistry: IMCPManagerRegistry` (set from TMCPIdHTTPServer.Start / TMCPStdioTransport.Create using the already-present but unused CoreManager fields, IdHTTPServer.pas:41,67; StdioTransport.pas:17,33, or by the consumer). Add `IMCPManagerEnumerator` (`function GetManagers: TArray<IMCPCapabilityManager>`) implemented by TMCPManagerRegistry rather than changing IMCPManagerRegistry (consumers may implement that interface). BuildServerCapabilities: for each manager: if Supports(IMCPCapabilityDeclaration) use it; else map GetCapabilityName in ['tools','resources','prompts','completions','logging'] → `{}`; 'core' → nothing. If no registry is assigned, fall back to today's static tools+resources object.

### GAP-BP-11 [minor, effort S] Responses may omit `result` or carry a non-object `result`

- Requirements: BP-03, BP-04
- Current: When a manager returns TValue.Empty for a request that has an id (e.g. an `id`-bearing `notifications/initialized`, CoreManager.pas:59-63) the response is `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1}` with no `result` (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:192-200). When a manager returns a string, `result` becomes a JSON string (:196-199), not an object.
- Required: BP-03: "Result responses MUST include a `result` field" and the result is an object ("MAY follow any JSON object structure"); BP-04 needs an object to hold `resultType`.
- Compat impact: Built-in managers always return TJSONObject, so no change for them. A consumer's custom manager returning a string would today produce invalid MCP; wrapping keeps compiling but changes its wire output — log a warning and document.
- Suggested change: In ProcessRequest: Empty → `result := TJSONObject.Create` (then modern injection adds resultType/_meta). String → if it parses as a JSON object, use the parsed object; otherwise wrap as `{"value": <string>}` and TLogger.Warning('Manager returned non-object result for %s'). Other TValue kinds → same wrapping via ToString.

### GAP-BP-12 [minor, effort S] Legacy-only methods (`ping`, `initialize`, `notifications/initialized`) are served regardless of era

- Requirements: BP-16, BP-15, BP-19
- Current: TMCPCoreManager.HandlesMethod/ExecuteMethod (CoreManager.pas:48-68) answer `ping` and `initialize` without any era information; a modern request `ping` with `_meta` gets `{}` (no resultType), and `initialize` carrying modern `_meta` is treated as a legacy handshake.
- Required: BP-16: ping / initialize / notifications/initialized / logging/setLevel / resources/subscribe / resources/unsubscribe are not part of 2026-07-28; BP-15: unknown method → -32601 (+404 on HTTP) in the modern era. BP-19: `initialize` selects legacy semantics.
- Compat impact: Legacy ping/initialize untouched. Only clients that mix modern `_meta` with legacy methods are affected (non-conformant clients). Optional `LenientModernPing` setting can keep answering ping for tolerance.
- Suggested change: In TMCPJsonRpcProcessor after context building: `if (Ctx.Era = peModern) and MatchStr(Method, MCP_LEGACY_ONLY_METHODS) then raise EMCPError.MethodNotFound(Method)` where MCP_LEGACY_ONLY_METHODS = ('ping','initialize','logging/setLevel','resources/subscribe','resources/unsubscribe','roots/list'). Treat `initialize` itself as legacy before the modern check (BP-19), i.e. the era rule: method = 'initialize' → peLegacy wins even if `_meta` is present. Optionally `TMCPSettings.LenientModernPing` (default False) removes 'ping' from the list.

### GAP-BP-14 [minor, effort S] `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel` is not validated

- Requirements: BP-17
- Current: `_meta` is never parsed (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:185-190); an invalid level such as `"verbose"` is accepted silently. (The server never emits `notifications/message`, so the MUST NOT part is vacuously met.)
- Required: BP-17: "If the io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel value ... is not a recognized log level, the server SHOULD reject that request with ... -32602".
- Compat impact: None for legacy; modern clients sending an invalid level now get -32602 (+400 per GAP-BP-05 policy only if you choose to classify it as a `_meta` validation error; otherwise 200). Consumers unaffected.
- Suggested change: In BuildRequestContext: if `_meta` has 'io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel' and it is not a TJSONString whose value is in MCP_LOG_LEVELS = ('debug','info','notice','warning','error','critical','alert','emergency') → raise EMCPError.InvalidParams('Invalid io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel'). Store the level in Ctx.LogLevel for future use. Do not add `logging` to capabilities (feature is Deprecated).

### GAP-BP-17 [minor, effort S] Other shared mutable state is not thread-safe under Indy's thread-per-connection model

- Requirements: BP-13, BP-19
- Current: TServerStatusResource.IncrementRequestCount / ConnectionOpened / ConnectionClosed use plain Inc/Dec on class vars (Resources/MCPServer.Resource.Server.pas:95-109) and are called from every HTTP request (IdHTTPServer.pas:177-179,206). TMCPIdHTTPServer.GetNextEventID does a non-atomic `Inc(FEventIDCounter)` (IdHTTPServer.pas:416-420). TMCPRegistry.EnsureInitialized lazily creates class dictionaries without a lock (Registration.pas:42-49) — safe only because it is driven from unit `initialization` sections. TMCPManagerRegistry.FManagers (ManagerRegistry.pas:13) and the managers' TDictionary fields are read concurrently, which is safe only while nobody registers after Start.
- Required: A dual-era server "MAY serve both eras concurrently on the same endpoint"; serving concurrent stateless requests requires shared state to be race-free.
- Compat impact: None visible to clients or consumers.
- Suggested change: Use `AtomicIncrement/AtomicDecrement` (System.SyncObjs / intrinsic) for FRequestCount, FActiveConnections and FEventIDCounter (or remove GetNextEventID: SSE event ids are gone in 2026-07-28 and optional in legacy). Guard TMCPRegistry.EnsureInitialized with a class constructor (create dictionaries eagerly) instead of lazy checks. Document that RegisterManager/RegisterTool/RegisterResource must complete before TMCPIdHTTPServer.Start / TMCPStdioTransport.Run, or make TMCPManagerRegistry copy-on-write (TMultiReadExclusiveWriteSynchronizer) if runtime registration is a goal.

### GAP-BP-18 [minor, effort M] JSON-RPC batches are rejected with -32700, which conflicts with advertising 2025-03-26 as a supported legacy revision

- Requirements: BP-27, BP-21
- Current: ParseJSONRequest raises 'JSON-RPC request must be an object' for a TJSONArray body (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:52-56), which the handler turns into -32700 Parse error with id null (:211-217). IdHTTPServer.IsRequestOnlyNotificationsOrResponses does understand arrays (:453-465) but only for the 202 shortcut.
- Required: BP-27: 2025-03-26 "MUST support receiving JSON-RPC batches"; batching removed from 2025-06-18 onward.
- Compat impact: Decision-dependent. If 2025-03-26 is dropped from MCP_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS, an initialize for 2025-03-26 is answered with 2025-11-25 (allowed by BP-21) and no batch support is needed. If it is kept, batch handling is additive.
- Suggested change: Preferred: support only 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25 as legacy revisions (both batch-free) and answer 2025-03-26 requests with MCP_LATEST_LEGACY_PROTOCOL_VERSION. Alternative: in ProcessRequestEx, if the parsed body is a TJSONArray and Era = peLegacy: process each element with the single-message path, collect non-empty responses into a TJSONArray, return it (202/empty when all were notifications); reject arrays with -32600 for modern requests.

### GAP-BP-19 [minor, effort S] Error-code constants are duplicated and have no home for the new MCP codes

- Requirements: BP-07
- Current: JSONRPC_* constants are declared twice: JsonRpcProcessor.pas:27-32 and IdHTTPServer.pas:94-99. No -32020/-32021/-32022 constants anywhere.
- Required: BP-07: only spec-defined codes from -32020..-32099 with their exact meanings; implementation needs single authoritative definitions.
- Compat impact: Keep the existing identifiers (JSONRPC_PARSE_ERROR etc.) exported from a shared unit and re-exported/aliased where they were, so consumer code referencing `MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.JSONRPC_INTERNAL_ERROR` still compiles.
- Suggested change: Move the JSONRPC_* constants to MCPServer.Types (or new MCPServer.Protocol.Errors) and add MCP_ERROR_HEADER_MISMATCH = -32020, MCP_ERROR_MISSING_REQUIRED_CLIENT_CAPABILITY = -32021, MCP_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION = -32022; delete the duplicates in IdHTTPServer.pas (it already uses MCPServer.Types) and keep them in JsonRpcProcessor.pas as `= MCPServer.Types.JSONRPC_...` aliases for one release.

### GAP-BP-20 [minor, effort S] Version constants and documentation still describe a single-version 2025-06-18 server

- Requirements: BP-19, BP-20, BP-09
- Current: MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION = '2025-06-18' (Types.pas:11) is the only version constant; README.md:6,30 advertise 2025-06-18; Resources/MCPServer.Resource.Project.pas:101 reports 'MCP 2025-06-18'.
- Required: Dual-era: supported modern set ('2026-07-28') and legacy set must be explicit for `supportedVersions`, `data.supported`, initialize negotiation and header validation.
- Compat impact: Keep MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION for source compatibility (document as deprecated alias). README changes only.
- Suggested change: Add the constants from GAP-BP-01 to MCPServer.Types; make Resource.Project report `MCP_LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION` plus the legacy list; update README badge/Features with a 'Protocol versions & dual-era behaviour' section describing era detection, HTTP status mapping, and the library extension points (IMCPCapabilityManagerEx, IMCPCapabilityDeclaration, EMCPError).

### GAP-BP-16 [optional, effort S] No infrastructure to emit MissingRequiredClientCapability (-32021)

- Requirements: BP-11
- Current: The server never initiates sampling/elicitation/roots, so no request currently requires a client capability (vacuously conformant), but there is no way for a manager or consumer tool to raise the typed error with `data.requiredCapabilities` (CreateErrorResponse has no data, JsonRpcProcessor.pas:128-144).
- Required: BP-11: -32021 with `data.requiredCapabilities: ClientCapabilities` and HTTP 400.
- Compat impact: Additive only.
- Suggested change: Covered by EMCPError.MissingRequiredClientCapability (GAP-BP-06) + TMCPRequestContext.RequireClientCapability (GAP-BP-13) + 400 mapping (GAP-BP-05). Document usage in README for tools that will implement MRTR elicitation later.

### GAP-BP-21 [optional, effort S] No `instructions` and no extended Implementation metadata (title/description/websiteUrl) available for discover/initialize

- Requirements: BP-14, BP-12, BP-20
- Current: TMCPSettings (Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas:11-49) exposes only ServerName/ServerVersion; Initialize emits serverInfo{name,version} (CoreManager.pas:128-131); `instructions` is never emitted.
- Required: BP-14/BP-20: `instructions` optional; Implementation optional fields title, description, websiteUrl, icons.
- Compat impact: Additive settings with empty defaults; nothing emitted when unset.
- Suggested change: Add TMCPSettings.Instructions, ServerTitle, ServerDescription, ServerWebsiteUrl, DiscoverTtlMs (default 3600000), EmitServerInfo (default True) with ini keys under [Server]; use them in BuildServerInfo (shared by initialize result, discover `_meta`, and result `_meta` injection).

### GAP-BP-22 [optional, effort S] HTTP accepts client-sent JSON-RPC *responses* with 202 in the modern era; stdio has a dead duplicate error path

- Requirements: BP-06, BP-01
- Current: IsRequestOnlyNotificationsOrResponses returns True for bodies containing `result`/`error` (IdHTTPServer.pas:448-449) → 202. Modern streamable-http: "The client MUST NOT send JSON-RPC responses" (server response unspecified). TMCPStdioTransport.Run has an except block emitting -32603 with id null (StdioTransport.pas:73-94) that is unreachable because ProcessRequest catches everything (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:204-219).
- Required: BP-06/BP-01 only loosely; in the modern era a response body is not a valid client message.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients may still POST responses (allowed in 2025-xx) — keep 202 for legacy era. Consumers unaffected.
- Suggested change: If the transport hints indicate a modern request (MCP-Protocol-Version header = modern version) and the body is a response object, return 400 with a JSON-RPC -32600 error without id; otherwise keep 202. Leave the stdio except block as a last-resort guard but note it in comments.

### Already conformant

- Unknown method → JSON-RPC -32601 for both eras: TMCPManagerRegistry.GetManagerForMethod returns nil (Core/MCPServer.ManagerRegistry.pas:45-58) → ExecuteMethodCall raises 'Method [%s] not found' (Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:121-123) → mapped to JSONRPC_METHOD_NOT_FOUND (:214-215). Only the HTTP status (404 in modern era) and the string-based mapping are missing (GAP-BP-05/06).
- Unparseable JSON → -32700 with `id: null` (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:48-50, 211-217; AddRequestIDToResponse :99-102) — matches JSON-RPC 2.0 parse-error semantics for both eras.
- Notifications (no `id`) receive no response (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:174-181) and the HTTP transport answers notification-only bodies with 202 Accepted and no body (Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:343-352) — BP-06 for both eras; `notifications/initialized` (legacy BP-23) and `notifications/cancelled` (modern BP-18 'MAY ignore') are accepted silently.
- Legacy `ping` returns an empty result object `{}` (Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:142-155) — BP-24.
- Legacy `initialize` returns `protocolVersion`, `capabilities`, `serverInfo{name,version}` (CoreManager.pas:99-135) — the InitializeResult required members of BP-20 are present (negotiation and capability content issues are GAP-BP-07/08).
- Response `id` preserves the request's type (string vs integer) (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:105-108) — BP-03/BP-05.
- Error objects always contain integer `code` and string `message` (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:136-139) — BP-05.
- The server never emits -32002, -32042, or any code in -32000..-32099; only -32700/-32601/-32603 are produced (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:27-32, 211-215) — BP-07 error-code policy is not violated today.
- The server never initiates JSON-RPC requests and the stdio transport writes only responses to stdout (Server/MCPServer.StdioTransport.pas:64-71; logging goes to stderr via TLogger.UseStdErr, MCPServer.dpr:162-163) — BP-18 and BP-30 (one message per line, no embedded newlines since TJSONObject.ToJSON emits compact JSON).
- The server never emits `notifications/message`, so the modern rule 'MUST NOT emit notifications/message for a request without logLevel' (BP-17) and 'MUST NOT rely on capabilities the client has not declared' (BP-11) hold vacuously; `logging/setLevel`, `notifications/roots/list_changed`, `resources/subscribe` are simply unknown methods (-32601), which is correct because `logging` is not advertised and `resources.subscribe` is advertised as false.
- Request handling does not depend on prior requests: FSessionID is written but never consulted, there is no 'initialized' gating, tools/resources are stateless per call (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:183-190; ToolsManager.pas:230-251; ResourcesManager.pas:124-177) — the core of BP-13 statelessness is already satisfied for modern clients once `_meta` is honoured.
- HTTP methods other than GET/POST/OPTIONS (including DELETE) return 405 Method Not Allowed (IdHTTPServer.pas:200-204) — legacy BP-26 'MAY respond with 405' and modern 'HTTP GET or DELETE ... 405' for DELETE.
- TMCPJsonRpcProcessor holds no per-request mutable state (only FManagerRegistry, JsonRpcProcessor.pas:15) so a single instance can be shared by all Indy worker threads (IdHTTPServer.pas:144); TMCPManagerRegistry/TMCPToolsManager/TMCPResourcesManager collections are populated before Start and only read afterwards; TLogger serialises writes with a critical section (Core/MCPServer.Logger.pas:165-198).
- Library surface that must be preserved is small and can be kept intact: IMCPCapabilityManager (3 methods, Types.pas:39-44), IMCPManagerRegistry (2 methods, :46-50), TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.Create/ProcessRequest, TMCPCoreManager.Create(Settings)/SessionID, TMCPIdHTTPServer.ManagerRegistry/CoreManager/Settings/Start/Stop, TMCPStdioTransport.Create(Registry, CoreManager)/Run, TMCPRegistry class API, TMCPToolBase<T>/TMCPToolBase<T,R>/TMCPResourceBase<T>, MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION — every suggested change above is additive (new overloads, new optional interfaces, new constants).

### Notes

- Era-detection algorithm for the dual-era server (single place, TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.BuildRequestContext): 1) method = 'initialize' → LEGACY (negotiate per BP-21; ignore any `_meta`); 2) params._meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion'] is a string → MODERN (validate version → -32022; require clientCapabilities → -32602; validate headers → -32020); 3) HTTP only: MCP-Protocol-Version header names a modern version but body has no `_meta` → MODERN malformed → -32602/400 (or -32020/400 as header/body mismatch — pick one and document; -32602 is what basic/index.mdx prescribes for a missing required field); 4) otherwise → LEGACY (header absent ⇒ assume 2025-03-26 per 2025-06-18+ transports, or the negotiated version). On stdio only steps 1, 2, 4 apply (no header layer).
- supportedVersions / data.supported content decision: versioning.mdx's example lists both a modern and a legacy version ("supported": ["2026-07-28", "2025-11-25"]). Listing legacy revisions is informative for dual-era clients (they know legacy needs `initialize`), but a modern-only client might naively retry with `_meta.protocolVersion = "2025-11-25"`; the server must then answer -32022 again (legacy versions are not servable via per-request `_meta`) or accept it leniently. Recommendation: `supportedVersions` = modern versions first, then legacy; `_meta` requests naming a legacy version → -32022 with the same list, message 'Legacy versions require the initialize handshake'.
- HTTP 404 has opposite meanings across eras: modern = 'method not found' (-32601 body); legacy = 'session terminated, re-initialize'. The status mapping therefore MUST be gated on the detected era, otherwise a legacy client receiving 404 for a typo'd method would loop on re-initialization.
- The `Pos('not found', E.Message)` heuristic (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:214) currently misclassifies tool/resource lookup failures and even SSL-file errors as -32601; replacing it with EMCPError is a prerequisite for correct -32602 (unknown tool / resource not found in 2026-07-28) handling in the tools/resources areas.
- Central injection strategy protects library consumers: because every response passes through TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest, `resultType`, `_meta.serverInfo`, and (for the six cacheable methods server/discover, tools/list, prompts/list, resources/list, resources/templates/list, resources/read) default `ttlMs: 0` / `cacheScope: "private"` can be added there when absent in modern-era results, so custom managers written against the old API automatically produce schema-valid 2026-07-28 results. Managers that know better (e.g. tools/list with ttlMs 3600000 / public) set the fields themselves and the injector leaves them alone. ttlMs/cacheScope for the list/read methods are owned by the tools/resources areas; only server/discover is in this area.
- Legacy sessions: the cleanest dual-era design is 'stateless in both eras' — 2025-06-18/2025-11-25 make `Mcp-Session-Id` a MAY, so not minting a session removes the FSessionID race, the body-scraping in IdHTTPServer.pas:533-547, and all 400/404 session obligations. If sessions are kept for legacy, they must live in the HTTP transport (per-session store with locking), never in a manager shared by stdio and HTTP.
- Modern `ping`: strictly it is an unimplemented method (-32601/404). Answering `{resultType:"complete"}` to a modern ping is tolerant but technically non-conformant; some transitional clients may still send it. Suggest a `LenientModernPing` setting defaulting to False and a test for both behaviours.
- Modern `initialize`: a modern-only client will never send it; a legacy client always will. If a future config flag disables legacy support, BP-29 says the -32601 error message SHOULD name the supported versions (e.g. 'initialize is not supported; this server speaks 2026-07-28 (stateless)'). Cheap to implement in the same EMCPError factory.
- Deprecated features (Roots, Sampling, Logging via notifications/message, DCR) are 'SHOULD NOT adopt' for new implementations — do not add `logging` capability, `logging/setLevel`, or `notifications/message` while upgrading; keep TLogger on stderr/file.
- Extensions: the server supports none; omit `capabilities.extensions` and ignore `clientCapabilities.extensions`. Custom `_meta` keys the server might add later must use a vendor prefix such as `com.gdksoftware/…` (reserved second labels `modelcontextprotocol`/`mcp`).
- Concurrency model reminder: TIdHTTPServer invokes HandleHTTPRequest on a worker thread per connection; the stdio transport is strictly sequential (Readln/Writeln loop). Anything reachable from IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod (custom tools, resources, TServerStatusResource counters, TMCPRegistry) must be reentrant. Long-lived `subscriptions/listen` on stdio would need a writer lock around Writeln(Output) — out of this area but the same lock should be introduced when the SSE/subscription work lands.
- Open question for the orchestrator: keep 2025-03-26 in the legacy set (requires JSON-RPC batch receive, GAP-BP-18) or answer such clients with 2025-11-25 (permitted by BP-21). Recommendation: drop 2025-03-26 from the advertised set but still accept its `initialize` (answering 2025-11-25).
- Open question: whether to validate the legacy `MCP-Protocol-Version` header at all (BP-22 'invalid or unsupported → 400'). Minimal compliant behaviour: reject only values that are neither in the legacy set nor in the modern set; treat absent as 2025-03-26/negotiated.
- Related non-area observations found while reading: ResourcesManager.ReadResource dereferences `Params` without a nil check (ResourcesManager.pas:134) → EAccessViolation → -32603 instead of -32602 when `params` is absent; ToolsManager returns 'Error: Tool not found' as an isError tool result instead of -32602 (ToolsManager.pas:247-248); legacy GET without `Accept: text/event-stream` returns a custom JSON info body with 200 (IdHTTPServer.pas:301-313) whereas 2025-11-25 transports require text/event-stream or 405. These belong to the tools/resources/transport areas but interact with the error-code work here.
- Suggested test matrix for this area (headless, JSON in/out via TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequestEx): legacy initialize for each of 2025-03-26/2025-06-18/2025-11-25/'garbage'/2026-07-28 → negotiated version; legacy ping; modern server/discover happy path (resultType, supportedVersions, capabilities, ttlMs>=0, cacheScope, _meta.serverInfo); modern tools/list with `_meta` → resultType + serverInfo; missing clientCapabilities → -32602/400; unknown version → -32022/400 with data.supported/requested; header/body version mismatch → -32020/400; unknown method modern → -32601/404 vs legacy → -32601/200; `id: null` → -32600; missing jsonrpc → -32600; modern ping → -32601 (or lenient); notification → no body/202; concurrent initialize from 50 threads → no AV (after FSessionID removal).

### Requirements

- **BP-01** (MUST, both): "All messages between MCP clients and servers MUST follow the JSON-RPC 2.0 specification." (identical sentence in 2025-06-18, 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Messages; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/index.mdx § Messages_
- **BP-02** (MUST NOT, both): "Requests MUST include a string or integer ID." "Unlike base JSON-RPC, the ID MUST NOT be null." Modern adds: "The request ID MUST NOT match the ID of any other request the sender has issued and not yet received a response for." (client obligation). A message with `id: null` or a non-string/non-integer id is therefore not a valid request and not a notification (notifications "MUST NOT include an ID"); the server should answer -32600 Invalid Request (schema InvalidRequestError: "missing required fields like `jsonrpc` or `method`, or using invalid types for these fields").  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Requests; 2025-11-25/basic/index.mdx § Requests_
- **BP-03** (MUST, both): "Result responses MUST include the same ID as the request they correspond to." "Result responses MUST include a `result` field." "The `result` MAY follow any JSON object structure." (schema: `result: Result` is an object type in every revision).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Result Responses; 2025-11-25/basic/index.mdx § Result Responses_
- **BP-04** (MUST, modern): "The `result` MUST include a `resultType` field to indicate the type of the result." Schema: "Servers implementing this protocol version MUST include this field." Values: "complete" for ordinary results, "input_required" for MRTR interim results; "A `resultType` of any value unrecognized by the client MUST be considered invalid." Clients treat an absent field as "complete" only for earlier-protocol servers, so legacy-era results need not carry it.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Result Responses / ResultType; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts `Result.resultType`; changelog.mdx Major change 8_
- **BP-05** (MUST, both): "Error responses MUST include the same ID as the request they correspond to (except in error cases where the ID could not be read due a malformed request)." "Error responses MUST include an `error` field with a `code` and `message`." "Error codes MUST be integers." "Error responses MAY include a `data` member with additional information of any type." Schema Error.message: "SHOULD be limited to a concise single sentence."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Error Responses; 2025-11-25/basic/index.mdx § Error Responses_
- **BP-06** (MUST NOT, both): "Notifications are sent from the client to the server or vice versa, as a one-way message. The receiver MUST NOT send a response." "Notifications MUST NOT include an ID." (Streamable HTTP, both eras: a POST whose body is a notification is answered with "HTTP status code 202 Accepted with no body" if accepted.)  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Notifications; 2025-11-25/basic/index.mdx § Notifications_
- **BP-07** (MUST NOT, modern): Error code allocation policy: "-32000 to -32019 — legacy ... New codes MUST NOT be allocated in this sub-range, and new implementations SHOULD NOT use codes from this sub-range at all." "-32020 to -32099 — reserved for the MCP specification ... Implementations MUST NOT emit any code from this sub-range that is not defined by this specification and MUST use defined codes only with their specified meanings." Defined: -32020 HeaderMismatch, -32021 MissingRequiredClientCapability, -32022 UnsupportedProtocolVersion. "Implementations of this protocol version MUST NOT emit these codes: -32002 — resource not found (2025-11-25 and earlier; replaced by -32602) ... -32042 — URL elicitation required." "New error codes for purposes not defined by this specification SHOULD be allocated outside the JSON-RPC reserved range (-32768 to -32000)."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Error Codes; schema.ts comment block "MCP error codes"; changelog.mdx Minor change 12_
- **BP-08** (MUST, modern): HTTP only. "Every POST request to the MCP endpoint MUST include an `MCP-Protocol-Version` header." "The header value MUST match the `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion` field carried in the request body's `_meta`. If the values do not match, the server MUST reject the request with `400 Bad Request` and a `HeaderMismatch` JSON-RPC error." `Mcp-Method` (all requests) and `Mcp-Name` (tools/call, resources/read, prompts/get) "are REQUIRED for compliance". "When rejecting a request due to header validation failure, servers MUST return HTTP status `400 Bad Request` and MUST include a JSON-RPC error response using ... `-32020`". Failure conditions: "A required standard header (MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name) is missing", value mismatch (after Base64 sentinel decode), invalid characters. Escape hatch for dual-era: "A server that supports clients implementing protocol versions earlier than 2025-06-18 ... MAY treat a request that omits the header as protocol version 2025-03-26."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx § Protocol Version Header, § Standard Request Headers, § Server Validation; schema.ts `HeaderMismatchError` / `HEADER_MISMATCH = -32020`; schema.ts `RequestMetaObject["io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion"]`_
- **BP-09** (MUST, modern): "If the server does not implement the requested version (whether the version is unknown to the server, or is a known version the server has chosen not to support), it MUST respond with an `UnsupportedProtocolVersionError` listing the versions it does support" — JSON `error.code = -32022`, `error.message = "Unsupported protocol version"`, `error.data.supported: string[]`, `error.data.requested: string` (both data members required by the schema). Schema: "For HTTP, the response status code MUST be `400 Bad Request`."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx § Protocol Version Negotiation; schema.ts `UnsupportedProtocolVersionError` / `UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION = -32022`; streamable-http.mdx § Protocol Version Header_
- **BP-10** (MUST, modern): "Client requests carry the following io.modelcontextprotocol/* fields in `_meta`; fields marked as required MUST be included on every request": `io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion` (string, required), `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities` (ClientCapabilities object, required; "an empty object means the client supports no optional capabilities"), `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo` (Implementation, optional), `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel` (LoggingLevel, optional). "A request missing any required field is malformed; the server MUST reject it with JSON-RPC error code `-32602` (Invalid params). On HTTP, the response status MUST be `400 Bad Request`." Servers use these "to identify the protocol version and capabilities in use without relying on any prior connection state."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § _meta → Per-request protocol fields; schema.ts `RequestMetaObject`, `RequestParams { _meta: RequestMetaObject }`_
- **BP-11** (MUST, modern): "A server MUST NOT rely on capabilities the client has not declared. If processing a request requires a capability the client did not include in `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities`, the server MUST return a `MissingRequiredClientCapabilityError` (`-32021`) whose `data.requiredCapabilities` lists the missing capabilities. On HTTP, the response status MUST be `400 Bad Request`." Schema: "Servers MUST NOT infer capabilities from prior requests."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § _meta → Per-request protocol fields; schema.ts `MissingRequiredClientCapabilityError` / `MISSING_REQUIRED_CLIENT_CAPABILITY = -32021`; schema.ts `RequestMetaObject["io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities"]`_
- **BP-12** (SHOULD, modern): "Servers SHOULD include the following io.modelcontextprotocol/* field in every result's `_meta`, unless specifically configured not to do so, to identify themselves without relying on any prior connection state": `io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo` of type `Implementation` ("requires `name` and `version`; other fields are optional": title, description, websiteUrl, icons). "`io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo` and `io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo` are self-reported ... Implementations SHOULD NOT use them to change the behavior ... and SHOULD NOT rely on them for security decisions."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § _meta → Per-response protocol fields; schema.ts `ResultMetaObject`; server/discover.mdx § DiscoverResult_
- **BP-13** (MUST NOT, modern): "Servers MUST NOT rely on prior requests over the same connection to establish context (e.g., capabilities, protocol version, client identity). Every request supplies this metadata in its `_meta` field." "Servers SHOULD be prepared to handle requests associated with multiple tasks, threads, or conversations." "Servers SHOULD NOT require that a client reuse the same connection or process to perform related operations." "State that needs to span multiple requests ... MUST be referenced by an explicit identifier the client passes on each request." Changelog: "Remove protocol-level sessions and the `Mcp-Session-Id` header from the Streamable HTTP transport." streamable-http.mdx § Earlier Streamable HTTP Revisions (modern-only servers): "An `Mcp-Session-Id` header on a request: ignore it, and do not mint or echo session IDs."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § Statelessness; changelog.mdx Major changes 1–2_
- **BP-14** (MUST, modern): "`server/discover` lets a client query a server's supported protocol versions, capabilities, and identity before sending any other requests. Servers MUST implement it." Request: method `server/discover`, `params` carrying only `_meta`. Result (schema required: `resultType`, `supportedVersions`, `capabilities`, `ttlMs`, `cacheScope`): `supportedVersions: string[]` ("The client should choose one of these for subsequent requests"), `capabilities: ServerCapabilities` (tools/resources/prompts/completions/logging/experimental/extensions; server/tools.mdx: "Servers that support tools MUST declare the `tools` capability"; resources.mdx likewise), optional `instructions`, `_meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo']` ("Servers SHOULD include this field"). caching.mdx: "Servers MUST include caching hints on results with `resultType: \"complete\"` returned by ... `server/discover`"; "Servers MUST provide a `ttlMs` value that is `>= 0`"; `cacheScope` is `"public"` ("does not contain user-specific data") or `"private"`.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/discover.mdx (whole page); basic/versioning.mdx § Protocol Version Negotiation; schema.ts `DiscoverRequest`, `DiscoverResult extends CacheableResult`, `CacheableResult`; server/utilities/caching.mdx § Cacheable Results, § Time-to-Live_
- **BP-15** (MUST, both): Schema (both eras): "a server returns this error when a client invokes a method the server does not implement — either a genuinely unknown method, or one gated behind a server capability the server did not advertise" → code `-32601`. Modern HTTP additionally: "If the server does not implement the requested RPC method, it MUST respond with `404 Not Found` and a JSON-RPC error with code `-32601` (`Method not found`)." In the legacy era 404 has a different meaning (2025-11-25 transports § Session Management: after terminating a session the server "MUST respond to requests containing that session ID with HTTP 404 Not Found" and the client "MUST start a new session"), so -32601 must be served with HTTP 200 for legacy requests.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts `MethodNotFoundError` / `METHOD_NOT_FOUND = -32601`; basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx § Protocol Version Header_
- **BP-16** (MUST, modern): Removed from the modern method set: "remove the `initialize`/`notifications/initialized` handshake"; "Remove `ping`, `logging/setLevel`, and `notifications/roots/list_changed`" (SEP-2575: "`ping`: Removed in both directions"); `resources/subscribe`/`resources/unsubscribe` replaced by `subscriptions/listen`; `roots/list` as a server-initiated RPC removed. A modern-era request for any of these is a method "the server does not implement" → BP-15 (-32601; HTTP 404). `logging/setLevel` is replaced by `_meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel']`.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/changelog.mdx Major changes 2, 4, 5; schema.ts `ClientRequest` union (DiscoverRequest | CompleteRequest | GetPromptRequest | ListPromptsRequest | ListResourcesRequest | ListResourceTemplatesRequest | ReadResourceRequest | SubscriptionsListenRequest | CallToolRequest | ListToolsRequest) and `ClientNotification = CancelledNotification`; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/seps/2575-stateless-mcp.md § Deprecated and Removed RPCs_
- **BP-17** (MUST NOT, modern): "To receive log messages for a specific request, include `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel` in the request's `_meta`. The server MUST NOT emit `notifications/message` for a request that does not include this field." "If the `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel` value carried in a request's `_meta` is not a recognized log level, the server SHOULD reject that request with ... `-32602` (Invalid params)". Valid levels: debug, info, notice, warning, error, critical, alert, emergency. Logging itself is Deprecated as of 2026-07-28 ("New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it").  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/utilities/logging.mdx § Per-request log level, § Error Handling; schema.ts `RequestMetaObject["io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel"]`_
- **BP-18** (MUST NOT, modern): "Servers MUST NOT initiate JSON-RPC requests, and clients do not send JSON-RPC responses." stdio: "The server MUST NOT write JSON-RPC requests to `stdout`." Cancellation: "Servers MUST NOT send `notifications/cancelled` for any other purpose" than tearing down a `subscriptions/listen` stream; servers receiving `notifications/cancelled` SHOULD stop processing and "MAY ignore cancellation notifications if ... The request cannot be cancelled".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/index.mdx (Overview); basic/transports/stdio.mdx § Receiving Messages; basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx_
- **BP-19** (MAY, both): "A server that wishes to support both legacy clients (which expect an `initialize` handshake) and modern clients (which use per-request metadata) MAY implement both behaviors." Era selection rule: "A dual-era server selects its behavior from how the client opens: A request carrying modern per-request `_meta` is served statelessly according to this revision. An `initialize` request selects legacy semantics, scoped to the stdio process (stdio) or the session (HTTP), as specified by the negotiated legacy protocol version." "A dual-era server MAY serve both eras concurrently on the same endpoint or process." Matrix row Legacy→Dual-era: "Works. The server answers `initialize` and serves the client according to the negotiated legacy revision." Detection contract the server must honour: "a recognized modern JSON-RPC error (such as UnsupportedProtocolVersionError) identifies a modern server"; on HTTP a dual-era client inspects the body of a `400 Bad Request`; on stdio it probes with `server/discover` and "The fallback MUST NOT be keyed to one specific error code".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx § Terminology, § Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions, § Compatibility Matrix_
- **BP-20** (MUST, legacy): "The initialization phase MUST be the first interaction between client and server." "The client MUST initiate this phase by sending an `initialize` request containing: Protocol version supported, Client capabilities, Client implementation information" (`params.protocolVersion`, `params.capabilities`, `params.clientInfo`). "The server MUST respond with its own capabilities and information": `result.protocolVersion`, `result.capabilities` (ServerCapabilities), `result.serverInfo` (Implementation: name, version, optional title), optional `result.instructions`. schema.json InitializeResult required: capabilities, protocolVersion, serverInfo.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Initialization; 2025-06-18/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Initialization; 2025-03-26/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Initialization_
- **BP-21** (MUST, legacy): "In the `initialize` request, the client MUST send a protocol version it supports. This SHOULD be the latest version supported by the client. If the server supports the requested protocol version, it MUST respond with the same version. Otherwise, the server MUST respond with another protocol version it supports. This SHOULD be the latest version supported by the server. If the client does not support the version in the server's response, it SHOULD disconnect." (The `-32602 "Unsupported protocol version"` example under § Error Handling is illustrative of "Failure to negotiate"; the normative rule is to answer with another supported version.)  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Version Negotiation (same text in 2025-06-18 and 2025-03-26)_
- **BP-22** (MUST, legacy): "During the operation phase, the client and server exchange messages according to the negotiated capabilities. Both parties MUST: Respect the negotiated protocol version; Only use capabilities that were successfully negotiated." On HTTP (2025-06-18+ transports § Protocol Version Header): the client "MUST include the `MCP-Protocol-Version: <protocol-version>` HTTP header on all subsequent requests"; "if the server does not receive an `MCP-Protocol-Version` header, and has no other way to identify the version ... the server SHOULD assume protocol version `2025-03-26`"; "If the server receives a request with an invalid or unsupported `MCP-Protocol-Version`, it MUST respond with `400 Bad Request`."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Operation; 2025-06-18/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Operation (2025-06-18 changelog item 9 changed SHOULD→MUST)_
- **BP-23** (MUST, legacy): "After successful initialization, the client MUST send an `initialized` notification" (`notifications/initialized`, no id, no response). "The client SHOULD NOT send requests other than pings before the server has responded to the `initialize` request." "The server SHOULD NOT send requests other than pings and logging before receiving the `initialized` notification." No server-side MUST to reject pre-initialize requests; the server must accept the notification silently (BP-06).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Initialization (initialized notification bullets)_
- **BP-24** (MUST, legacy): "Either the client or server can initiate a ping by sending a `ping` request" ("a standard JSON-RPC request with no parameters"). "The receiver MUST respond promptly with an empty response": `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":...,"result":{}}`. Legacy ping must keep working in the dual-era server; it is removed in the modern era (BP-16).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/utilities/ping.mdx § Behavior Requirements_
- **BP-25** (MUST, legacy): "Capability objects can describe sub-capabilities like: `listChanged`: Support for list change notifications (for prompts, resources, and tools); `subscribe`: Support for subscribing to individual items' changes (resources only)". "Servers that support tools MUST declare the `tools` capability" / "Servers that support resources MUST declare the `resources` capability" (same MUST in 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25 server/tools.mdx & resources.mdx). The generated JSON schemas do not set `additionalProperties: false` on capability objects, so undefined keys (e.g. `supportsProgress`) are tolerated but carry no meaning; `sessionId` is not a defined `InitializeResult` member (session id lives only in the `Mcp-Session-Id` HTTP header).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx § Capability Negotiation; schema.json 2025-06-18/2025-11-25 `ServerCapabilities` (tools: {listChanged?: boolean}; resources: {subscribe?: boolean, listChanged?: boolean}; prompts: {listChanged?: boolean}; logging: {}; completions: {}; experimental; 2025-11-25 adds tasks); 2026-07-28 server/tools.mdx § Capabilities_
- **BP-26** (MAY, legacy): "A server using the Streamable HTTP transport MAY assign a session ID at initialization time, by including it in an `MCP-Session-Id` header on the HTTP response containing the `InitializeResult`. The session ID SHOULD be globally unique and cryptographically secure ... MUST only contain visible ASCII characters (0x21 to 0x7E)." "If an `MCP-Session-Id` is returned by the server during initialization, clients ... MUST include it ... on all of their subsequent HTTP requests. Servers that require a session ID SHOULD respond to requests without an `MCP-Session-Id` header (other than initialization) with HTTP 400 Bad Request." "The server MAY terminate the session at any time, after which it MUST respond to requests containing that session ID with HTTP 404 Not Found." DELETE: "The server MAY respond to this request with HTTP 405 Method Not Allowed." A server that does not mint a session id has no session obligations.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx § Session Management (same in 2025-06-18 § Session Management)_
- **BP-27** (MUST, legacy): 2025-03-26 only: "MCP implementations MAY support sending JSON-RPC batches, but MUST support receiving JSON-RPC batches." (HTTP body may be "An array batching one or more requests and/or notifications".) From 2025-06-18: "Remove support for JSON-RPC batching"; "The body of the POST request MUST be a single JSON-RPC request, notification, or response." 2026-07-28: "The body of the HTTP POST MUST be a single JSON-RPC request or notification. The client MUST NOT send JSON-RPC responses." So batch support is required only if 2025-03-26 is in the server's supported legacy set.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-03-26/basic/index.mdx § Batching; 2025-06-18/changelog.mdx Major change 1; 2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx § Sending Messages to the Server item 3_
- **BP-28** (MUST, modern): "Certain key names are reserved by MCP for protocol-level metadata ... implementations MUST NOT make assumptions about values at these keys." Reserved: progressToken, io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion, /clientInfo, /clientCapabilities, /logLevel, /subscriptionId, traceparent/tracestate/baggage. Any prefix whose second label is `modelcontextprotocol` or `mcp` is reserved; server-emitted custom `_meta` keys must use a vendor prefix (e.g. `com.gdksoftware/`). Extensions: "advertised in the `extensions` field of capabilities"; "If one party supports an extension but the other does not, the supporting party MUST either revert to core protocol behavior or reject the request" (server supports none → omit `extensions`).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx § _meta (Reserved keys, key name format); basic/versioning.mdx § Extension Negotiation_
- **BP-29** (SHOULD, modern): "A server that supports only modern versions SHOULD name the protocol versions it supports in any error it returns to an `initialize` request, on any transport: legacy clients have no fall-forward mechanism, and this message may be the only diagnostic they can surface to users." Applies to the dual-era server only if legacy support is switched off by configuration.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx § Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions_
- **BP-30** (MUST, both): stdio framing for every era: "Each message is a single JSON-RPC request, notification, or response. Messages are delimited by newlines, and MUST NOT contain embedded newlines." "The server MUST NOT write anything to its `stdout` that is not a valid MCP message." "The server MAY write UTF-8 strings to `stderr` for any logging purposes." Modern § Request Metadata: "All request metadata for the stdio transport is carried inline in the JSON-RPC message body ... There is no header layer."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx (top bullets); 2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx § stdio_

## Streamable HTTP transport (modern 2026-07-28 + legacy 2025-03-26/2025-06-18/2025-11-25 shapes): headers, SSE streaming, sessions, Origin/CORS, binding, HTTP status mapping — server side, dual-era

Requirements extracted: 37. Gaps: 17.


### GAP-HTTP-01 [blocker, effort S] Origin validation is skipped when CORS is disabled or AllowedOrigins='*'

- Requirements: HTTP-02
- Current: VerifyAndSetCORSHeaders exits immediately when not FSettings.CorsEnabled (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:221-222) and only compares the Origin when FSettings.CorsAllowedOrigins <> '*' (line 227). With [CORS] Enabled=0, or AllowedOrigins=*, any browser Origin is accepted (line 257 even emits Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *). Matching is exact-string SameText (line 236), so 'http://localhost' does not match 'http://localhost:5173'.
- Required: HTTP-02: Origin MUST be validated on all incoming connections; present-and-invalid → 403 (both eras). Validation is a security control independent of whether CORS response headers are emitted.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: none (non-browser clients send no Origin; absent Origin stays allowed). Library consumers: TMCPSettings gains new properties; existing CorsEnabled/CorsAllowedOrigins keep compiling. Operators who set Enabled=0 to 'turn off security' will now get 403 for browser origins not in the list — document; keep AllowedOrigins=* as the explicit opt-out.
- Suggested change: Split the two concerns in TMCPIdHTTPServer: (1) new function ValidateOrigin(RequestInfo, ResponseInfo): Boolean that always runs (before the endpoint check, as now) — if RawHeaders.Values['Origin'] <> '' and not IsOriginAllowed(Origin) then ResponseNo := 403, ContentType application/json, body '{"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32600,"message":"Origin not allowed"}}' (HTTP-36), Exit False; (2) SetCORSHeaders only when CorsEnabled. Add TMCPSettings.AllowedOrigins ([Security] AllowedOrigins, default 'http://localhost,http://127.0.0.1,https://localhost,https://127.0.0.1' + allow wildcard-port patterns like 'http://localhost:*'), keep CorsAllowedOrigins as an alias that reads the same value for backwards compatibility. IsOriginAllowed: parse scheme://host[:port], compare scheme+host case-insensitively, port exact or '*'. Add 'Vary: Origin' when echoing a specific origin.

### GAP-HTTP-02 [blocker, effort M] MCP-Protocol-Version header is never read; no era/version classification, no 400/-32022

- Requirements: HTTP-16, HTTP-17, HTTP-19, HTTP-20, HTTP-28, HTTP-29
- Current: The only occurrence of the header name is in the CORS Allow-Headers list (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:259-260). HandlePostRequest (lines 316-362) never inspects MCP-Protocol-Version nor params._meta; every request is processed identically regardless of version. MCPServer.Types.pas:11 hard-codes MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION = '2025-06-18' and CoreManager.Initialize (src/Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:101) always returns it.
- Required: Modern: header MUST be present and MUST equal _meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion'] (else 400 + -32020); unsupported version → 400 + -32022 with data.supported/requested. Legacy: invalid/unsupported header → 400; absent header → assume 2025-03-26 (SHOULD legacy / MAY modern-dual-era). Dual-era: a request carrying modern _meta is served statelessly; initialize selects legacy semantics.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: unchanged behaviour when they send a supported legacy version or no header; clients sending an unknown version now get 400 (spec-required). Library consumers: keep MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION constant (semantics: default legacy negotiated version) and add MCP_LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION / MCP_SUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS; TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest(string,string) retained as a thin overload so TMCPStdioTransport and third-party callers compile unchanged.
- Suggested change: Add to MCPServer.Types.pas: const MCP_LATEST_PROTOCOL_VERSION = '2026-07-28'; MCP_SUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS: array[0..3] of string = ('2026-07-28','2025-11-25','2025-06-18','2025-03-26'); MCP_HEADER_PROTOCOL_VERSION = 'MCP-Protocol-Version'; MCP_ERR_HEADER_MISMATCH = -32020; MCP_ERR_MISSING_CLIENT_CAPABILITY = -32021; MCP_ERR_UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION = -32022; type TMCPProtocolEra = (peLegacy, peModern); TMCPRequestContext = record ProtocolVersion: string; Era: TMCPProtocolEra; SessionID: string; end. In TMCPIdHTTPServer add function ClassifyRequest(RequestInfo; Body: TJSONObject; out Ctx: TMCPRequestContext; out ErrorJSON: string; out HttpStatus: Integer): Boolean implementing: Header := Trim(RequestInfo.RawHeaders.Values[MCP_HEADER_PROTOCOL_VERSION]); MetaVersion := Body.params._meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion'] (if present). If Header = '' then: if MetaVersion <> '' → 400, -32020 'required header MCP-Protocol-Version missing' else Ctx.ProtocolVersion := '2025-03-26', Era := peLegacy. Else if Header not in MCP_SUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSIONS → 400 + -32022 with data {supported:[...], requested: Header} (valid for both eras; legacy MUST 400, body allowed). Else if Header >= '2026-07-28' (string compare of ISO dates works) → Era := peModern; if MetaVersion <> Header → 400 + -32020. Else Era := peLegacy (ignore _meta).
