Make the supported list configurable via TMCPSettings.SupportedProtocolVersions ([Protocol] SupportedVersions=...) so operators can pin legacy-only or modern-only. Add ProcessRequest overload: function ProcessRequest(Request: TJSONObject; const Ctx: TMCPRequestContext; out ErrorCode: Integer): string; old overload calls it with a legacy context.

### GAP-HTTP-03 [blocker, effort M] No validation of Mcp-Method / Mcp-Name standard headers (no -32020 path, no Base64 sentinel decoding)

- Requirements: HTTP-21, HTTP-22, HTTP-23, HTTP-26, HTTP-28
- Current: Neither 'Mcp-Method' nor 'Mcp-Name' appears anywhere in src (grep); HandlePostRequest goes straight from Accept sniffing to FJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:337-357). No -32020 constant exists (only -32700..-32603 at lines 95-99 and JsonRpcProcessor.pas:28-32).
- Required: Modern requests: Mcp-Method REQUIRED and MUST equal body.method (case-sensitive); Mcp-Name REQUIRED for tools/call & prompts/get (= params.name) and resources/read (= params.uri); values may be '=?base64?...?=' encoded and MUST be decoded (UTF-8) before comparison; invalid Base64 / invalid characters / missing / mismatch → 400 + JSON-RPC error -32020 with the request id. Header-name lookup case-insensitive; trim surrounding whitespace. Not required on notifications or on legacy-era requests.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: unaffected (validation only runs when Era = peModern). Modern SDK clients already send these headers. Library consumers: none (transport-internal); custom managers/tools unchanged.
- Suggested change: New unit src/Server/MCPServer.HttpHeaders.pas (pure functions, unit-testable): function DecodeMcpHeaderValue(const Raw: string; out Value: string; out Err: string): Boolean — Trim; if starts with '=?base64?' and ends with '?=' (case-sensitive) then strict-validate payload (chars [A-Za-z0-9+/], '=' padding only at end, Length mod 4 = 0; do NOT rely on TNetEncoding.Base64 leniency — check before calling TNetEncoding.Base64.DecodeStringToBytes, then TEncoding.UTF8.GetString) else verify every char is 0x20-0x7E or #9 (otherwise Err 'invalid characters'). function ValidateStandardHeaders(RequestInfo: TIdHTTPRequestInfo; Body: TJSONObject; out Err: string): Boolean: Method := Body.GetValue<string>('method'); McpMethod := RequestInfo.RawHeaders.Values['Mcp-Method'] (Indy TIdHeaderList name lookup is case-insensitive — verify with a 'mcp-method' test); missing → Err; Trim(McpMethod) <> Method → Err. If Method in ['tools/call','prompts/get'] then Expected := params.name; if 'resources/read' then Expected := params.uri; when Expected applies: header missing → Err; DecodeMcpHeaderValue fails → Err; decoded <> Expected → Err. In HandlePostRequest, on Err: ResponseNo := 400; ContentType application/json; body := TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.CreateErrorResponse(id, -32020, 'Header mismatch: ' + Err) (make CreateErrorResponse public/class). Run these checks only for requests (has id) with Ctx.Era = peModern; for notifications validate Mcp-Method only if present.

### GAP-HTTP-04 [blocker, effort M] HTTP status is always 200 for JSON-RPC errors; unknown method must be 404, meta/capability/parse errors 400; method-not-found detection is a fragile string heuristic

- Requirements: HTTP-18, HTTP-28, HTTP-30, HTTP-05
- Current: HandlePostRequestJSON sets ResponseNo := 200 unconditionally (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:553) and HandlePostRequestSSE likewise (line 509). Unparseable bodies also yield 200 with a -32700 body (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:212-217 via HTTP path). TMCPJsonRpcProcessor maps exceptions to -32601 by Pos('not found', E.Message) (src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:214-215) — any tool raising e.g. 'File not found' becomes method-not-found; ExecuteMethodCall raises a plain Exception (line 123).
- Required: Modern: unknown RPC method → 404 + -32601; missing required _meta fields → 400 + -32602; -32021 → 400; header validation → 400 + -32020; unsupported version → 400 + -32022; every such 4xx MUST carry the JSON-RPC error body (era detection). Both eras: a notification the server cannot accept → HTTP error status (400). Legacy: keep 200 for -32601 (a 404 would be read by legacy clients as 'session terminated' per HTTP-32 and trigger re-initialize).
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: no change (status mapping applied only when Era = peModern, except 400 for unparseable bodies which the legacy spec also allows). Library consumers: introduce EMCPError(Code, Message, Data) in MCPServer.Types; existing custom managers that raise plain Exception keep working (mapped to -32603 as today, but no longer accidentally -32601 when the message contains 'not found' — behaviour change to document). TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest(string,string) signature retained.
- Suggested change: MCPServer.Types.pas: EMCPError = class(Exception) Code: Integer; Data: TJSONObject; end; EMCPMethodNotFound = class(EMCPError). TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ExecuteMethodCall raises EMCPMethodNotFound.Create(-32601, ...). ProcessRequest(TJSONObject, Ctx, out ErrorCode) sets ErrorCode from EMCPError.Code (0 on success, -32603 for other exceptions) and includes E.Data as error.data when assigned (needed for -32022 supported/requested and -32021 requiredCapabilities). TMCPIdHTTPServer.HttpStatusForError(Ctx, Code): Integer := case Code of -32601: if Era=peModern then 404 else 200; -32020,-32021,-32022,-32700,-32600: 400; -32602: 400 when the error originated from _meta validation (raise EMCPError with a flag/HttpStatus field so the processor can say '400'), else 200; else 200. Give EMCPError an optional HttpStatus property so any layer can pin the status. Also set ResponseNo := 400 for -32700 parse errors and for array bodies outside 2025-03-26 (-32600).

### GAP-HTTP-05 [major, effort S] 202 'with no body' is not guaranteed under Indy (default HTML body) and notifications are never dispatched to managers

- Requirements: HTTP-05
- Current: HandlePostRequest sets ResponseNo := 202 and returns without setting ContentText/ContentLength (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:343-352). Indy's TIdHTTPResponseInfo generates a default '<HTML><BODY><B>202 Accepted</B></BODY></HTML>' body when both ContentText and ContentStream are empty (behaviour of IdCustomHTTPServer WriteHeader/WriteContent — Indy sources are not present in this container, verify with curl -i). The same applies to the empty SSE bodies at lines 294 and 506 and the 204 at line 526. Additionally the notification is never passed to FJsonRpcProcessor (Exit at line 351), and JsonRpcProcessor itself also drops notifications before dispatch (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:174-181), so IMCPCapabilityManager.HandlesMethod('notifications/...') implementations (e.g. CoreManager.pas:51,59) are dead code on both transports.
- Required: HTTP-05: accepted notification → 202 with no body (both eras). Legacy notifications/cancelled and notifications/initialized should at least reach the managers so a dual-era server can track legacy session state or honour cancellation (SHOULD, may be ignored).
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: strictly better (clean 202). Library consumers: custom managers start receiving notifications via ExecuteMethod (return value ignored) — a manager that raises on unknown notifications would now log an error instead of nothing; document. No signature changes.
- Suggested change: For 202/204 responses set ResponseInfo.ContentLength := 0 and ResponseInfo.ContentText := '' explicitly (and ContentType := ''), then verify with curl that the body is empty; if Indy still emits the HTML page, call ResponseInfo.WriteHeader manually after setting ContentLength := 0 so WriteContent is skipped. Dispatch notifications: in HandlePostRequest call FJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessNotification(Body, Ctx) (new method that finds the manager via GetManagerForMethod and calls ExecuteMethod inside try/except with TLogger.Error) before returning 202; only return an HTTP error (400 + JSON-RPC error without id) when the body is not a valid JSON-RPC message.

### GAP-HTTP-06 [major, effort S] GET handling: returns 200 JSON 'endpoint info' (neither SSE nor 405) and an empty immediately-closed SSE stream

- Requirements: HTTP-27, HTTP-31, HTTP-01
- Current: HandleGetRequest (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:271-314): if Accept contains text/event-stream → 200 text/event-stream with ContentText '' (lines 283-299, plus echo of Mcp-Session-Id at 289-291); otherwise 200 application/json '{"url":..., "transport":...}' (lines 303-313). Modern spec removed the GET stream entirely; the changelog (item 4, item 9) removed GET and Last-Event-ID.
- Required: Modern: GET → 405 (SHOULD). Legacy: server MUST either return text/event-stream or 405 — a 200 JSON body violates the legacy MUST. Since 405 is permitted in every revision, a dual-era server can answer 405 unconditionally; legacy SDK clients (TS/Python StreamableHTTP client) treat 405 on GET as 'server offers no standalone stream' and continue.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: 405 is explicitly allowed; clients that currently open the empty stream keep working (they handle 405). The non-standard 'endpoint info' JSON disappears from the MCP endpoint — if anyone relied on it (README does not document it), expose it on a different path (e.g. GET /) or a Settings flag. Library consumers: none.
- Suggested change: Replace HandleGetRequest body with: ResponseInfo.ResponseNo := 405; ResponseInfo.CustomHeaders.Values['Allow'] := 'POST, OPTIONS'; ContentLength := 0. Same for DELETE (currently the else branch at lines 200-204) — add the Allow header (RFC 9110 requires it on 405). Optionally keep a TMCPSettings.LegacyGetStream: Boolean (default False) that restores the old 'open SSE and close immediately' answer for legacy Accept: text/event-stream GETs; when enabled, do not echo Mcp-Session-Id for modern-era requests. Remove the endpoint-info JSON or move it to a non-MCP path such as '/' guarded by the same Origin check.

### GAP-HTTP-07 [major, effort L] SSE responses are buffered (ContentText set once): no incremental streaming, no keep-alive comments, no disconnect-as-cancellation, no long-lived subscriptions/listen stream, Stop() cannot close open streams

- Requirements: HTTP-10, HTTP-11, HTTP-12, HTTP-13, HTTP-14, HTTP-35
- Current: HandlePostRequestSSE computes the full response first (FJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest at src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:488) and assigns one string to ResponseInfo.ContentText (line 501); Indy writes it with Content-Length after the handler returns. Nothing can be written to the client while a tool executes; there is no hook for notifications/progress or notifications/message; the connection thread has no way to detect client disconnect (no IOHandler access — Context is not passed to HandlePostRequest*, line 53-55); Stop() (lines 162-170) simply sets Active := False, which would block on any long-lived handler thread. The SSE event format itself is fine ('event: message', 'data: ', LF LF at lines 101-105, 495-499).
- Required: HTTP-10/11: notifications related to the request may precede the final response on the same stream; final response terminates the stream. HTTP-14 (MUST): client closing the stream = cancellation; server SHOULD stop work and MUST NOT send further messages. HTTP-35: subscriptions/listen stream stays open, acknowledged first, graceful completion result on shutdown. HTTP-12/13: X-Accel-Buffering: no (already set) and periodic ':' keep-alive comments. Legacy HTTP-15: disconnect is NOT cancellation (era-dependent).
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: unaffected for plain requests (JSON responses recommended by default; SSE only when streaming is needed). Library consumers: IMCPTool.Execute / IMCPResource.Read / IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod signatures unchanged; streaming is exposed through an ambient, optional TMCPRequestContext.Current (threadvar) with SendNotification/IsCancelled/CheckCancelled, so existing tools compile and run as before and new tools opt in. SSE responses use Connection: close, which drops HTTP keep-alive for streamed responses only.
- Suggested change: Pass Context: TIdContext into HandlePostRequest/HandlePostRequestSSE. New class TMCPSseWriter (src/Server/MCPServer.SseWriter.pas) wrapping TIdContext+TIdHTTPResponseInfo: Open — set ContentType 'text/event-stream', CharSet 'utf-8', Cache-Control no-cache, X-Accel-Buffering no, ContentLength := -1, CloseConnection := True (HTTP/1.1 needs close-delimited body unless chunked; Indy does not chunk-encode IOHandler writes automatically — if keep-alive must be preserved, set TransferEncoding := 'chunked' and frame each write as hex-length CRLF data CRLF, terminating with '0' CRLF CRLF), then ResponseInfo.WriteHeader; WriteEvent(const JSON: string) → Context.Connection.IOHandler.Write('data: ' + JSON + #10#10, IndyTextEncoding_UTF8) (JSON never contains raw newlines when produced by ToJSON); WriteComment → IOHandler.Write(': keep-alive'#10#10); IsConnected → IOHandler.Connected and not IOHandler.ClosedGracefully (call IOHandler.CheckForDisconnect(False) first); Close → Context.Connection.Disconnect. Catch EIdConnClosedGracefully/EIdSocketError on write and mark Cancelled. Ambient context: threadvar CurrentContext in MCPServer.RequestContext.pas with class property TMCPRequestContext.Current; fields Era, ProtocolVersion, ProgressToken, LogLevel, Cancelled: Boolean, OnNotification: TProc<string,TJSONObject>. Execution model in HandlePostRequest: default to application/json; switch to SSE lazily on the first SendNotification (headers not yet written) — requires running the handler so that the response is only committed after execution; simplest: always run ProcessRequest with a sink that buffers until first notification, then Open SSE and flush; if no notification occurred respond JSON. For subscriptions/listen (if implemented by the managers area) the manager returns a marker (e.g. TValue holding a TMCPStreamHandle) and the transport loops: write acknowledged, then WaitFor(FShutdownEvent or handle.Event, 15000ms) → on timeout WriteComment (doubles as disconnect probe), on event flush queued notifications, on disconnect exit (cancellation), on shutdown write completion result then Close. Keep a TThreadList<TMCPSseWriter> FOpenStreams; Stop sets FShutdownEvent, waits up to N seconds for streams to drain, force-disconnects the rest, then FHTTPServer.Active := False. Era rule: mark Cancelled on disconnect only when Ctx.Era = peModern (legacy: log only).

### GAP-HTTP-10 [major, effort S] Server binds to all interfaces; Settings.Host is only used for log text

- Requirements: HTTP-03
- Current: Start sets FHTTPServer.DefaultPort := FPort and Active := True with no Bindings entry (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:155-156), which makes Indy listen on 0.0.0.0 (and, depending on Indy version, ::). FSettings.Host ('localhost' default, MCPServer.Settings.pas:86) is used only in the log line (IdHTTPServer.pas:159) and the removed GET info JSON (line 309).
- Required: HTTP-03: when running locally, SHOULD bind only to 127.0.0.1.
- Compat impact: Operators who reach the server from another machine (README mentions 'Remote connections', Cloudflare Tunnel) must set the new BindAddress=0.0.0.0 explicitly — call out in README/changelog. Library consumers: TMCPIdHTTPServer.Port property unchanged; new BindAddress property defaults to 127.0.0.1 unless Settings.Host is a non-loopback name/IP.
- Suggested change: Add TMCPSettings.BindAddress ([Server] BindAddress, default '127.0.0.1'; also bind '::1' when IPv6 is desired). In TMCPIdHTTPServer.Start: FHTTPServer.Bindings.Clear; with FHTTPServer.Bindings.Add do begin IP := BindAddress; Port := FPort; IPVersion := Id_IPv4 (add a second binding with Id_IPv6 for '::1'); end; keep DefaultPort for HandleQuerySSLPort. Log a warning when BindAddress = '0.0.0.0' and AllowedOrigins = '*'.

### GAP-HTTP-11 [major, effort S] CORS Allow/Expose headers do not cover the modern header names and Mcp-Param-*; DELETE not listed; no Vary: Origin

- Requirements: HTTP-21, HTTP-25, HTTP-26, HTTP-02
- Current: Access-Control-Allow-Headers is 'Accept, Content-Type, Last-Event-ID, Mcp-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Session-Id', Allow-Methods 'POST, GET, OPTIONS', Expose-Headers 'Mcp-Session-Id' (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:257-262). Browser-based modern clients sending Mcp-Method / Mcp-Name / Mcp-Param-* (and Authorization) will fail preflight. OPTIONS handler returns 200 with no Content-Length (line 265-269).
- Required: Not an MCP MUST per se, but required for the MUST-send headers (HTTP-21/25) to be usable from browser clients when CORS is enabled; Mcp-Param-{Name} names are dynamic.
- Compat impact: Legacy browser clients keep working (old names remain listed). Library consumers: none.
- Suggested change: Allow-Headers := 'Accept, Content-Type, Authorization, Last-Event-ID, MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Session-Id, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name' and, on OPTIONS preflight, append the request's Access-Control-Request-Headers value verbatim (covers Mcp-Param-*; alternatively '*' when Allow-Origin is '*' and no credentials). Allow-Methods := 'POST, GET, DELETE, OPTIONS'. Expose-Headers keep 'Mcp-Session-Id' (legacy). Add 'Vary: Origin' whenever a specific origin is echoed. Set ContentLength := 0 on the OPTIONS response (204 is also fine).

### GAP-HTTP-08 [minor, effort S] Mcp-Session-Id is echoed unconditionally (modern SHOULD-not) and minted inconsistently for legacy (only on the JSON path, never on the SSE path); non-standard sessionId in InitializeResult; shared FSessionID field is not thread-safe

- Requirements: HTTP-27, HTTP-32
- Current: Session header is read at src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:333 and echoed back at 348-349, 485-486, 548-549 regardless of era. On the JSON path a fresh session id is lifted out of result.sessionId with a Pos('"sessionId"') heuristic and a hard 'as TJSONObject' cast (lines 533-547; EInvalidCast → Indy 500 if a manager returns a string result containing that text, see JsonRpcProcessor.pas:197). The SSE path (used whenever Accept contains text/event-stream, which every conformant client sends) never sets Mcp-Session-Id on the initialize response, so sessions are effectively never established for conformant clients. CoreManager.Initialize adds a non-schema 'sessionId' field to the result and overwrites the single shared FSessionID from every Indy thread (src/Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:97,126).
- Required: Modern (HTTP-27): ignore Mcp-Session-Id; do not mint or echo. Legacy (HTTP-32): minting is MAY; if minted it must be on the InitializeResult response regardless of JSON/SSE path; a server that never terminates sessions never needs 404/400 session errors.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: two valid options — (a) stateless legacy: never mint, ignore incoming ids (clients that still send an old id are unaffected); (b) mint per-initialize with an in-memory session table. Either is spec-conformant; (a) is the smaller change and matches the modern stateless model. Library consumers: TMCPCoreManager.SessionID property can remain (return '' or last minted) — mark deprecated; removing result.sessionId is invisible to spec clients.
- Suggested change: Option (a) recommended: delete the Mcp-Session-Id echo for Era = peModern; for legacy keep echoing an incoming id (harmless) but stop minting: remove ResultJSON.AddPair('sessionId', ...) from CoreManager.Initialize and remove the Pos('"sessionId"') block from HandlePostRequestJSON. If session-scoped legacy state is wanted later (negotiated version, initialized flag, log level), add TMCPLegacySessionStore (TDictionary<string, TMCPLegacySession> under TCriticalSection, idle expiry) minted in HandlePostRequest when Body.method = 'initialize' and set on BOTH JSON and SSE responses; then honour HTTP-32: unknown id → 404 only for ids this server minted-and-expired (keep a tombstone set) and otherwise be lenient. Guard FSessionID with the store instead of a class field.

### GAP-HTTP-09 [minor, effort S] SSE event ids: non-atomic global counter (legacy uniqueness MUST at risk) and ids are obsolete in the modern era

- Requirements: HTTP-33, HTTP-13
- Current: GetNextEventID does Inc(FEventIDCounter) on a plain Int64 field (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:416-420) from Indy's per-connection threads; duplicates are possible under concurrent requests, violating the legacy 'MUST be globally unique' rule. Every SSE response emits 'id: N' (lines 492-496). The 2026-07-28 changelog item 9 removes SSE event IDs; Last-Event-ID is (correctly) never read.
- Required: Legacy: ids are MAY; if present MUST be unique. Modern: no ids; Last-Event-ID ignored.
- Compat impact: None for clients (ids are optional in legacy, absent in modern). Library consumers: none.
- Suggested change: Simplest: drop 'id:' lines and remove FEventIDCounter/GetNextEventID entirely (allowed in all revisions; no resumability is implemented anyway). If ids are kept for legacy streams, use TInterlocked.Increment(FEventIDCounter) (System.SyncObjs) or AtomicIncrement, and emit them only when Ctx.Era = peLegacy.

### GAP-HTTP-13 [minor, effort S] JSON-RPC batch bodies (2025-03-26 legacy) are rejected with a -32700 parse error; modern array bodies should be -32600/400

- Requirements: HTTP-09, HTTP-08
- Current: IsRequestOnlyNotificationsOrResponses accepts arrays (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:453-465) and returns 202 for all-notification batches, but a batch containing requests reaches TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ParseJSONRequest which raises 'JSON-RPC request must be an object' (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:52-56) → reported as -32700 with id null and HTTP 200 (lines 212-217).
- Required: HTTP-09: a 2025-03-26 client MAY send batches; server must answer with JSON/SSE responses (MAY be batched). HTTP-08: modern bodies are single messages — arrays are invalid input (400 + -32600 recommended).
- Compat impact: Only 2025-03-26 clients that actually batch (rare; no mainstream SDK does). Library consumers: none.
- Suggested change: In HandlePostRequest after parsing: if Body is TJSONArray then if Ctx.ProtocolVersion = '2025-03-26' then process each element via ProcessRequest and return a JSON array of the non-empty responses (202 when all were notifications, as now) else ResponseNo := 400 with -32600 'Batching is not supported in protocol version X'. Keep IsRequestOnlyNotificationsOrResponses.

### GAP-HTTP-14 [minor, effort S] Thread-unsafe counters: TServerStatusResource and FEventIDCounter are incremented from Indy worker threads without atomics; 'active connections' actually counts in-flight requests

- Requirements: HTTP-34, HTTP-14
- Current: TServerStatusResource.IncrementRequestCount/ConnectionOpened/ConnectionClosed use plain Inc/Dec on class vars (src/Resources/MCPServer.Resource.Server.pas:95-109) and are called once per HTTP request from HandleHTTPRequest (IdHTTPServer.pas:177-179,206), so under keep-alive the value is 'requests in flight', not connections. GetResourceData reads them unsynchronised (lines 139-140). FEventIDCounter: see GAP-HTTP-09.
- Required: Not an MCP normative rule; a correctness prerequisite for the concurrent streaming model (multiple SSE streams per client are allowed; HTTP-34 requires per-stream message isolation) and for the legacy unique-id MUST.
- Compat impact: None. Library consumers embedding TServerStatusResource see the same API.
- Suggested change: Use AtomicIncrement/AtomicDecrement (System.SyncObjs TInterlocked) for FRequestCount/FActiveConnections; read with AtomicCmpExchange or a volatile read. Either rename the JSON field to activeRequests or derive ActiveConnections from FHTTPServer.Contexts.LockList.Count/UnlockList exposed through a callback. Do the same for any new open-stream registry (TThreadList).

### GAP-HTTP-15 [minor, effort S] Accept parsing: substring match on the first Accept header only; SSE chosen whenever offered; request with id=null treated as a request and answered with 204/empty stream

- Requirements: HTTP-06
- Current: AcceptsSSE := Pos('text/event-stream', AcceptHeader) > 0 (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:422-425) — ignores q=0, ignores a second 'Accept:' header line (RawHeaders.Values returns the first). SSE is selected for every request that offers it (line 354), even though nothing is streamed. When ProcessRequest returns '' for a request whose id is null/bool (ExtractRequestID → Empty, JsonRpcProcessor.pas:82-87,174-181) the transport answers 204 (line 524-528) or an empty SSE body (line 506).
- Required: HTTP-06: a request MUST get application/json or text/event-stream; nothing else. Server chooses the format; JSON is the cheaper default when no notifications will be sent.
- Compat impact: Clients that currently receive SSE for every call will receive application/json — both are mandatory for clients (2025-03-26 onward). Library consumers: none.
- Suggested change: Default to application/json; use SSE only when streaming is required (progress/logging/subscriptions per GAP-HTTP-07). Implement AcceptsSSE by iterating all RawHeaders entries named 'Accept', splitting on ',', trimming, and checking media-type = 'text/event-stream' (or '*/*') with q <> 0. If streaming is needed but the client did not offer text/event-stream, fall back to JSON with the final response only. Treat id=null as an invalid request (-32600, 400) rather than a notification.

### GAP-HTTP-16 [minor, effort S] No authentication hook on the HTTP transport

- Requirements: HTTP-04
- Current: No Authorization handling anywhere in TMCPIdHTTPServer; README suggests 'claude mcp add ... --header "Authorization: Bearer your-token"' (README.md:353) but the token is ignored.
- Required: HTTP-04: servers SHOULD implement proper authentication for all connections (the full OAuth spec is a separate area).
- Compat impact: Opt-in; default open as today. Library consumers: new optional OnAuthenticate event / Settings.BearerToken; no breaking change.
- Suggested change: Add TMCPIdHTTPServer.OnAuthenticate: TFunc<TIdHTTPRequestInfo, Boolean> (or an IMCPHttpAuthenticator interface) invoked after Origin validation; when it returns False respond 401 with 'WWW-Authenticate: Bearer' and a JSON-RPC error body without id. Provide a built-in static-bearer implementation driven by [Security] BearerToken in settings.ini. Never log the Authorization header (current logging at IdHTTPServer.pas:331 logs whole bodies; add a redaction option).

### GAP-HTTP-17 [minor, effort S] Documentation/config does not describe the dual-era transport (README badge 2025-06-18, no settings for versions/bind/origin)

- Requirements: HTTP-29, HTTP-27, HTTP-01
- Current: README.md:6 badge 'MCP-2025-06-18', :30 'Implements MCP specification 2025-06-18 with Streamable HTTP and SSE', :107 'Claude Code (SSE support)'; settings.ini.example has only [Server]/[CORS]/[SSL]; TMCPSettings has no version, bind-address or security-origin keys (src/Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas:83-96).
- Required: Derived from HTTP-27/29: operators need to know that GET/DELETE answer 405, sessions are not minted, which versions are served, and how to bind/expose the server.
- Compat impact: None at runtime; new INI keys have defaults so existing settings.ini files keep loading (TIniFile.Read* with defaults, Settings.pas:143-155).
- Suggested change: Add to settings.ini.example and TMCPSettings: [Server] BindAddress=127.0.0.1; [Protocol] SupportedVersions=2026-07-28,2025-11-25,2025-06-18,2025-03-26; LegacyGetStream=0; [Security] AllowedOrigins=...; BearerToken=. README: update badge to 2026-07-28 (dual-era), document header requirements for modern clients (MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name), the 405 on GET/DELETE, stateless sessions, curl examples for both eras, and the BindAddress change.

### GAP-HTTP-12 [optional, effort M] No Mcp-Param-* validation path and no way for tools to declare x-mcp-header

- Requirements: HTTP-24, HTTP-25
- Current: No 'Mcp-Param' handling in the transport; the schema generator has no attribute for x-mcp-header (out of this area, but the transport needs the binding list). Unrecognized Mcp-Param-* headers are currently ignored, which is what the spec requires for headers the server does not recognize.
- Required: HTTP-24 (MAY) feature; if any tool declares x-mcp-header, HTTP-25 validation becomes MUST: header present → decoded value must equal argument (numeric compare for integers, 'true'/'false' for booleans); argument absent/null → header must not be expected; argument present but header missing → reject; invalid characters → reject; all with 400 + -32020.
- Compat impact: None until a tool opts in. Library consumers: new optional attribute (e.g. [SchemaHeader('Region')]) on TMCPToolBase<T> param classes; existing tools unchanged.
- Suggested change: Transport side: in ValidateStandardHeaders for tools/call, obtain the tool's inputSchema (TMCPRegistry.CreateTool(Name).GetInputSchema, cached per tool name in a TDictionary<string, TArray<THeaderBinding>> guarded by TMonitor; THeaderBinding = record HeaderName, PropertyPath: string; Kind: (hbString, hbInteger, hbBoolean) end, collected by walking 'properties' chains only). For each binding: ArgValue := arguments at path; Header := RawHeaders.Values['Mcp-Param-' + HeaderName]; if ArgValue is nil/TJSONNull then (if Header <> '' → treat as mismatch → -32020) else begin if Header = '' → -32020 'missing Mcp-Param-...'; DecodeMcpHeaderValue; compare: hbInteger → StrToInt64/TryStrToFloat numeric equality, hbBoolean → decoded = 'true'/'false' matching TJSONBool, hbString → exact end. Ignore Mcp-Param-* headers with no binding. Reject bindings that violate the x-mcp-header constraints at registration time (log + drop the annotation) so the server never advertises an invalid tool.

### Already conformant

- Single configurable MCP endpoint path with 404 for other paths (HTTP-01): src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:184-192 (FSettings.Endpoint, default '/mcp' in src/Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas:89).
- DELETE (and any other method) answers 405 (HTTP-27 modern, HTTP-32 legacy MAY): src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:200-204 (only the RFC 9110 'Allow' header is missing).
- Origin present-and-not-allowed → 403 when CORS is enabled with an explicit list (HTTP-02 partial): src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:244-251; absent Origin is allowed (non-browser clients), which is correct.
- Notification/response-only POST bodies get 202 (HTTP-05, modulo Indy default body — see GAP-HTTP-05): src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:343-352.
- Requests are answered with either Content-Type application/json (HandlePostRequestJSON, lines 530-553) or text/event-stream (HandlePostRequestSSE, lines 479-509) (HTTP-06).
- UTF-8 in and out (HTTP-07): body read with IndyTextEncoding_UTF8 (line 328); JSON response written via TStringStream(..., TEncoding.UTF8) (line 551); SSE CharSet 'utf-8' (line 480).
- SSE stream headers already include Cache-Control: no-cache and X-Accel-Buffering: no (HTTP-12): lines 285-287 and 481-483.
- SSE framing is valid per the SSE spec: 'event: message', 'data: <json>', blank-line terminator with LF (lines 101-105, 495-499); the final JSON-RPC response ends the body, so the stream terminates with the response (HTTP-10/HTTP-11 'SHOULD terminate').
- Server never sends JSON-RPC requests or unrelated notifications on a response stream (HTTP-10 MUST NOT, HTTP-34): only the single response is ever written (lines 488-501).
- Last-Event-ID is never read and no replay is attempted (HTTP-27 modern 'ignore'; HTTP-33 legacy MAY): grep shows the header only in the CORS allow list (line 260).
- Session id, when echoed, is a GUID string (visible ASCII, HTTP-32): src/Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:97.
- OPTIONS preflight is handled (lines 194-195, 265-269) and CORS headers are emitted only when CorsEnabled (line 221-222).
- HTTP keep-alive is enabled for the POST-per-message model (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:118) and Indy runs each connection on its own thread, which is compatible with per-request SSE streams.
- TLS is available via TaurusTLS/OpenSSL (lines 364-408) so the server can be deployed with authentication/transport security as the spec's security section expects.
- JSON-RPC parse errors are reported as -32700 with id null (src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:209-217), matching the error object rules (only the HTTP status needs to change for modern era).

### Notes

- Indy source is not available in this container (find found no IdCustomHTTPServer.pas), so all statements about Indy internals (default HTML body for empty 202/204, WriteHeader/IOHandler streaming, case-insensitive TIdHeaderList lookup, Bindings default 0.0.0.0) must be verified on the Windows build with curl -i / curl -N before finalising the plan.
- Recommended request pipeline order for POST: Origin (403) → endpoint (404) → parse body (400/-32700) → array handling (2025-03-26 batch vs 400/-32600) → notification? (validate Mcp-Method only if present, dispatch, 202 no body) → era/version classification (missing header + _meta → 400/-32020; unknown version → 400/-32022; header ≠ _meta → 400/-32020) → modern standard headers Mcp-Method/Mcp-Name/Mcp-Param-* (400/-32020) → _meta required fields (400/-32602) → dispatch → status mapping (-32601 → 404 modern only; -32021 → 400; else 200) → JSON or SSE.
- Era classification key: header value (2026-07-28 or later = modern; 2025-03-26/06-18/11-25 = legacy; absent = legacy 2025-03-26 unless the body carries _meta.protocolVersion, in which case it is a modern request missing its header). Legacy clients do not send MCP-Protocol-Version on the initialize request itself (2025-06-18 lifecycle note: 'on all subsequent requests'), so initialize always arrives header-less and must be treated as legacy.
- 404 must never be used for legacy-era requests: legacy clients interpret 404 + Mcp-Session-Id as 'session terminated' and re-initialize (HTTP-32 item 4). The -32601 → 404 mapping is modern-era only.
- Every 4xx returned to a modern request must carry the JSON-RPC error body (-32020/-32021/-32022/-32602/-32601): dual-era clients use the body to decide whether to fall back to initialize (HTTP-28). An empty 400 would make a modern client think this is a legacy server.
- Notifications over HTTP have no defined header requirements in 2026-07-28 ('header requirements for notification POSTs are not defined by this revision'); do not reject a notification for missing Mcp-Method; the only client→server notification in the modern core is notifications/cancelled, which is stdio-only.
- Modern era: a POST body that is a JSON-RPC response object is invalid ('The client MUST NOT send JSON-RPC responses'); the spec gives no server rule — recommend 400 + -32600 (id null) for modern era, 202 for legacy era (legacy clients send responses to server requests such as ping/roots/list even though this server never issues them).
- Base64 sentinel decoding must be strict: System.NetEncoding.TBase64Encoding silently skips invalid characters, but SEP-2243's conformance table requires 400 + HeaderMismatch for invalid padding or characters; validate the alphabet and length mod 4 before decoding. The markers '=?base64?' and '?=' are case-sensitive and lowercase; a plain value that happens to look like the sentinel is always encoded by clients, so no ambiguity remains after decoding.
- SSE event ids and the FEventIDCounter can be removed outright: modern removed them (changelog item 9 'Remove SSE stream resumability and message redelivery (the Last-Event-ID header and SSE event IDs)'), legacy makes them optional, and the server implements no replay.
- Dropping the GET stream to 405 is safe for legacy SDK clients: the TypeScript and Python StreamableHTTP client transports treat 405 on their standalone GET as 'no server-initiated stream available' and continue. The MCP Inspector talks to the server through its own Node proxy, so CORS is irrelevant for Inspector testing; CORS only matters for browser pages calling the server directly.
- Streaming design implication for the library API: keep IMCPTool.Execute(const Arguments: TJSONObject): TValue and IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod unchanged; expose a threadvar-backed TMCPRequestContext.Current (Era, ProtocolVersion, ProgressToken, LogLevel, IsCancelled, SendProgress, SendLog) so existing custom tools/managers keep compiling and only new code opts into progress/log notifications and cooperative cancellation. Indy runs each connection on its own thread so a threadvar is a safe ambient carrier for the duration of ExecuteMethod.
- With Indy, incremental SSE requires writing headers manually (ResponseInfo.WriteHeader after setting ContentLength := -1) and then writing to Context.Connection.IOHandler; because Indy does not chunk-encode IOHandler writes, set CloseConnection := True so HTTP/1.1 clients read until close, or implement chunked framing by hand if keep-alive on streamed responses matters. Periodic ': keep-alive' comment writes double as the disconnect probe that implements 'disconnect = cancellation' (HTTP-14).
- TMCPIdHTTPServer.Stop currently just sets Active := False; once long-lived subscriptions/listen streams exist, Stop must signal a shutdown event, write the graceful completion result on each open stream, disconnect, and only then deactivate, otherwise Active := False blocks waiting for handler threads.
- Legacy cancellation (notifications/cancelled over HTTP) can legitimately be ignored ('Servers MAY ignore cancellation notifications if ... the request cannot be cancelled'); without sessions there is no safe way to correlate request ids across clients, so a dual-era server should simply log them.
- Legacy session policy decision for the orchestrator: stateless legacy (never mint Mcp-Session-Id, ignore incoming ids) is spec-conformant and matches the current effective behaviour for conformant clients; minting sessions is only worthwhile if the lifecycle area wants per-client legacy state (negotiated version when the header is absent, logging/setLevel, initialized flag). If sessions are minted they must be set on both JSON and SSE initialize responses.
- Both TMCPStdioTransport and TMCPIdHTTPServer construct their own TMCPJsonRpcProcessor; the new ProcessRequest overload with TMCPRequestContext should be shared so the stdio transport's era handling (server/discover probe, notifications/cancelled) can reuse the same status/error plumbing.
- Dead code to clean up while touching the transport: HTTP_NOT_ACCEPTABLE (IdHTTPServer.pas:88) and KEEP_ALIVE_TIMEOUT (line 79) are unused; the JSON body is parsed twice per request (transport line 341 and processor line 48) — pass the parsed TJSONObject into the processor.
- Non-spec hardening observed: the non-Taurus SSL path enables TLS 1.0/1.1 (IdHTTPServer.pas:396); full request bodies are logged at Info level (line 331) which will include tool arguments and, if added, bearer tokens — add redaction/level control.
- x-mcp-header support depends on the tools/schema area adding an attribute (e.g. [SchemaHeader('Region')]) to MCPServer.Schema.Generator; the transport-side validation (GAP-HTTP-12) should be written against the emitted inputSchema JSON so it also works for hand-built schemas from TMCPToolBase.BuildSchema.
- server/discover is a modern MUST ('Servers MUST implement server/discover', versioning.mdx §Protocol Version Negotiation) and carries only _meta (no Mcp-Name); it must pass the same header validation (MCP-Protocol-Version + Mcp-Method: server/discover). Belongs to the lifecycle/core area but the transport must not special-case it.
- Suggested conformance test list (curl/Delphi DUnitX against a running server): mcp-method lowercase accepted; Mcp-Method: TOOLS/CALL rejected -32020/400; Mcp-Name with surrounding spaces accepted; missing Mcp-Method on modern request → 400/-32020; header/_meta version mismatch → 400/-32020; MCP-Protocol-Version: 1900-01-01 → 400/-32022 with supported[]; no header + no _meta → served as 2025-03-26; no header + _meta → 400/-32020; unknown method modern → 404/-32601, legacy → 200/-32601; notification → 202 with Content-Length: 0; GET/DELETE → 405 + Allow; Origin: http://evil.example → 403 even with CORS disabled; =?base64?SGVsbG8?= (bad padding) → 400/-32020; Base64 UTF-8 Mcp-Name matching a non-ASCII tool name accepted; client closing an SSE stream mid-tool marks the context cancelled.

### Requirements

- **HTTP-01** (MUST, both): Modern: "The server MUST provide a single HTTP endpoint path (hereafter referred to as the MCP endpoint) that supports POST." Legacy (2025-03-26/06-18/11-25): "...that supports both POST and GET methods."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx (intro, before 'Security & Endpoint'); docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Streamable HTTP; 2025-11-25 same_
- **HTTP-02** (MUST, both): "Servers MUST validate the Origin header on all incoming connections to prevent DNS rebinding attacks. If the Origin header is present and invalid, servers MUST respond with HTTP 403 Forbidden. The HTTP response body MAY comprise a JSON-RPC error response that has no id." (2025-06-18 has the first sentence only; 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28 add the 403 sentence.)  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Security & Endpoint item 1; 2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx §Security Warning; 2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Security Warning_
- **HTTP-03** (SHOULD, both): "When running locally, servers SHOULD bind only to localhost (127.0.0.1) rather than all network interfaces (0.0.0.0)."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Security & Endpoint item 2; same wording in 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25 §Security Warning_
- **HTTP-04** (SHOULD, both): "Servers SHOULD implement proper authentication for all connections."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Security & Endpoint item 3; legacy §Security Warning item 3_
- **HTTP-05** (MUST, both): Notification (legacy: notification or response) POST: "If the server accepts it, the server MUST return HTTP status code 202 Accepted with no body. If the server cannot accept it, it MUST return an HTTP error status code (e.g., 400 Bad Request). The HTTP response body MAY comprise a JSON-RPC error response that has no id."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Sending Messages item 5; 2025-06-18 & 2025-11-25 §Sending Messages to the Server item 4_
- **HTTP-06** (MUST, both): Request POST: "If the body is a JSON-RPC request, the server MUST return either Content-Type: application/json (a single JSON object) or Content-Type: text/event-stream (an SSE response stream). The client MUST support both." (Client MUST send Accept listing both types.)  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Sending Messages items 2 and 6; legacy §Sending Messages to the Server items 2 and 5_
- **HTTP-07** (MUST, both): "JSON-RPC messages MUST be UTF-8 encoded."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/index.mdx §Messages; 2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx intro_
- **HTTP-08** (MUST, modern): Modern body shape: "The body of the HTTP POST MUST be a single JSON-RPC request or notification. The client MUST NOT send JSON-RPC responses." Server implication: no batch handling in modern era; a response object or array body is not valid modern input.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Sending Messages item 4_
- **HTTP-09** (MUST, legacy): 2025-03-26 only: "The body of the POST request MUST be one of the following: A single JSON-RPC request, notification, or response; An array batching one or more requests and/or notifications; An array batching one or more responses." and "If the input consists solely of (any number of) JSON-RPC responses or notifications ... MUST return 202"; "If the input contains any number of JSON-RPC requests, the server MUST either return text/event-stream ... or application/json". Batching was removed in 2025-06-18 (changelog item 1).  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-03-26/basic/transports.mdx §Sending Messages to the Server items 3-5; docs/specification/2025-06-18/changelog.mdx item 1_
- **HTTP-10** (MUST, modern): On an SSE response stream: "The server MAY send JSON-RPC notifications — for example, notifications/progress or notifications/message — before the final response. These notifications MUST relate to the originating client request. The server MUST NOT send independent JSON-RPC requests on this stream." and "The final JSON-RPC response SHOULD terminate the stream."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Receiving Messages_
- **HTTP-11** (SHOULD, legacy): Legacy SSE on POST: "The SSE stream SHOULD eventually include a JSON-RPC response for the JSON-RPC request sent in the POST body." "The server MAY send JSON-RPC requests and notifications before sending the JSON-RPC response. These messages SHOULD relate to the originating client request." "The server SHOULD NOT close the SSE stream before sending the JSON-RPC response ... unless the session expires." "After the JSON-RPC response has been sent, the server SHOULD close the SSE stream."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Sending Messages to the Server item 6; 2025-11-25 same section (uses 'terminate')_
- **HTTP-12** (SHOULD, modern): "When initiating an SSE stream, servers SHOULD include the X-Accel-Buffering: no header in the HTTP response."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Receiving Messages_
- **HTTP-13** (MAY, modern): Keep-alive (non-normative encouragement): "For long-lived streams — in particular the subscriptions/listen response stream — servers are encouraged to periodically emit an SSE comment line (a line beginning with a colon, e.g. ':\r\n') as a keep-alive." Also: "Resumable SSE streams via Last-Event-ID are not supported."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Receiving Messages (Note)_
- **HTTP-14** (MUST, modern): "Closing the SSE response stream MUST be treated by the server as cancellation of that request. ... The server SHOULD stop work on the cancelled request as soon as practical and MUST NOT send any further messages for it." Cancellation page: "Streamable HTTP: Closing the SSE response stream is the cancellation signal. The server MUST treat a client disconnect as cancellation of that request. No notifications/cancelled message is required or expected."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Cancellation; docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx §Transport-Specific Cancellation_
- **HTTP-15** (SHOULD NOT, legacy): Legacy: "Disconnection SHOULD NOT be interpreted as the client cancelling its request. To cancel, the client SHOULD explicitly send an MCP CancelledNotification." (Server MAY ignore cancellation notifications it cannot honour.)  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Sending Messages to the Server item 6; 2025-11-25 same_
- **HTTP-16** (MUST, modern): "Every POST request to the MCP endpoint MUST include an MCP-Protocol-Version header." "The header value MUST match the io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion field carried in the request body's _meta. If the values do not match, the server MUST reject the request with 400 Bad Request and a HeaderMismatch JSON-RPC error."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Request Metadata > Protocol Version Header; schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts RequestMetaObject.'io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion' doc comment_
- **HTTP-17** (MUST, modern): "If the server does not implement the requested protocol version (whether the version is unknown to the server, or is a known version the server has chosen not to support), it MUST respond with 400 Bad Request and an UnsupportedProtocolVersionError listing its supported versions." Schema: error.code -32022, error.data.supported: string[], error.data.requested: string; "For HTTP, the response status code MUST be 400 Bad Request."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Protocol Version Header; schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL_VERSION / UnsupportedProtocolVersionError; schema/2026-07-28/examples/UnsupportedProtocolVersionError/unsupported-version.json_
- **HTTP-18** (MUST, modern): "If the server does not implement the requested RPC method, it MUST respond with 404 Not Found and a JSON-RPC error with code -32601 (Method not found). The JSON-RPC error body distinguishes this case from a 404 returned by a legacy HTTP+SSE server."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Protocol Version Header_
- **HTTP-19** (MAY, modern): Dual-era rule: "A server that supports clients implementing protocol versions earlier than 2025-06-18 (which did not define the MCP-Protocol-Version header) MAY treat a request that omits the header as protocol version 2025-03-26. A server that does not support such clients MUST reject a request without the header per Server Validation."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Protocol Version Header_
- **HTTP-20** (MUST, legacy): Legacy header rule: "For backwards compatibility, if the server does not receive an MCP-Protocol-Version header, and has no other way to identify the version ... the server SHOULD assume protocol version 2025-03-26. If the server receives a request with an invalid or unsupported MCP-Protocol-Version, it MUST respond with 400 Bad Request." (Legacy clients send the header only on requests after initialize.)  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Protocol Version Header; 2025-11-25 same; 2025-06-18/basic/lifecycle.mdx Note after Version Negotiation_
- **HTTP-21** (MUST, modern): Standard request headers: Mcp-Method (source: method) required for all requests; Mcp-Name (source: params.name or params.uri) required for tools/call, resources/read, prompts/get. "These headers are REQUIRED for compliance." Validation failure conditions include "A required standard header (MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name) is missing." Notifications: "header requirements for notification POSTs are not defined by this revision."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Standard Request Headers; §Server Validation; §Sending Messages (Note)_
- **HTTP-22** (MUST, modern): "Servers that process the request body MUST reject requests where the values specified in the headers do not match the corresponding values in the request body." "When rejecting a request due to header validation failure, servers MUST return HTTP status 400 Bad Request and MUST include a JSON-RPC error response using ... -32020 HeaderMismatch". Conditions: required standard header missing; header value does not match body value (after Base64 decoding); header value contains invalid characters.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Server Validation; schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts HEADER_MISMATCH = -32020, HeaderMismatchError; schema/2026-07-28/examples/HeaderMismatchError/header-mismatch.json_
- **HTTP-23** (MUST, modern): Base64 sentinel: "Mcp-Param-{Name}: =?base64?{Base64EncodedValue}?=" ... "The same encoding rule applies to the Mcp-Name header value." "These markers are case-sensitive and MUST appear exactly as shown (lowercase). Servers and intermediaries that need to inspect these values MUST decode them accordingly. In particular, servers MUST decode an encoded Mcp-Name or Mcp-Param-{Name} value before comparing it to the corresponding request body value during Server Validation." Encoded payload is Base64 of the UTF-8 representation; SEP-2243 test table: invalid Base64 padding/characters → server MUST reject with 400 + HeaderMismatch.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Value Encoding; seps/2243-http-standardization.md §Test cases (lines 753-754)_
- **HTTP-24** (MAY, modern): x-mcp-header is optional for servers: "MCP servers MAY designate specific tool parameters to be mirrored into HTTP headers using an x-mcp-header extension property". If used, the server MUST enforce the constraints on x-mcp-header values (non-empty, RFC 9110 token, unique case-insensitively, primitive types integer/string/boolean only — no number, statically reachable via properties chain only) and "Server developers SHOULD NOT mark sensitive parameters ... with x-mcp-header".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Custom Headers from Tool Parameters > Schema Extension; docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools.mdx §x-mcp-header_
- **HTTP-25** (MUST, modern): Mcp-Param-{Name} server behaviour: "Servers MUST reject requests with a recognized Mcp-Param-{Name} header that contains invalid characters." "Any server that processes the message body MUST validate that encoded header values, after decoding if Base64-encoded, match the corresponding values in the request body. Servers MUST reject requests with a 400 Bad Request HTTP status and JSON-RPC error code -32020 (HeaderMismatch) if any validation fails." Table: value provided → server MUST validate header matches body; value null / not in arguments → server MUST NOT expect the header; client omits header but value is in body → server MUST reject. Note: "servers SHOULD compare the header value and the body value numerically" for integers. Unrecognized Mcp-Param-* headers: "MUST forward it and otherwise ignore it".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Server Behavior for Custom Headers; §Server Validation (Note)_
- **HTTP-26** (MUST, both): "Header names ... are case-insensitive. Clients and servers MUST use case-insensitive comparisons for header names. Header values (such as method names) are case-sensitive." SEP-2243 tests: 'mcp-method: tools/call' MUST accept; 'Mcp-Method: TOOLS/CALL' MUST reject; 'Mcp-Name:  foo ' MUST accept (trim whitespace).  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Case Sensitivity; seps/2243-http-standardization.md §Test cases (lines 676-687)_
- **HTTP-27** (SHOULD, modern): Modern-only server receiving legacy traffic: "HTTP GET or DELETE to the MCP endpoint: respond with 405 Method Not Allowed. An Mcp-Session-Id header on a request: ignore it, and do not mint or echo session IDs. A Last-Event-ID header: ignore it; streams are not resumable." Dual-era: "Servers and clients that need to interoperate with counterparts speaking those protocol versions implement the behavior described in the corresponding revision ... in addition to the version-negotiation fallback described above."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Backward Compatibility > Earlier Streamable HTTP Revisions_
- **HTTP-28** (MUST, modern): Era detection contract (server side of the client rule): on 400 the client inspects the body; "If the body contains a recognized modern JSON-RPC error, the server speaks a modern version ... If the body is empty or is not a recognized modern JSON-RPC error, fall back to initialize." Hence every 400 a dual-era server returns to a modern request MUST carry the JSON-RPC error body (-32020/-32021/-32022/-32602), otherwise dual-era clients will misdetect the server as legacy. Also for 404: "The JSON-RPC error body distinguishes this case from a 404 returned by a legacy HTTP+SSE server".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Backward Compatibility; §Protocol Version Header; docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx §Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions and §Compatibility Matrix_
- **HTTP-29** (MAY, modern): Dual-era server selection: "A dual-era server selects its behavior from how the client opens: A request carrying modern per-request _meta is served statelessly according to this revision. An initialize request selects legacy semantics, scoped to ... the session (HTTP), as specified by the negotiated legacy protocol version. A dual-era server MAY serve both eras concurrently on the same endpoint or process."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx §Compatibility Matrix (closing paragraphs)_
- **HTTP-30** (MUST, modern): HTTP status mapping for per-request metadata errors: "A request missing any required field [io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion, io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities] is malformed; the server MUST reject it with JSON-RPC error code -32602 (Invalid params). On HTTP, the response status MUST be 400 Bad Request." and MissingRequiredClientCapabilityError (-32021): "On HTTP, the response status MUST be 400 Bad Request."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx §_meta > Per-request protocol fields (lines 373-392)_
- **HTTP-31** (MUST, legacy): Legacy GET: "The server MUST either return Content-Type: text/event-stream in response to this HTTP GET, or else return HTTP 405 Method Not Allowed, indicating that the server does not offer an SSE stream at this endpoint." If a stream is opened: "The server MUST NOT send a JSON-RPC response on the stream unless resuming"; "The server MAY close the SSE stream at any time."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Listening for Messages from the Server items 3-4; 2025-11-25 same_
- **HTTP-32** (MUST, legacy): Legacy sessions: server "MAY assign a session ID at initialization time, by including it in an Mcp-Session-Id header on the HTTP response containing the InitializeResult"; "The session ID MUST only contain visible ASCII characters (0x21 to 0x7E)"; "Servers that require a session ID SHOULD respond to requests without an Mcp-Session-Id header (other than initialization) with HTTP 400"; "The server MAY terminate the session at any time, after which it MUST respond to requests containing that session ID with HTTP 404 Not Found"; client on 404 "MUST start a new session"; DELETE: "The server MAY respond to this request with HTTP 405 Method Not Allowed".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Session Management items 1-5; 2025-11-25 same (header spelled MCP-Session-Id)_
- **HTTP-33** (MUST, legacy): Legacy SSE event IDs: "Servers MAY attach an id field to their SSE events ... If present, the ID MUST be globally unique across all streams within that session—or all streams with that specific client, if session management is not in use." "The server MUST NOT replay messages that would have been delivered on a different stream." 2025-11-25 adds: "The server SHOULD immediately send an SSE event consisting of an event ID and an empty data field in order to prime the client to reconnect" and "If the server does close the connection prior to terminating the SSE stream, it SHOULD send an SSE event with a standard retry field before closing".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Resumability and Redelivery; docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx §Sending Messages to the Server item 6 and §Resumability and Redelivery_
- **HTTP-34** (MUST, legacy): "The server MUST send each of its JSON-RPC messages on only one of the connected streams; that is, it MUST NOT broadcast the same message across multiple streams."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx §Multiple Connections; 2025-11-25 same_
- **HTTP-35** (MUST, modern): Long-lived subscription streams over HTTP: "The server MUST send notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged as the first message ... and MUST NOT send any notification on the subscription before it." Stream ends when "The client cancels it — close the SSE stream (HTTP)"; server teardown: "it SHOULD send a successful subscriptions/listen response to signal a graceful end ... then close the stream." Cancellation page: "A server MUST send notifications/cancelled referencing a subscriptions/listen request ID when it tears down that subscription stream ... Servers MUST NOT send notifications/cancelled for any other purpose." Logging: request-scoped notifications/message "MUST NOT be sent on the listen stream or on any stream other than the one carrying the response to the request".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/subscriptions.mdx §Acknowledgment, §Cancellation, §Graceful Closure; docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx intro; docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/utilities/logging.mdx lines 67-71_
- **HTTP-36** (MAY, both): 403 body for invalid Origin "MAY comprise a JSON-RPC error response that has no id"; non-accepted notification error body "MAY comprise a JSON-RPC error response that has no id".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §Security & Endpoint item 1, §Sending Messages item 5; 2025-11-25 §Security Warning_
- **HTTP-37** (SHOULD NOT, both): Deprecated HTTP+SSE (2024-11-05) transport: "New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it" — a dual-era server is not required to host the old SSE/POST endpoint pair; supporting it is only 'should' guidance for servers 'wanting to support older clients'.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx §HTTP+SSE Transport (2024-11-05) Warning; 2025-06-18 §Backwards Compatibility_

## stdio transport, cancellation, progress, shutdown, dual-era probe (server/discover)

Requirements extracted: 45. Gaps: 12.


### GAP-DE-01 [blocker, effort M] server/discover answered with -32601 (legacy probe outcome); no DiscoverResult, so modern/dual-era clients can never enter modern mode

- Requirements: DE-01, DE-02, DE-03, DE-07
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:121-123 raises 'Method [server/discover] not found...' because no manager handles it (CoreManager.pas:48-53; ToolsManager.pas:64-67; ResourcesManager.pas:59-64); lines 214-215 map any message containing 'not found' to JSONRPC_METHOD_NOT_FOUND (-32601). Per stdio.mdx '## Backward Compatibility' that is exactly the 'any other error → legacy' outcome, so the current binary is a well-behaved legacy server, but can never be detected as modern. Server identity/version live in TMCPSettings.ServerName/ServerVersion (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas:87-88) and MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION = '2025-06-18' (Types.pas:11).
- Required: DE-01: servers MUST implement server/discover; DE-03: dual-era server must answer it with a DiscoverResult — or a recognized modern error (-32022 UnsupportedProtocolVersionError with data.supported/data.requested, DE-02) — before any initialize and regardless of process state; DE-07: answer consistently for the whole process lifetime (also after an initialize has selected legacy semantics). Result shape (schema.ts DiscoverResult extends CacheableResult): `resultType:"complete"`, `supportedVersions:string[]`, `capabilities:{tools:{},resources:{}}`, `ttlMs`, `cacheScope:"public"|"private"`, optional `instructions`, `_meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo:{name,version}` (SHOULD).
- Compat impact: Modern era only; zero effect on legacy clients (they never send server/discover). Library consumers: adding the method inside TMCPCoreManager (or a new TMCPDiscoveryManager registered by default in RunStdioServer/RunHTTPServer) keeps `IMCPCapabilityManager` unchanged; consumers who build their own registry must register the new manager to become dual-era (document in README 'Using as a Library'). Consumers with custom managers that expose extra capabilities need a hook to contribute to `capabilities` — e.g. an optional `IMCPCapabilityDescriber` interface probed with Supports().
- Suggested change: Add `'server/discover'` to `TMCPCoreManager.HandlesMethod` and implement `Discover(const Params: TJSONObject): TValue` (or new unit Managers/MCPServer.DiscoveryManager.pas) that: (a) validates `params._meta` per ST-07 (missing protocolVersion/clientCapabilities → -32602; unsupported version → -32022 with `data.supported` = modern versions list and `data.requested`); (b) returns TJSONObject {resultType:'complete', supportedVersions:[MCP_MODERN_VERSIONS...], capabilities:{tools:{}, resources:{}} (add listChanged/subscribe only when GAP-ST-07 lands; ask other managers via `IMCPCapabilityDescriber`), _meta:{'io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo':{name:FSettings.ServerName, version:FSettings.ServerVersion}}, ttlMs: 3600000, cacheScope:'public'}; (c) never touches FSessionID/initialize state. Add constants in MCPServer.Types: `MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION_2026_07_28 = '2026-07-28'`, `MCP_MODERN_VERSIONS`, `MCP_LEGACY_VERSIONS = ['2025-11-25','2025-06-18','2025-03-26']`; keep `MCP_PROTOCOL_VERSION` (consumers reference it) as the latest legacy default. Replace the `Pos('not found', E.Message)` heuristic (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:214-215) with typed exceptions (`EMCPMethodNotFound`, `EMCPInvalidParams`, `EMCPUnsupportedProtocolVersion` carrying `data`) so a resource/tool error text containing 'not found' can never surface as -32601 and flip a probing client into legacy mode.

### GAP-DE-02 [blocker, effort M] No per-request era detection and no per-process legacy mode: _meta is ignored, initialize always negotiates '2025-06-18', modern requests are served with legacy semantics

- Requirements: DE-05, DE-06, DE-07, DE-09, ST-07, ST-08
- Current: TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest never inspects `params._meta` (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:185-190); the only process state is `TMCPCoreManager.FSessionID` set on initialize (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:97,126) and the result always says `protocolVersion: '2025-06-18'` (CoreManager.pas:101; Types.pas:11) regardless of `params.protocolVersion`, so a 2025-03-26 client asking for 2025-03-26 is told 2025-06-18 (violates DE-09 if the server claims 2025-03-26 support) and a 2025-11-25 client is downgraded (allowed). A modern `tools/call` carrying `_meta` (allowed as first message, DE-06) is processed under legacy semantics: no `resultType`, no serverInfo, no -32022/-32602 checks; a modern `ping` would be answered instead of -32601 (ping removed in 2026-07-28). `notifications/initialized` and `initialize` are accepted at any time and repeatedly (CoreManager.pas:57-63).
- Required: DE-05: modern `_meta` request → stateless modern semantics; `initialize` → legacy semantics scoped to the stdio process at the negotiated legacy version; DE-06/DE-07: both eras may coexist in one process and the era answer must be stable; DE-09: echo the requested legacy version when supported, else the latest supported legacy version; ST-07/ST-08: modern requests validated from their own `_meta` only, never from process state.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: unchanged behaviour except correct version echo (2025-03-26 clients now get 2025-03-26 and batching per GAP-ST-05; 2025-11-25 clients get 2025-11-25). Modern clients: become servable. Library consumers: `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest(RequestBody, SessionID)` retained (delegates to the new overload with a default process session); `IMCPCapabilityManager` untouched; new `IMCPContextAwareManager` optional. Consumers that subclass/replace TMCPCoreManager must adopt the new `TMCPTransportSession` object to get per-process negotiation; otherwise they stay legacy-only (still valid).
- Suggested change: New unit Protocol/MCPServer.Session.pas: `TMCPTransportSession = class  NegotiatedLegacyVersion: string; Initialized: Boolean; Era: TMCPProtocolEra; Lock: TCriticalSection end` — one instance per stdio process (owned by TMCPStdioTransport) and per Mcp-Session-Id on HTTP (cross-area). In TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessMessage: `Era := eraModern if params._meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion'] exists else eraLegacy`; modern path: validate required _meta keys (-32602), version ∈ MCP_MODERN_VERSIONS (-32022), build TMCPRequestContext(ProtocolVersion, ClientCapabilities, ProgressToken) and dispatch through a modern method table (server/discover, tools/list, tools/call, resources/list, resources/read, resources/templates/list, subscriptions/listen; `initialize`/`ping`/`notifications/initialized` → -32601 under modern _meta); legacy path: `initialize` → CoreManager negotiates `params.protocolVersion` ∈ MCP_LEGACY_VERSIONS else latest legacy, stores it in the session, returns it (DE-09); other legacy requests use `Session.NegotiatedLegacyVersion` (policy for legacy requests before initialize: keep today's permissive behaviour and assume the latest legacy version — see notes). Results: add `resultType:'complete'` only on the modern path so legacy responses are byte-compatible with today. Add `Era`/`ProtocolVersion` to IMCPRequestContext so managers (tools/resources) can shape results per era.

### GAP-ST-01 [major, effort L] Synchronous single-threaded stdio loop: one request blocks stdin reading, no serialized async writer, no concurrency

- Requirements: ST-06, ST-08, ST-09, DE-06, CN-01, CN-02, DE-10, PG-09, SD-04
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Server/MCPServer.StdioTransport.pas:54-96 — `while not Eof(Input)` → `Readln` → `FJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest` (synchronous, runs the tool on the reader thread) → `Writeln(Output)`/`Flush`. While a tool executes (ToolsManager.pas:120-128 → Tool.Base.pas:158-168) no further line is read, so a `notifications/cancelled` for that request, a legacy `ping`, or any interleaved modern request sits unread in the pipe until the tool returns. stdout is written only from this one thread; there is no writer abstraction any other component could use to emit `notifications/progress`, `notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged`, subscription notifications, or a graceful `subscriptions/listen` result. Contrast: the HTTP transport already executes the same processor concurrently on Indy worker threads (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:144,488,522).
- Required: Modern: process is not a session; "clients may interleave unrelated requests on the same transport" (ST-08); dual-era server MAY serve both eras concurrently (DE-06); server must be able to emit request-scoped and subscription notifications on the single channel before/alongside responses (ST-06, PG-09) and observe cancellations while work is in flight (CN-01/CN-02). Legacy: ping "MUST respond promptly" (DE-10) even during a long tool call. Any of this needs (a) a reader that keeps consuming stdin while requests run and (b) a single serialized writer for stdout.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: today's behaviour is functionally correct but latency-bound; after the change they gain prompt ping/cancel. Modern clients: without this, subscriptions/listen, cancellation and progress cannot be implemented on stdio at all (blocker for those features). Library consumers: `TMCPStdioTransport.Create(ManagerRegistry, CoreManager)` and `Run` keep their signatures; custom tools/resources/managers will be executed from worker threads on stdio — they already are on HTTP (Indy), so thread-safe implementations are already the contract, but consumers who relied on strict single-threaded stdio execution need an opt-out (`MaxConcurrentRequests := 1`), which preserves in-order sequential dispatch while still allowing the reader/writer split (so cancel/ping are observed). Shared mutable state in the repo that is not thread-safe today: TServerStatusResource counters use plain Inc/Dec (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Resources/MCPServer.Resource.Server.pas:95-108) and TMCPCoreManager.FSessionID (CoreManager.pas:97).
- Suggested change: Unit MCPServer.StdioTransport: (1) add `IMCPMessageWriter = interface  procedure WriteMessage(const Json: string); end` implemented by a `TMCPStdioWriter` that owns a `TCriticalSection` (or a `TThreadedQueue<string>` drained by one writer thread) and performs write+flush of exactly one line per call; (2) `Run` becomes: reader loop on the calling thread reading lines → for notifications call processor synchronously (cheap, must be fast to honour cancel) → for requests enqueue a work item executed on `TTask.Run`/a bounded `TThreadPool` (new property `MaxConcurrentRequests: Integer` default e.g. 8; `1` = sequential compatibility mode) whose completion writes the response through the writer; (3) `TMCPInflightRegistry` (TDictionary<string /*id key*/, IMCPRequestContext> + lock) registered before dispatch and removed after the response is written (or suppressed when cancelled). (4) Expose the writer to managers/tools via the request context (see GAP-CN-01/GAP-PG-01). (5) Keep `Run` blocking until EOF and all in-flight work drained (with a bounded drain timeout so EOF still exits promptly, SD-01). Also mirror the `IMCPMessageWriter` abstraction in MCPServer.IdHTTPServer for the SSE path (cross-area) so tools get the same API on both transports.

### GAP-CN-01 [major, effort M] notifications/cancelled is never dispatched; no in-flight request registry, no cancellation signal reaches tools; responses are always sent

- Requirements: CN-01, CN-02, CN-03, CN-04, CN-05, CN-06, CN-07
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:174-181 — any message without `id` is logged (`'Notification received: ' + MethodName`) and `Exit`s; `notifications/cancelled` params (`requestId`, `reason`) are never read, the reason is not logged (CN-06), and nothing is cancelled. No manager registers it (CoreManager.pas:48-53 only handles initialize/notifications/initialized/ping). `IMCPTool.Execute(const Arguments: TJSONObject)` (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Base.pas:18,41,59,76) and `IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod(Method, Params)` (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.Types.pas:43) carry no cancellation token. The response is unconditionally written (StdioTransport.pas:66-71) even if a cancel arrived. Combined with GAP-ST-01 the cancel for the running request cannot even be read before completion, so the server only ever hits the 'already completed' MAY-ignore case (CN-03).
- Required: CN-01: SHOULD stop processing, free resources, not send a response; CN-02 (modern stdio): "MUST NOT send any further messages for it" once cancelled; CN-03/CN-05: ignore unknown/completed/malformed silently; CN-04: handle the late-arrival race gracefully; CN-06: log the reason; CN-07: server-sent notifications/cancelled only for tearing down a subscriptions/listen stream, and a client cancel referencing a listen id MUST stop that subscription's notifications.
- Compat impact: Both eras (same wire format in 2025-03-26..2026-07-28). Legacy clients that time out and send cancel currently get a late response, which they 'SHOULD ignore' — works by accident but wastes server work. Library consumers: no signature break if cancellation is exposed via a thread-local/context accessor rather than changing `IMCPTool.Execute`; tools that never check cancellation keep running to completion (permitted by CN-03 'cannot be cancelled'), but their response is suppressed by the transport, which is a visible behaviour change only when a client actually cancels. Custom `IMCPCapabilityManager` implementations keep compiling because the interface (with its GUID) is left untouched and a new optional interface is probed with `Supports()`.
- Suggested change: New unit MCPServer.RequestContext: `IMCPRequestContext = interface  function GetRequestId: TJSONValue; function GetProtocolVersion: string; function GetEra: TMCPProtocolEra; function GetProgressToken: TJSONValue; function IsCancelled: Boolean; procedure CheckCancelled; {raises EMCPRequestCancelled} procedure ReportProgress(const Progress: Double; const Total: Double = -1; const Msg: string = ''); function GetWriter: IMCPMessageWriter; end` with a `TMCPRequestContext` implementation (cancel flag via TInterlocked, monotonic progress guard) and a thread-local class property `TMCPRequestContext.Current` set by the transport around dispatch. Add `IMCPContextAwareManager = interface ['{new GUID}'] function ExecuteMethodEx(const Method: string; const Params: TJSONObject; const Context: IMCPRequestContext): TValue; end` — processor does `if Supports(Manager, IMCPContextAwareManager, Ex) then Ex.ExecuteMethodEx(...) else Manager.ExecuteMethod(...)`. Add `IMCPCancellableTool = interface ... function ExecuteWithContext(const Arguments: TJSONObject; const Context: IMCPRequestContext): TValue; end` optionally implemented by TMCPToolBase<T>/<T,R> forwarding to Execute (default) so existing subclasses compile unchanged. In TMCPJsonRpcProcessor add `ProcessMessage(const Line: string; const Session: TMCPTransportSession; out Kind: TMCPMessageKind): string` (old `ProcessRequest` kept, delegating): when Method = 'notifications/cancelled' → parse params.requestId (accept string or number, key on JSON text), log reason at Info, call `InflightRegistry.Cancel(id)`; unknown/finished ids and malformed params → log at Debug and return '' (never write an error for a notification, ST-11). Transport: after the worker finishes, `if Context.IsCancelled then discard response else Writer.WriteMessage(response)`; `ReportProgress` becomes a no-op once cancelled (CN-02). Subscription teardown by client cancel (once GAP-ST-07 exists): registry lookup of listen ids → stop stream, send nothing further. Never emit server-side notifications/cancelled except in the listen-teardown path (CN-07) — enforce by giving the writer no generic 'send cancel' API outside TMCPSubscriptionStream.

### GAP-SD-01 [major, effort S] SIGTERM is swallowed in stdio mode; stdio loop never observes ShutdownEvent; leak-report dialog can block exit

- Requirements: SD-01, SD-02, SD-03
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/MCPServer.dpr:65-72 installs `SignalHandler` for SIGINT/SIGTERM (lines 180-183) that only calls `ShutdownEvent.SetEvent`; `RunStdioServer` (dpr:114-141) never waits on or polls `ShutdownEvent` — `TMCPStdioTransport.Run` exits only on `Eof(Input)` (StdioTransport.pas:54). Because the handler replaces the default terminate action, SIGTERM from a client following the spec escalation no longer kills the process: the blocked `Readln` is either restarted (BSD `signal()` semantics) or raises EInOutError, which the `except` at StdioTransport.pas:73-95 turns into a spurious `{"id":null,"error":{"code":-32603}}` on stdout and then keeps looping. The client must escalate to SIGKILL. Windows: `ConsoleCtrlHandler` (dpr:47-62) returns True for CTRL_C/CTRL_BREAK (suppresses default exit) but clients use TerminateProcess, so only POSIX is affected. Additionally `ReportMemoryLeaksOnShutdown := True` (dpr:169) is unconditional; with the default memory manager on Windows a detected leak is reported via a modal dialog at process exit, which would hold the subprocess open after EOF until the client force-kills it.
- Required: SD-01: exit promptly on EOF (currently met when no leak dialog appears); SD-02: SIGTERM must terminate the process within a reasonable time; SD-03: server may close stdout and exit; ST-02: no non-MCP output (the spurious null-id error).
- Compat impact: Both eras (legacy lifecycle describes the same SIGTERM→SIGKILL escalation). No wire change. Library consumers embedding TMCPStdioTransport in their own .dpr are unaffected by the dpr fix but benefit from `Run` exposing a `Stop`/`RequestShutdown` method they can call from their own signal handler.
