- Suggested change: MCPServer.dpr: in stdio mode either do not install the SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers (leave default terminate action) or make `SignalHandler` set the event AND unblock the reader (POSIX: `__close(0)`; Windows: `CloseHandle(GetStdHandle(STD_INPUT_HANDLE))`) so `Eof(Input)` returns True. TMCPStdioTransport: add `procedure Stop` (sets a volatile FTerminated flag checked by the reader loop, closes stdin handle) and make `Run` honour a `ShutdownEvent: TEvent` property; on EOF/Stop: stop accepting work, drain in-flight requests with a bounded wait (e.g. 2 s), send graceful `subscriptions/listen` completion results for open subscriptions (SD-04, once GAP-ST-07 exists), flush and close stdout, return. Set `ReportMemoryLeaksOnShutdown := {$IFDEF DEBUG}not HasStdioFlag{$ELSE}False{$ENDIF}` (or route leak reports to stderr via FastMM options) so no dialog can block exit. Log 'Signal received' only to stderr (already the case).

### GAP-ST-02 [major, effort S] stdin/stdout use Delphi Text I/O with the system ANSI code page on Windows: non-ASCII JSON is not UTF-8 on the wire

- Requirements: ST-04, ST-01
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Server/MCPServer.StdioTransport.pas:57 `Readln(Input, InputLine)` and :68/:89 `Writeln(Output, ...)` use the RTL `Text` records `Input`/`Output`, which decode/encode UnicodeString with the file's code page = `DefaultSystemCodePage` unless `SetTextCodePage` is called; nothing in the repo calls `SetTextCodePage`. On Windows (the primary target per /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/build.bat Win32/Win64 dcc32/dcc64 builds) that is the ANSI ACP (e.g. 1252), so a request containing e.g. 'é' or an emoji in tool arguments is mis-decoded and a response containing them is not UTF-8 (lossy '?'). On Linux the default is UTF-8 so it works there. `Writeln` also emits CRLF on Windows (not a violation: CR is JSON whitespace, but LF-only is what every SDK reader expects). NOTE: verified against RTL semantics, not by compiling — needs a runtime check on Windows with a non-ASCII echo call.
- Required: ST-04: "JSON-RPC messages MUST be UTF-8 encoded" (both eras); ST-01 newline framing.
- Compat impact: Both eras, Windows builds only. Fix is invisible to clients except that non-ASCII round-trips correctly. Library consumers: internal to `TMCPStdioTransport.Run`; consumers who wrote their own loop with `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor` are unaffected (they own their I/O).
- Suggested change: Minimal: at the top of `TMCPStdioTransport.Run` call `SetTextCodePage(Input, CP_UTF8); SetTextCodePage(Output, CP_UTF8);` and write with `Write(Output, Json + #10); Flush(Output)` instead of `Writeln`. Preferred (needed anyway for GAP-ST-01): replace Text I/O with `THandleStream` over `GetStdHandle(STD_INPUT_HANDLE/STD_OUTPUT_HANDLE)` (Windows) / fd 0 and 1 (POSIX); read with a `TStreamReader.Create(Stream, TEncoding.UTF8, False /*DetectBOM*/)` line reader (or a manual byte buffer split on #10, stripping a trailing #13); write bytes via `TEncoding.UTF8.GetBytes(Json + #10)` directly to the stream — do NOT use `TStreamWriter` with `TEncoding.UTF8`, which can emit a BOM preamble that is not a valid MCP message. Add a test fixture with a non-ASCII `echo` argument.

### GAP-ST-04 [major, effort S] Library consumers must remember to set TLogger.UseStdErr themselves; TMCPStdioTransport itself logs and would write to stdout if they forget

- Requirements: ST-02, ST-03
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/MCPServer.dpr:161-167 sets `TLogger.UseStdErr := True` (only when `HasStdioFlag`) and then `LogToConsole := True`. `TMCPStdioTransport.Run` itself calls `TLogger.Info` (StdioTransport.pas:50-51,62,70,98) and `TLogger.DoWriteLog` writes with `WriteLn(LogLine)` to stdout whenever `FLogToConsole and not FUseStdErr` (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Core/MCPServer.Logger.pas:177-180). Every tool/manager logs via TLogger (e.g. ToolsManager.pas:243,255; CoreManager.pas:81,133). README '### STDIO Transport' (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/README.md:112-130) says logs go to stderr but the 'Using as a Library' section does not tell consumers to set `UseStdErr`.
- Required: ST-02: "The server MUST NOT write anything to its stdout that is not a valid MCP message"; ST-03 stderr logging allowed.
- Compat impact: Both eras. Shipped executable is already correct; the risk is only for library consumers who instantiate `TMCPStdioTransport` in their own program and enable `LogToConsole` — their stdout channel is silently corrupted with log lines. Forcing stderr inside the transport is a behaviour change only for such consumers and only in the direction the spec requires.
- Suggested change: In `TMCPStdioTransport.Create` set `TLogger.UseStdErr := True` (document it) and add a class-level guard `TLogger.StdoutReserved: Boolean` that `DoWriteLog` checks so console logging can never fall back to `Output` while a stdio transport exists; log a one-time warning to stderr if a consumer flips `UseStdErr` back to False. Update README 'Using as a Library' with an explicit stdio checklist (UseStdErr, no `Writeln` in tools, use `TLogger`). Keep `LogToConsole` default False (Logger.pas:103) so unit-initialization `RegisterTool` logs (Registration.pas:56) stay silent.

### GAP-ST-05 [major, effort M] JSON-RPC batch arrays rejected with -32700 (2025-03-26 clients MUST be able to send batches)

- Requirements: ST-10, DE-09
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:52-56 raises 'JSON-RPC request must be an object' for a TJSONArray; the handler at :209-213 then reports JSONRPC_PARSE_ERROR (-32700) with id null because `JSONRequest` is nil — the wrong code even for an unsupported batch (JSON-RPC prescribes -32600). The HTTP path has array awareness only for the 202/notification check (IdHTTPServer.pas:453-465) and then calls the same processor.
- Required: ST-10 (2025-03-26 only): "MUST support receiving JSON-RPC batches"; batching removed from 2025-06-18 onward, so for negotiated versions ≥ 2025-06-18 and for modern requests an array should get -32600 Invalid Request.
- Compat impact: Legacy 2025-03-26 clients only (rare in practice; most SDKs never batched). No effect on 2025-06-18+/modern clients other than a more accurate error code (-32600 instead of -32700). Library consumers: internal to the processor; the returned string may now be a JSON array for batch input — consumers who parse the processor's return value as an object must handle that (document; gate on negotiated version so it cannot happen unless they serve 2025-03-26).
- Suggested change: In `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessMessage`: if the parsed value is a TJSONArray and `Session.NegotiatedLegacyVersion = '2025-03-26'` → iterate elements, process each (requests and notifications), collect non-empty responses into a TJSONArray, return its JSON or '' if all were notifications (empty array → -32600 per JSON-RPC). Otherwise return `CreateErrorResponse(null, -32600, 'Batching not supported')`. Keep `ParseJSONRequest` for the object case. Decide explicitly whether 2025-03-26 is in `MCP_LEGACY_VERSIONS`; if it is dropped, this gap disappears and `initialize` for 2025-03-26 should negotiate up to 2025-06-18 (DE-09 allows that).

### GAP-ST-03 [minor, effort S] Outer stdio exception handler emits an uncorrelated id:null -32603 error for I/O failures and does not stop on a broken stdout

- Requirements: ST-13, ST-02, SD-03
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Server/MCPServer.StdioTransport.pas:73-95: `ProcessRequest` already catches its own exceptions and returns -32700/-32601/-32603 with the request id (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:204-218), so the outer `except` is reached only for failures outside request processing (Readln/Writeln/Flush EInOutError, logger failures, signal-interrupted reads). It then writes `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":null,"error":{"code":-32603,"message":...}}` to stdout — a response that corresponds to no request — and continues the loop; if stdout itself is the broken stream, the nested `Writeln` raises inside the handler and the exception escapes `Run` (dpr:193-195 logs it and the process exits, which is acceptable).
- Required: ST-13: error responses carry the request id except when the id could not be read from a malformed request; a transport-level failure is not that case. ST-02: only valid MCP messages on stdout. SD-03: on a closed output stream the server should just exit.
- Compat impact: Both eras. Clients ignore null-id errors in practice (they cannot correlate them), so removing the write changes nothing visible; exiting on EPIPE makes shutdown cleaner.
- Suggested change: Delete the stdout write from the outer handler: log to stderr with `TLogger.Error`, and if the exception came from the writer (EInOutError on stdout) set FTerminated and break the loop so `Run` returns and the process exits (SD-03). Leave the -32700/null-id parse-error path inside `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor` as the only null-id producer. With GAP-ST-01 the writer thread should treat a write failure the same way (signal shutdown).

### GAP-ST-06 [minor, effort S] id:null silently treated as a notification; client-sent responses (no method) answered with a -32601 error

- Requirements: ST-12, ST-11, ST-05, ST-13
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:75-87 `ExtractRequestID` returns TValue.Empty for a present-but-null (or boolean/object) `id`, and :174-181 then treats the message as a notification and drops it without any error. A message with an `id` but no `method` (a JSON-RPC response — which clients MUST NOT send in 2026-07-28 and which this server never solicits since it never sends requests) falls through to :168-171 (MethodName = '') → :121-123 → error response -32601 'Method [] not found' with the same id, i.e. the server answers a response with an error response.
- Required: ST-12: request id MUST NOT be null (→ -32600 Invalid Request, id null since the id is unusable); ST-11: never respond to notifications; ST-05/patterns: clients do not send responses — a stray response should be ignored/logged, not answered; ST-13 error-id rules.
- Compat impact: Both eras. Only malformed or stray messages are affected; conformant clients see no change.
- Suggested change: In `ProcessMessage`: (a) if the object has key `id` whose value is not TJSONString/TJSONNumber → return -32600 with id null; (b) if `method` is absent: if `result` or `error` present → log at Debug and return '' (ignore), else → -32600 with the parsed id; (c) optionally validate `jsonrpc = '2.0'` → -32600. Keep `ExtractRequestID` but add an `out IdPresent: Boolean` (or a `TMCPRequestId` record with `Kind: (ridNone, ridNull, ridString, ridNumber, ridInvalid)`).

### GAP-PG-01 [optional, effort M] progressToken ignored; no way for a tool/manager to emit notifications/progress before its response

- Requirements: PG-01, PG-02, PG-03, PG-06, PG-07, PG-08, PG-09
- Current: No code reads `params._meta` (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:185-190 passes `params` through untouched; ToolsManager.pas:96-118 extracts only `name`/`arguments`). There is no API to write a notification to stdout while a request is executing (StdioTransport.pas:64-71 writes exactly one response per request). `TMCPToolsCapability.SupportsProgress/SupportsCancellation` (Types.pas:63-71) and the `tools.supportsProgress:false / supportsCancellation:false` pairs emitted by CoreManager.pas:106-114 are not spec fields in any revision (the tools capability only defines `listChanged`).
- Required: PG-01/PG-07: sending progress is MAY, so absence is conformant. But an upgrade that adds long-running tools should implement it, and then PG-02 (token is string|number, echoed verbatim), PG-03 (progress strictly increasing), PG-06 (only for active requests), PG-08 (track tokens; rate-limit), PG-09 (stop after completion) are MUSTs/SHOULDs. Progress must be written on the same channel before the response (ST-06).
- Compat impact: Both eras (identical wire format 2025-03-26..2026-07-28). No effect on clients that send no progressToken. Library consumers: additive API (`TMCPRequestContext.Current.ReportProgress`), no change to `IMCPTool.Execute`; tools that never call it behave exactly as today. Removing the non-standard `supportsProgress/supportsCancellation` keys from the initialize result is a wire change that no known client reads (cross-ref core/lifecycle area).
- Suggested change: In TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessMessage: read `params._meta.progressToken` (clone the TJSONValue so the JSON type — string vs number — is preserved) into the `TMCPRequestContext`. `ReportProgress` implementation: if no token → return; if cancelled or completed (context sealed by the transport right before writing the response) → return; enforce monotonicity (if Progress <= LastProgress then raise EMCPProgressError in DEBUG / clamp+log in RELEASE); rate limit with `MinProgressIntervalMs` (default 100, property on the transport); build `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/progress","params":{"progressToken":<token>,"progress":<double>,"total":<double>?,"message":<string>?}}` with TJSONObject and hand it to `IMCPMessageWriter.WriteMessage` (stdio: locked stdout; HTTP: SSE `data:` event on the open response, cross-area). Track active tokens in the inflight registry (PG-08). Drop the `supportsProgress/supportsCancellation` fields from `TMCPCoreManager.Initialize` and mark `TMCPToolsCapability` properties deprecated (keep the class so consumer code compiles).

### GAP-ST-07 [optional, effort L] No subscriptions/listen support on stdio (ack-first ordering, subscriptionId tagging, cancel-to-stop, graceful close)

- Requirements: ST-06, CN-07, SD-04, ST-05
- Current: No manager handles `subscriptions/listen` (CoreManager.pas:48-53, ToolsManager.pas:64-67, ResourcesManager.pas:59-64); initialize advertises `resources.subscribe:false, listChanged:false` (CoreManager.pas:116-124) so nothing is promised today. There is no server→client notification path at all on stdio (GAP-ST-01). The processor's one-request-one-response model (`ProcessRequest` returns a single string) cannot represent a long-lived request whose response is deferred until teardown.
- Required: If subscriptions are offered: `notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged` MUST be the first message carrying the subscription id and no notification may precede it (per subscription id, interleaving with other subscriptions allowed); every notification on the stream MUST carry `_meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId` = listen request id (ST-06); server MUST NOT send notification types not requested; client `notifications/cancelled` referencing the listen id MUST stop the stream (CN-07/SEP-2575); on server-initiated teardown SHOULD send the `subscriptions/listen` result with `resultType:'complete'` and `_meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId` (SD-04); server-sent `notifications/cancelled` only for tearing down such a stream (CN-07).
- Compat impact: Modern era only (legacy uses resources/subscribe + listChanged capability flags, currently advertised false). Purely additive for clients; only matters once the server advertises `tools.listChanged`/`resources.listChanged`/`resources.subscribe` in DiscoverResult. Library consumers: additive `IMCPSubscriptionSource` hook for managers that can raise list_changed/updated events; no existing API changes.
- Suggested change: Cross-area with the subscriptions/resources area; stdio-specific mechanics: `TMCPSubscriptionStream` (listen request id, agreed `SubscriptionFilter`, `IMCPMessageWriter`) created by a `TMCPSubscriptionsManager` (implements IMCPContextAwareManager for `subscriptions/listen`, returns a sentinel 'deferred' TValue so the transport does not write a response); on creation immediately write `notifications/subscriptions/acknowledged` through the locked writer (guaranteeing ack-first per id since all later notifications for that id go through the same stream object); every notification is built with `params._meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId']` = cloned listen id; the inflight registry maps listen ids to streams so `notifications/cancelled` stops them (CN-07); `TMCPStdioTransport.Run` on EOF/Stop iterates open streams and writes `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":<listenId>,"result":{"resultType":"complete","_meta":{"io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId":<listenId>}}}` before closing stdout (SD-04). Advertise `listChanged`/`subscribe` in DiscoverResult capabilities only when at least one source is registered.

### Already conformant

- ST-01 one message per line, no embedded newlines: responses are produced by `TJSONObject.ToJSON` (compact, escapes control characters) and written with a single `Writeln` — /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Server/MCPServer.StdioTransport.pas:68 and :89 (error path deliberately built with the JSON writer, comment at :78-80); /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:202,140.
- ST-02 nothing but MCP messages on stdout in the shipped executable: the only stdout writes in the tree are StdioTransport.pas:68 and :89 (grep of Writeln/WriteLn across src); the logger is switched to stderr before `LogToConsole` is enabled (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/MCPServer.dpr:161-167) and `TLogger.LogToConsole` defaults to False (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Core/MCPServer.Logger.pas:103), so unit-initialization `TMCPRegistry.RegisterTool` logs (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Core/MCPServer.Registration.pas:56, e.g. Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Echo.pas:46-50) never reach stdout.
- ST-03 stderr logging: `WriteLn(ErrOutput, LogLine)` when `FUseStdErr` (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Core/MCPServer.Logger.pas:177-178); README documents 'Log diagnostic messages to stderr' (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/README.md:123). ST-14: the server already logs to stderr rather than via the deprecated `notifications/message` feature.
- Per-message flush so the client sees each line immediately: `Flush(Output)` at StdioTransport.pas:69 and :93.
- SD-01 exit on EOF: `while not Eof(Input)` (StdioTransport.pas:54) → `Run` returns → `RunStdioServer` frees transport/settings and the program ends (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/MCPServer.dpr:134-140, 188-189); 'STDIO transport stopped - EOF reached' logged at :98.
- ST-05 / CN-07 by construction: there is no code path that writes a JSON-RPC request or a `notifications/cancelled` to stdout; the server only ever writes responses (StdioTransport.pas:64-71).
- ST-11 notifications never get a response: requests without id return '' (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:174-181) and the transport writes nothing when the result is '' (StdioTransport.pas:66).
- CN-03/CN-04/CN-05 degenerate conformance: late or unknown `notifications/cancelled` are logged and ignored, never answered with an error (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:179-180) — the 'processing has already completed' MAY-ignore case.
- PG-07: choosing not to send any progress notifications is explicitly permitted; the server currently sends none.
- ST-13 for malformed input: unparseable lines get -32700 with id null (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:209-217), the one case where a null id is allowed; `ExtractRequestID` is nil-safe (:69-73).
- DE-03 today (as a pure legacy server): `server/discover` yields -32601 'Method [server/discover] not found...' (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:121-123, 214-215), which the 2026-07-28 stdio probe classifies as 'legacy → fall back to initialize', so dual-era clients interoperate with the current binary correctly.
- Blank/whitespace-only lines are skipped rather than answered with a parse error (StdioTransport.pas:59-60) — tolerant of clients that emit an extra newline.
- Legacy shutdown for the HTTP mode: SIGINT/SIGTERM and Windows console control handlers set `ShutdownEvent`, and `RunHTTPServer` waits on it and stops the server cleanly (dpr:46-72, 101-107, 176-183).

### Notes

- Sandbox limitation: Delphi cannot be compiled here. Findings about RTL Text I/O code pages, CRLF line endings and TStreamWriter BOM preambles (GAP-ST-02) are grounded in documented RTL semantics and must be confirmed with a Windows runtime test (echo tool with 'é'/emoji over --stdio) before/after the fix.
- Open design question — `supportedVersions` in DiscoverResult: the versioning page's -32022 example lists both '2026-07-28' and '2025-11-25' under `supported`, but the stdio probe says the client will 'select a mutually supported version from supportedVersions and continue' with per-request _meta. Recommend listing only versions the server serves statelessly (['2026-07-28']) in `supportedVersions`/`data.supported`; legacy versions are reachable only through `initialize` (DE-05). Flag for the core/lifecycle area to decide consistently across stdio and HTTP.
- Open design question — legacy requests (no `_meta`) arriving before any `initialize` on the process: the legacy lifecycle makes initialize-first a client MUST and does not require servers to reject; the current code processes them permissively (CoreManager/ToolsManager never check state). 2026-07-28 stdio.mdx warns that such servers 'would process an era-ambiguous method (such as tools/call) under legacy semantics', which is why modern clients are told to probe. Recommendation: keep permissive behaviour (assume the latest supported legacy version) to avoid breaking existing tests/consumers; document it. Alternative: -32600 'initialize required' — stricter but a behaviour change for library consumers' integration tests.
- Because clients cache the era per process (DE-07), `server/discover` must keep returning DiscoverResult even after an `initialize` has switched the process to legacy mode, and an `initialize` must keep working after modern requests were served (DE-06 'concurrently'). Do not make era a one-way latch on the process; make it a per-request property with a per-process *legacy negotiated version* side-slot.
- Modern-era removals relevant to the stdio dispatcher: `ping`, `logging/setLevel`, `notifications/initialized`, `resources/subscribe` are not modern methods (2026-07-28 changelog items 4-5). Under modern `_meta` they should map to -32601; under legacy they keep working. `notifications/message` must never be emitted for modern requests lacking `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel` (the server never emits it today).
- Concurrency facts for GAP-ST-01: `TMCPJsonRpcProcessor` is stateless (only FManagerRegistry) and is already shared across Indy threads; `TMCPToolsManager.FTools` and `TMCPRegistry` dictionaries are read-only after construction; `TMCPCoreManager.FSessionID` and `TServerStatusResource` counters (Resource.Server.pas:95-108, plain Inc/Dec) are not thread-safe and are already racy under HTTP — fix with TInterlocked when introducing stdio concurrency. `TLogger` is lock-protected (Logger.pas:165-198).
- The `Pos('not found', E.Message) → -32601` heuristic (JsonRpcProcessor.pas:214-215) is a dual-era hazard: once server/discover exists, any exception text containing 'not found' raised while serving a modern request (e.g. 'Resource not found', 'Tool not found') would be reported as -32601, which a probing client could misread as 'legacy server'. Replace with typed exceptions before shipping GAP-DE-01.
- SEP-2575 '### Supporting Multiple Versions' is the design precedent for the dual-era server: keep `initialize` for legacy clients and expose `server/discover` and the stateless RPCs for modern ones on the same process; SEP-2575 'Why is it important for STDIO to be stateless as well?' explains why stdio must not treat process lifetime as a session (matches ST-08 note).
- Legacy `ping` from the client is in scope of the blocking-loop gap: with the current loop a ping during a long tool call is answered only after the tool returns; 2025-11-25 ping.mdx lets the client 'Consider the connection stale; Terminate the connection' on timeout. Concurrency (GAP-ST-01) fixes this without any change to `TMCPCoreManager.Ping`.
- Windows console control handler returns True for CTRL_C/CTRL_BREAK (dpr:47-62), suppressing default termination; this does not affect MCP clients, which use TerminateProcess/Job Objects, but means a human running `MCPServer.exe --stdio` in a console cannot Ctrl+C it (only Ctrl+Z/EOF works). Consider not installing the handler in stdio mode.
- StdioTransport.pas:62 and :70 log the full request and response lines at Info to stderr. Allowed by ST-03, but clients 'MAY capture' stderr, so tool arguments (possibly secrets) end up in client logs; consider Debug level or truncation.
- README (/home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/README.md:112-130 '### STDIO Transport', and 'Using as a Library') should gain: dual-era behaviour (server/discover probe vs initialize), cancellation/progress support and how tools use `TMCPRequestContext.Current`, the `MaxConcurrentRequests` opt-out, the `TLogger.UseStdErr` requirement for embedders, and the exact stdout framing (UTF-8, LF, one message per line).
- Timeouts (cancellation.mdx '## Timeouts') apply to the *sender* of requests; since the server never sends requests in the modern era (and sends none today in legacy either), no server-side timeout requirement applies here. If the HTTP transport ever emits legacy server→client requests (sampling/elicitation) that is another area.
- The ecosystem's stdio clients (Codex per README:360-373, Claude Desktop, MCP Inspector) all split stdout on '\n' and tolerate a trailing '\r'; switching to LF-only output (GAP-ST-02) is therefore safe and removes a Windows/Linux difference in the byte stream.

### Requirements

- **ST-01** (MUST NOT, both): "Messages are delimited by newlines, and MUST NOT contain embedded newlines." Each line is exactly one JSON-RPC request, notification, or response.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx (intro bullet list); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx '## stdio'; identical in 2025-06-18 and 2025-03-26_
- **ST-02** (MUST NOT, both): "The server MUST NOT write anything to its stdout that is not a valid MCP message."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx (intro bullet list); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx '## stdio'_
- **ST-03** (MAY, both): "The server MAY write UTF-8 strings to stderr for any logging purposes including informational, debug, and error messages." (2025-06-18 wording: "for logging purposes"). Client "SHOULD NOT assume stderr output indicates error conditions" (2025-11-25+).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx (intro bullet list); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx '## stdio'_
- **ST-04** (MUST, both): "MCP uses JSON-RPC to encode messages. JSON-RPC messages MUST be UTF-8 encoded."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/index.mdx '## Messages'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx (first paragraph)_
- **ST-05** (MUST NOT, modern): "The server MUST NOT write JSON-RPC requests to stdout. Server-to-client interactions are carried in InputRequiredResult replies"; patterns: "Servers MUST NOT initiate JSON-RPC requests, and clients do not send JSON-RPC responses."; transports overview: "No other message direction exists".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Receiving Messages'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/index.mdx (intro); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/index.mdx '## Messages'_
- **ST-06** (MUST, modern): Server writes three kinds of messages on the single channel: responses correlated by id; request-scoped notifications (notifications/progress, notifications/message); notifications for an active subscriptions/listen request, which "Clients MUST correlate ... using the io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId field in _meta". basic/index: "On notifications delivered via a subscriptions/listen stream, the server MUST include io.modelcontextprotocol/subscriptionId in _meta". "All messages share this single channel; there are no per-request streams."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Receiving Messages'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '### _meta' (Per-response protocol fields)_
- **ST-07** (MUST, modern): "All request metadata for the stdio transport is carried inline in the JSON-RPC message body. The protocol version, per-request capabilities, and optional client identity live in _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/*; ... There is no header layer." basic/index: fields io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion and io.modelcontextprotocol/clientCapabilities are Required and "MUST be included on every request"; "A request missing any required field is malformed; the server MUST reject it with JSON-RPC error code -32602 (Invalid params)."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Request Metadata'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '### _meta' (Per-request protocol fields)_
- **ST-08** (MUST NOT, modern): "Servers MUST NOT rely on prior requests over the same connection to establish context (e.g., capabilities, protocol version, client identity). Every request supplies this metadata in its _meta field." Note: "an open connection, such as a STDIO process, is not a conversation or session: clients may interleave unrelated requests on the same transport, and a server must not treat connection or process identity as a proxy for conversation or session continuity."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '## Statelessness'_
- **ST-09** (SHOULD NOT, modern): "Servers SHOULD NOT require that a client reuse the same connection or process to perform related operations." and "Servers SHOULD be prepared to handle requests associated with multiple tasks, threads, or conversations."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '## Statelessness'_
- **ST-10** (MUST, legacy): 2025-03-26 only: "MCP implementations MAY support sending JSON-RPC batches, but MUST support receiving JSON-RPC batches." stdio section: "Messages may be JSON-RPC requests, notifications, responses—or a JSON-RPC batch containing one or more requests and/or notifications." Removed in 2025-06-18 (changelog major change 1: "Remove support for JSON-RPC batching").  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-03-26/basic/index.mdx '### Batching'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-03-26/basic/transports.mdx '## stdio'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-06-18/changelog.mdx '## Major changes'_
- **ST-11** (MUST NOT, both): Notifications: "The receiver MUST NOT send a response." and "Notifications MUST NOT include an ID."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '### Notifications' (same text in 2025-11-25/basic/index.mdx)_
- **ST-12** (MUST NOT, both): Requests: "Requests MUST include a string or integer ID. Unlike base JSON-RPC, the ID MUST NOT be null." Server implication: a message with id:null is neither a valid request nor a notification and should be rejected as Invalid Request (-32600), not silently treated as a notification.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '### Requests' (same in 2025-11-25)_
- **ST-13** (MUST, both): "Error responses MUST include the same ID as the request they correspond to (except in error cases where the ID could not be read due a malformed request)." A null-id error is therefore reserved for unparseable/malformed input.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx '#### Error Responses'_
- **ST-14** (SHOULD, modern): Logging feature deprecated: "New implementations SHOULD NOT adopt it; existing implementations SHOULD migrate to logging to stderr for stdio transports, or to OpenTelemetry". Also "servers MUST NOT emit notifications/message for requests that did not include [io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel]".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/utilities/logging.mdx (Warning box); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/changelog.mdx '## Major changes' item 5_
- **CN-01** (SHOULD, both): "Servers receiving cancellation notifications SHOULD: Stop processing the cancelled request; Free associated resources; Not send a response for the cancelled request". On stdio the client "MUST send a notifications/cancelled notification referencing the request ID" (params.requestId, optional params.reason) — so a server must be able to receive and dispatch it while the request is in flight.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx '## Behavior Requirements' item 3 and '## Transport-Specific Cancellation'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/utilities/cancellation.mdx '## Behavior Requirements' item 4 ('Receivers of cancellation notifications SHOULD')_
- **CN-02** (MUST NOT, modern): "Servers SHOULD stop work on a cancelled request as soon as practical and MUST NOT send any further messages for it." (no response, no progress, no notifications/message after honoring a cancel).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Cancellation'; also /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/seps/2575-stateless-mcp.md '#### Request Cancellation'_
- **CN-03** (MAY, both): "Servers MAY ignore cancellation notifications if: The referenced request is unknown; Processing has already completed; The request cannot be cancelled".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx '## Behavior Requirements' item 4; 2025-11-25 utilities/cancellation.mdx item 5_
- **CN-04** (MUST, both): "Due to network latency, cancellation notifications may arrive after request processing has completed, and potentially after a response has already been sent. Both parties MUST handle these race conditions gracefully".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx '## Timing Considerations'; same heading in 2025-11-25 utilities/cancellation.mdx_
- **CN-05** (SHOULD, both): "Invalid cancellation notifications SHOULD be ignored: Unknown request IDs; Already completed requests; Malformed notifications" — preserving fire-and-forget semantics (never answer a notification with an error).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx '## Error Handling'; same in 2025-11-25_
- **CN-06** (SHOULD, both): "Both parties SHOULD log cancellation reasons for debugging".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx '## Implementation Notes'; same in 2025-11-25_
- **CN-07** (MUST NOT, modern): "A server MUST send notifications/cancelled referencing a subscriptions/listen request ID when it tears down that subscription stream. Servers MUST NOT send notifications/cancelled for any other purpose." schema: "Servers MUST NOT use this notification to cancel any other request." SEP-2575: on client cancel of a listen request "The server MUST stop sending notifications for that subscription."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/cancellation.mdx (intro); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts 'CancelledNotification' doc comment; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/seps/2575-stateless-mcp.md '#### Stopping a Subscription'_
- **CN-08** (MUST NOT, legacy): Client-side rule with server implication: "The initialize request MUST NOT be cancelled by clients" (2025-03-26..2025-11-25) and, 2025-11-25 only, "For task-augmented requests, the tasks/cancel request MUST be used instead of the notifications/cancelled notification." A server may treat a cancel of initialize as invalid and ignore it (CN-05).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/utilities/cancellation.mdx '## Behavior Requirements' items 2-3_
- **PG-01** (MAY, both): "The server MAY send progress notifications to report the status of requests the client has issued." Opt-in: client puts progressToken in params._meta.progressToken; server sends notifications/progress with params {progressToken, progress, total?, message?}.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx (intro, '## Progress Flow'); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/utilities/progress.mdx (same, 'receiver MAY')_
- **PG-02** (MUST, both): "Progress tokens MUST be a string or integer value" and "MUST be unique across all active requests" (client chooses). Server must accept and echo either JSON type unchanged (schema: ProgressToken = string | number).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Progress Flow'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts 'export type ProgressToken'_
- **PG-03** (MUST, both): "The progress value MUST increase with each notification, even if the total is unknown."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Progress Flow'; same in 2025-11-25_
- **PG-04** (MAY, both): "The progress and the total values MAY be floating point."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Progress Flow'_
- **PG-05** (SHOULD, both): "The message field SHOULD provide relevant human readable progress information."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Progress Flow'_
- **PG-06** (MUST, both): "Progress notifications MUST only reference tokens that: Were provided in an active request; Are associated with an in-progress operation".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Behavior Requirements' item 1; same in 2025-11-25_
- **PG-07** (MAY, both): "Servers receiving a request with a progress token MAY: Choose not to send any progress notifications; Send notifications at whatever frequency they deem appropriate; Omit the total value if unknown". Schema: "The receiver is not obligated to provide these notifications."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Behavior Requirements' item 2; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts 'RequestMetaObject.progressToken'_
- **PG-08** (SHOULD, both): "Clients and servers SHOULD track active progress tokens"; "Both parties SHOULD implement rate limiting to prevent flooding".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Implementation Notes'_
- **PG-09** (MUST, both): "Progress notifications MUST stop after completion." Patterns overview: "While the request is in flight, the server MAY send notifications scoped to it, such as notifications/progress" — i.e. progress precedes the response on the same channel.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/progress.mdx '## Implementation Notes'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/index.mdx '## Request and Response'_
- **SD-01** (SHOULD, modern): "Servers SHOULD exit promptly when their standard input is closed or reads return end-of-file. This is the primary graceful-shutdown signal and the only portable one, so honoring it reduces the need for forced termination." (Legacy revisions describe the same client procedure — close stdin, wait, SIGTERM, SIGKILL — without an explicit server keyword.)  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Shutdown'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx '### Shutdown' > '#### stdio'_
- **SD-02** (SHOULD, both): Client shutdown escalation: "1. Closing the input stream ... 2. Waiting for the server to exit. 3. If the server does not exit within a reasonable time, forcibly terminating the process ... On POSIX systems, forced termination typically escalates from SIGTERM to SIGKILL. On Windows ... TerminateProcess or Job Objects." Server implication: SIGTERM must remain an effective termination signal.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Shutdown'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx '#### stdio'_
- **SD-03** (MAY, both): "The server MAY initiate shutdown by closing its output stream to the client and exiting."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Shutdown'; 2025-11-25 lifecycle.mdx '#### stdio'_
- **SD-04** (SHOULD, modern): "When the server ends a subscription on its own initiative (for example, during shutdown), it SHOULD respond to the original subscriptions/listen request with a completion result before closing the stream." Also "Active subscriptions/listen streams must also be re-established after restart" (client), so the server holds no subscription state across restarts.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/subscriptions.mdx '### Graceful Closure'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Unexpected Termination'_
- **DE-01** (MUST, modern): "Servers MUST implement server/discover." It "lets a client query a server's supported protocol versions, capabilities, and identity before sending any other requests." Request carries only _meta; result is DiscoverResult {resultType, supportedVersions: string[], capabilities: ServerCapabilities, instructions?, ttlMs, cacheScope, _meta['io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo'] (SHOULD)}.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '## Protocol Version Negotiation'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/discover.mdx (intro, '### DiscoverResult'); /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts 'DiscoverRequest'/'DiscoverResult'_
- **DE-02** (MUST, modern): "If the server does not implement the requested version (whether the version is unknown to the server, or is a known version the server has chosen not to support), it MUST respond with an UnsupportedProtocolVersionError listing the versions it does support": error.code -32022, error.data.supported: string[], error.data.requested: string.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '## Protocol Version Negotiation'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts 'UnsupportedProtocolVersionError'_
- **DE-03** (MUST, modern): Probe semantics a dual-era server must satisfy: "The server returns a DiscoverResult: the server is modern." "The server returns a recognized modern JSON-RPC error such as UnsupportedProtocolVersionError: the server is modern ... Do not fall back to initialize." "The server returns any other error, or does not respond within a reasonable timeout: the server is legacy." "The fallback MUST NOT be keyed to one specific error code: legacy servers respond to unknown pre-initialize requests with implementation-defined errors (commonly -32601 or -32602) or not at all." Hence a dual-era server must answer server/discover with DiscoverResult (or -32022) before any initialize, and must never answer it with -32601/-32602/-32603.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Backward Compatibility'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '### Compatibility Matrix' rows 'Dual-era | Modern' and 'Legacy | Dual-era'_
- **DE-04** (MAY, both): "A server that wishes to support both legacy clients (which expect an initialize handshake) and modern clients (which use per-request metadata) MAY implement both behaviors." SEP-2575: "Such a server can continue to implement the old initialize RPC to handle legacy clients, while also exposing the new stateless RPCs (server/discover, etc.) for updated clients."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '## Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/seps/2575-stateless-mcp.md '### Supporting Multiple Versions'_
- **DE-05** (MUST, both): (Declarative normative text, no RFC keyword.) "A dual-era server selects its behavior from how the client opens: A request carrying modern per-request _meta is served statelessly according to this revision. An initialize request selects legacy semantics, scoped to the stdio process (stdio) or the session (HTTP), as specified by the negotiated legacy protocol version."  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '### Compatibility Matrix' (closing paragraphs)_
- **DE-06** (MAY, both): "A dual-era server MAY serve both eras concurrently on the same endpoint or process." And "A client that only supports modern versions does not need to probe" — so a modern request (e.g. tools/call with _meta) may be the first message on the process and must be served without initialize.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '### Compatibility Matrix'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx '## Backward Compatibility'_
- **DE-07** (SHOULD, modern): "The era determination is a property of the server, not of an individual request. Clients SHOULD cache the result for the lifetime of the server process (stdio) ... and MAY persist it across restarts of the same server configuration". Server implication: server/discover must answer identically for the whole process lifetime, including after an initialize has put the process into legacy mode.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '## Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions'_
- **DE-08** (SHOULD, modern): Conditional (only if legacy support is ever dropped): "A server that supports only modern versions SHOULD name the protocol versions it supports in any error it returns to an initialize request, on any transport".  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx '## Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions'_
- **DE-09** (MUST, legacy): Legacy version negotiation that fixes the per-process legacy semantics: "If the server supports the requested protocol version, it MUST respond with the same version. Otherwise, the server MUST respond with another protocol version it supports. This SHOULD be the latest version supported by the server." "The initialization phase MUST be the first interaction between client and server." (client obligation; server not required to enforce).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/lifecycle.mdx '#### Version Negotiation', '### Initialization'_
- **DE-10** (MUST, legacy): ping (legacy only; removed in 2026-07-28 changelog item 5): "The receiver MUST respond promptly with an empty response"; sender "MAY ... Consider the connection stale; Terminate the connection" on timeout. Server implication on stdio: a ping must be answerable while another request is executing.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/utilities/ping.mdx '## Behavior Requirements'; /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/changelog.mdx '## Major changes' item 5_

## Tools: definitions, JSON Schema 2020-12, annotations, icons, structuredContent, tool errors, x-mcp-header, naming, tools/list envelope (resultType/ttlMs/cacheScope/pagination/ordering), MRTR and progress on tools/call

Requirements extracted: 29. Gaps: 25.


### GAP-TL-01 [blocker, effort S] tools/list result lacks resultType, ttlMs and cacheScope

- Requirements: TL-04, TL-05, TL-06
- Current: TMCPToolsManager.BuildToolListResponse (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:213-228) emits only {"tools": [...]}. No manager or the processor (src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:192-200) adds an envelope.
- Required: Modern results MUST carry resultType:"complete"; tools/list MUST additionally carry ttlMs (>=0) and cacheScope ("public"|"private"), same cacheScope on every page. schema.json 2026-07-28 marks all four as required, so a validating modern client rejects the current response.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients tolerate the extra fields: Result in schema.json 2025-06-18/2025-11-25 has additionalProperties:{} (open), so emitting ttlMs/cacheScope unconditionally is safe. Library consumers: custom IMCPCapabilityManager implementations get resultType for free if it is injected in TMCPJsonRpcProcessor; TMCPToolsManager API unchanged, two new optional properties.
- Suggested change: (1) In TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest, after ExecuteMethodCall, when the request is modern (per-request _meta present / negotiated era from the lifecycle area) and the result object has no 'resultType', add 'resultType':'complete' (and merge _meta.io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo, see TL-25) — this covers custom managers. (2) In TMCPToolsManager add published properties ListTtlMs: Integer (default 0) and ListCacheScope: string (default 'public'); BuildToolListResponse adds TJSONNumber(ListTtlMs) as 'ttlMs' and 'cacheScope' always (harmless for legacy). Optionally read defaults from TMCPSettings (ToolsListTtlMs/ToolsListCacheScope in settings.ini). Because the list is static (built once in the constructor) a positive TTL such as 300000 is safe; keep 0 as the conservative default.

### GAP-TL-02 [blocker, effort S] tools/call result lacks resultType

- Requirements: TL-04
- Current: TMCPToolsManager.BuildToolCallResponse (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:130-183) never adds resultType; CallToolResult.required = [content, resultType] in schema.json 2026-07-28.
- Required: Every modern result MUST include resultType ("complete", or "input_required" for MRTR).
- Compat impact: Legacy clients ignore/tolerate the extra field (open Result). No library API change if injected centrally.
- Suggested change: Same envelope injection as GAP-TL-01 (processor-level, era-aware). ToolsManager must set resultType itself only for InputRequiredResult ('input_required', GAP-TL-19) so the processor's 'add if absent' rule leaves it alone.

### GAP-TL-03 [blocker, effort S] TMCPToolBase<T,R> results have structuredContent but no content array

- Requirements: TL-17, TL-20, TL-19
- Current: TMCPToolBase<T,R>.Execute returns a TJSONObject (src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Base.pas:183-202); BuildToolCallResponse's TJSONObject branch (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:168-181) adds only 'structuredContent' (+ optional isError) — the required 'content' field is absent and no text fallback is produced.
- Required: CallToolResult.content is required in every revision (schema.json required=[content] / [content,resultType]); a tool returning structured content SHOULD also return the serialized JSON in a TextContent block (MUST for non-object values per SEP-2106).
- Compat impact: Purely additive on the wire; consumers using TMCPToolBase<T,R> get a valid result. No API change.
- Suggested change: In BuildToolCallResponse's TJSONObject branch, also add 'content': [ {type:'text', text: JsonResult.ToJSON} ] before 'structuredContent'. Do the same in the new TMCPToolResult path (GAP-TL-15) when SetStructuredContent is used without explicit text.

### GAP-TL-04 [major, effort S] Unknown tool returns an isError result instead of JSON-RPC -32602

- Requirements: TL-21, TL-28
- Current: TMCPToolsManager.CallTool (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:245-248) sets ResultValue := 'Error: Tool not found: X' which BuildToolCallResponse turns into {content:[text], isError:true}. Raising a plain Exception would not help: TMCPJsonRpcProcessor maps any message containing 'not found' to -32601 (src/Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:214-215) and everything else to -32603.
- Required: "Unknown tool" is a protocol error returned as a JSON-RPC error with code -32602 (both eras).
- Compat impact: Clients that relied on a text result for a missing tool (none spec-conformant) see a JSON-RPC error instead. Library consumers: new exception class is additive; custom managers may adopt it to control error codes.
- Suggested change: Add to MCPServer.Types (or new MCPServer.Errors.pas): EMCPProtocolError = class(Exception) with property Code: Integer and optional Data: TJSONValue; constants MCP_ERROR_INVALID_PARAMS = -32602 etc. TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest catches EMCPProtocolError first and uses E.Code (and data) in CreateErrorResponse; keep the existing heuristic for other exceptions. TMCPToolsManager.CallTool raises EMCPProtocolError.Create(-32602, 'Unknown tool: ' + ToolName) when FTools.TryGetValue fails.

### GAP-TL-05 [major, effort S] Malformed tools/call params produce an isError result instead of -32602

- Requirements: TL-21, TL-29
- Current: ExtractToolNameAndArguments (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:96-118) returns False for nil params / missing or empty name; a non-string name is silently stringified (NameValue.Value) and a non-object 'arguments' is silently treated as absent (line 116-117). CallTool then returns BuildToolCallResponse('Error: Invalid tool parameters') (lines 237-241), i.e. isError:true.
- Required: "Malformed requests (requests that fail to satisfy CallToolRequest schema)" are protocol errors (-32602).
- Compat impact: Wire behaviour changes only for invalid requests. No library API change.
- Suggested change: In ExtractToolNameAndArguments raise EMCPProtocolError(-32602, 'Invalid params: name must be a non-empty string') when Params=nil, name missing, or NameValue is not TJSONString; raise EMCPProtocolError(-32602, 'Invalid params: arguments must be an object') when 'arguments' exists but is not TJSONObject. Keep Arguments=nil (or empty object, see GAP-TL-06) when the key is absent.

### GAP-TL-06 [major, effort S] tools/call without 'arguments' passes a nil TJSONObject into the deserializer (nil dereference)

- Requirements: TL-29, TL-21
- Current: ExtractToolNameAndArguments leaves Arguments=nil when the key is absent (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:103, 115-117); TMCPToolBase<T>.Execute / <T,R>.Execute call TMCPSerializer.Deserialize<T>(Arguments) unconditionally (src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Base.pas:162, 189); DeserializeObject iterates 'for Pair in Json do' on the nil object (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Serializer.pas:111) — this dereferences nil and is expected to raise an access violation, which ExecuteTool (ToolsManager.pas:122-127) converts into {isError:true, text:'Error executing tool: Access violation ...'}. (Could not compile/run Delphi in this environment; conclusion is from reading the code path.) Even the zero-parameter get_time tool hits this path when a client omits 'arguments', which the schema explicitly allows.
- Required: `arguments` is optional in CallToolRequestParams in all revisions; a request without it must be processed normally.
- Compat impact: Fix is invisible to conforming clients; library consumers' tools receive an empty object instead of nil (custom TMCPToolBase.Execute implementations that already guard nil keep working).
- Suggested change: In TMCPToolsManager.CallTool, when 'arguments' is absent create a local empty TJSONObject (owned by the manager, freed after Execute) and pass it; additionally make TMCPSerializer.DeserializeObject nil-safe (if not Assigned(Json) then Exit after the required-check of GAP-TL-07). Add a unit test/manual test: {"method":"tools/call","params":{"name":"get_time"}}.

### GAP-TL-07 [major, effort M] Tool input validation is incomplete (required not enforced, types silently coerced, hand-written schemas never validated)

- Requirements: TL-22, TL-10, TL-21
- Current: TMCPSerializer.DeserializeObject skips properties absent from the JSON (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Serializer.pas:130-131) so a missing required parameter silently keeps the default value although the schema advertised it in 'required' (Schema.Generator.pas:93-94, 140-150). Type mismatches are coerced: non-numeric JSON for Integer → StrToIntDef(...,0) (Serializer.pas:199-209), Float → 0, Boolean from any string (lines 224-236), wrong-typed array elements dropped (lines 403-410). Only unknown parameter names (lines 111-118) and enum values (lines 285-288) are rejected. Tools deriving from the non-generic TMCPToolBase (Tool.Base.pas:27-42, BuildSchema/Execute abstract) get no validation at all.
- Required: "Servers **MUST**: Validate all tool inputs"; "servers **MUST** validate schemas according to their declared or default dialect"; validation failures are tool execution errors with actionable text (SEP-1303).
- Compat impact: Behaviour change: tools whose callers omitted a parameter that is not marked [Optional] now get isError:true instead of a default value — this is what the advertised schema already promised. Provide TMCPSerializer.StrictMode (default True) as an escape hatch. Custom TMCPToolBase tools unaffected unless they call the new validator.
- Suggested change: (a) In DeserializeObject, before the property loop, raise EArgumentException('Missing required parameter "%s"') for every writable property without OptionalAttribute that is absent from Json; raise EArgumentException('Parameter "%s": expected integer, got string') on type-kind mismatches instead of StrToIntDef/StrToFloatDef fallbacks. (b) Add MCPServer.Schema.Validator.pas with class function TMCPSchemaValidator.Validate(const Schema: TJSONObject; const Instance: TJSONValue; out Errors: TArray<string>): Boolean covering type, required, enum, properties (recursive), items, additionalProperties, minimum/maximum, minLength/maxLength, pattern (TRegEx), const, nullable type arrays; never resolve $ref (same-document '#/$defs/...' only), depth cap (TL-11). (c) TMCPToolsManager.ExecuteTool validates Arguments against Tool.InputSchema when the tool does not derive from the generic bases (or always, cheap) and returns isError with the joined messages. (d) Document supported dialect (2020-12 subset) in README.

### GAP-TL-08 [major, effort S] Schema generator maps Integer/Int64 to "number" instead of "integer"

- Requirements: TL-10, TL-15
- Current: TMCPSchemaGenerator.GetJsonTypeFromRttiType: tkInteger, tkInt64 → 'number' (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Schema.Generator.pas:115).
- Required: JSON Schema 2020-12 has a distinct "integer" type; the basic spec's own example uses {"type":"integer","minimum":0}; x-mcp-header MUST only be applied to integer/string/boolean and "Parameters with type `number` are not permitted", so no Integer parameter of this library could ever be header-mirrored; LLM clients may also send 3.5 for a 'number'.
- Compat impact: Emitted schema text changes (consumers snapshotting schemas in tests must update). The deserializer already accepts integers; no runtime behaviour change for clients.
- Suggested change: Change the mapping to 'integer' for tkInteger/tkInt64 (and tkChar/tkWChar → 'string'). Keep tkFloat → 'number', but map TDateTime/TDate/TTime (RttiType.Handle = TypeInfo(TDateTime) etc.) to 'string' with 'format':'date-time' (and teach ConvertJsonToValue/ConvertValueToJson ISO 8601 via System.DateUtils.ISO8601ToDate/DateToISO8601). Detect Boolean via RttiType.Handle = TypeInfo(Boolean) (also ByteBool/WordBool/LongBool) instead of RttiType.Name = 'Boolean' (line 119).

### GAP-TL-09 [major, effort M] Nested objects emit no properties and arrays emit empty items; sets emit untyped arrays

- Requirements: TL-10, TL-08, TL-19
- Current: GetJsonTypeFromRttiType returns bare 'object' for any tkClass other than TJSONArray (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Schema.Generator.pas:124-128) and GenerateSchema adds 'items': {} for every array (lines 68-69) — although TMCPSerializer can deserialize nested classes, TArray<T> and TList<T> with typed elements (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Serializer.pas:242-257, 377-443). tkSet → 'array' with empty items (line 123). Result: LLMs cannot see the shape of nested inputs, and generated outputSchemas under-describe structuredContent.
- Required: Schemas MUST be valid 2020-12 (they are) but MUST also describe inputs so the server can 'Validate all tool inputs'; outputSchema is meant to let clients 'validate structured results'. The generator should emit the full shape it knows from RTTI.
- Compat impact: Additive schema detail; no wire-breaking change. Library consumers with nested param classes get richer schemas automatically.
- Suggested change: Refactor TMCPSchemaGenerator into a recursive GenerateTypeSchema(RttiType, Visited: TList<PTypeInfo>, Depth): TJSONObject: tkClass (non-TJSONValue) → recurse into properties (object with properties/required); TJSONObject/TJSONValue → {} (any); TJSONArray → {type:array}; TRttiDynamicArrayType → items := GenerateTypeSchema(ElementType); TList<T>/TObjectList<T> → items from the 'Add' parameter type (reuse FindAddMethod logic); tkSet → {type:array, items:{type:string, enum:[...]}, uniqueItems:true}; tkRecord → object over fields; cycle guard by Visited/Depth (emit {} on cycle). Share one RTTI→JSON-type table between generator and serializer (GAP-TL-10).

### GAP-TL-10 [major, effort M] structuredContent produced by TMCPToolBase<T,R> can violate the generated outputSchema

- Requirements: TL-19, TL-18
- Current: GenerateSchema(R) marks every read/write property as required (Schema.Generator.pas:93-94, 140-150), but TMCPSerializer.Serialize/ConvertValueToJson omits: (a) dynamic-array/TList properties — there is no tkDynArray branch and TList is not a TJSONValue (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Serializer.pas:333-374), so array-typed properties that the schema declares as required arrays never appear; (b) nil object properties (Value.IsEmpty → Exit at line 330) declared as required objects; (c) non-Boolean enumerations are serialized through Value.AsBoolean (lines 346-356) while the schema says {type:string, enum:[...]} (lines 87-91, 152-170) — wrong type or EInvalidCast. Only object roots are produced (fine for legacy).
- Required: "Servers **MUST** provide structured results that conform to this schema."
- Compat impact: Output for tools using arrays/enums/nil objects in R changes from missing/incorrect fields to correct ones; consumers relying on the current omission are unlikely. No API change.
- Suggested change: In ConvertValueToJson add tkDynArray (iterate via Value.GetArrayLength/GetArrayElement → TJSONArray), tkClass TList<T>/TObjectList<T> (RTTI 'Count'/'Items' or 'ToArray'), tkSet (array of GetEnumName), tkEnumeration non-Boolean → TJSONString(GetEnumName), tkRecord; emit TJSONNull for nil objects and make the generator mark class/array properties without [Required]... simplest: generator emits 'type':['object','null'] for class-typed properties, or treats nil as absent and does not list class-typed properties in 'required' unless explicitly non-nullable. Add a debug-mode self-check (TMCPSchemaValidator from GAP-TL-07) that validates structuredContent against outputSchema and logs a warning on mismatch.

### GAP-TL-22 [major, effort M] No rate limiting or access-control hook for tool invocations

- Requirements: TL-22
- Current: TMCPToolsManager.CallTool executes any registered tool unconditionally (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:230-251); TMCPIdHTTPServer has no per-client throttling (src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas:172-208). Only list_files implements its own path-based access check (src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.ListFiles.pas:62-69).
- Required: "Servers **MUST**: ... Implement proper access controls; Rate limit tool invocations; Sanitize tool outputs".
- Compat impact: Opt-in hooks/settings; default behaviour unchanged except an optional rate limiter that is off by default. Library consumers get a single interception point for authorization.
- Suggested change: Add TMCPToolsManager.OnBeforeCallTool: reference to procedure(const Tool: IMCPTool; const Arguments: TJSONObject; const Ctx: IMCPRequestContext) (raise EMCPToolError to deny with isError, or EMCPProtocolError(-32602/-32603) for protocol-level refusal) and OnAfterCallTool for output sanitisation/logging. Add a simple thread-safe token bucket keyed by remote IP (TMonitor-protected TDictionary<string,TBucket>) in TMCPIdHTTPServer with settings RateLimitPerMinute (0 = off) returning HTTP 429 with a JSON-RPC -32603 body; for stdio, a global bucket. Document that per-tool authorization is the embedding application's responsibility.

### GAP-TL-11 [minor, effort S] tools/list order is TDictionary iteration order, not registration order

- Requirements: TL-03
- Current: TMCPRegistry stores factories in TDictionary<string,TMCPToolFactory> and GetToolNames returns FTools.Keys.ToArray (src/Core/MCPServer.Registration.pas:20, 91-96); TMCPToolsManager stores tools in TDictionary<string,IMCPTool> and iterates FTools.Values (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:24, 223). Order is hash-table order: stable within one process run in practice (Delphi's string hash is deterministic) but not the registration order and liable to change whenever an unrelated tool is added or the table grows.
- Required: "Servers **SHOULD** return tools in a deterministic order (i.e., the same ordering across requests when the underlying set of tools has not changed)."
- Compat impact: Only the order of the tools array changes. Additive API (TMCPRegistry keeps a TList<string> of names in registration order).
- Suggested change: Add class var FToolOrder: TList<string> to TMCPRegistry (append on first RegisterTool of a name; GetToolNames returns FToolOrder.ToArray) and FOrder: TList<IMCPTool> (or TList<string>) to TMCPToolsManager; BuildToolListResponse iterates the list. Optionally a SortToolsByName: Boolean property for alphabetical order.

### GAP-TL-12 [minor, effort S] tools/list ignores 'cursor'; invalid cursors return the full list instead of -32602

- Requirements: TL-07
- Current: ExecuteMethod dispatches 'tools/list' to ListTools without Params (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:71-72, 253-257); 'cursor' is never read; nextCursor never emitted (fine, pagination is optional) but a bogus cursor is silently accepted.
- Required: "Invalid cursors **SHOULD** result in an error with code -32602 (Invalid params)." Servers SHOULD provide stable cursors if they paginate.
- Compat impact: Only affects clients sending a cursor this server never issued. Additive property ListPageSize.
- Suggested change: Change ListTools to take Params; read params.cursor; add property ListPageSize: Integer (0 = single page). Cursor = base64 of the start index (or the last tool name for stability); if a cursor is present and cannot be decoded / is out of range raise EMCPProtocolError(-32602, 'Invalid cursor'); emit nextCursor when more tools remain; same ttlMs/cacheScope on all pages (TL-06).

### GAP-TL-13 [minor, effort S] Tool names are not validated; duplicate registrations silently replace or crash at startup

- Requirements: TL-12
- Current: TMCPRegistry.RegisterTool uses AddOrSetValue (last registration silently wins) with no format check (src/Core/MCPServer.Registration.pas:51-57); TMCPToolsManager.RegisterTool uses FTools.Add, so a duplicate reaching it raises EListError during TMCPToolsManager.Create (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:79-82). The registry key (RegisterTool name) and Tool.Name (FName set in the tool constructor) are not checked for consistency.
- Required: Names SHOULD be 1..128 chars of [A-Za-z0-9_.-], no spaces/commas, unique within the server.
- Compat impact: Default behaviour: log a warning only, so existing consumers with non-conforming names keep working; optional strict mode raises. Duplicate detection surfaces as a clear message instead of EListError.
- Suggested change: Add TMCPToolNaming.IsValid(const Name: string; out Reason: string): Boolean in MCPServer.Tool.Base (regex ^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,128}$); TMCPRegistry.RegisterTool logs TLogger.Warn for invalid names and for re-registration of an existing name (raise EMCPRegistrationError when class var StrictToolNames = True). TMCPToolsManager.RegisterBuiltInTools warns when Tool.Name <> registry name and registers under Tool.Name.

### GAP-TL-14 [minor, effort M] No way to declare annotations (readOnlyHint etc.), icons or _meta on a tool; empty description always emitted

- Requirements: TL-13, TL-14
- Current: IMCPTool exposes only Name/Title/Description/InputSchema/OutputSchema/Execute (src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Base.pas:11-25); CreateToolJSON emits name, title (only if <> name), description (even when ''), inputSchema, outputSchema (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:185-211). The only attributes are Optional/SchemaDescription/SchemaEnum (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Types.pas:14-35).
- Required: Tool MAY carry annotations {title, readOnlyHint, destructiveHint, idempotentHint, openWorldHint}, icons [{src (https:// or data:), mimeType?, sizes?, theme?}], _meta. Optional but expected by modern hosts for confirmation UX.
- Compat impact: Purely additive. Direct IMCPTool implementers are not forced to change: new capabilities go through an optional interface discovered with Supports(). All three base classes gain protected fields/setters.
- Suggested change: In MCPServer.Tool.Base: TMCPToolAnnotations = record (Title: string; ReadOnlyHint, DestructiveHint, IdempotentHint, OpenWorldHint: TMCPOptionalBool [unset/false/true]); TMCPIcon = record (Src, MimeType, Theme: string; Sizes: TArray<string>); IMCPToolMetadata = interface ['{new GUID}'] function GetAnnotations: TMCPToolAnnotations; function GetIcons: TArray<TMCPIcon>; function GetMeta: TJSONObject; end; implement in TMCPToolBase, TMCPToolBase<T>, TMCPToolBase<T,R> via protected FAnnotations/FIcons/FMeta plus helpers SetReadOnly/SetDestructive/AddIcon(Src, MimeType, Sizes, Theme). Optionally class attributes [ToolAnnotations(...)]/[ToolIcon(...)] read via RTTI. CreateToolJSON: if Supports(Tool, IMCPToolMetadata, Meta) emit 'annotations' (only set hints), 'icons', '_meta' (clone); omit 'description' when empty; reject icon src not starting with 'https://' or 'data:' with a warning.

### GAP-TL-15 [minor, effort M] No typed API for image/audio/resource_link/embedded-resource content, block annotations, per-block _meta or explicit structuredContent

- Requirements: TL-17, TL-18, TL-20, TL-27
- Current: A tool result is a string → single text block (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:148-167), a TJSONObject → structuredContent only (lines 168-181), or a raw TJSONArray passed through verbatim as 'content' (lines 141-147; undocumented in README, and TMCPToolBase<T>.ExecuteWithParams is typed string so only the non-generic TMCPToolBase can use it). No helper builds base64 image/audio blocks, resource_link ({uri,name,...}), embedded resources ({resource:{uri,text|blob}}), annotations {audience,priority,lastModified} or result-level _meta.
- Required: Content block shapes and optional annotations per TL-17; structuredContent may be any JSON value (modern) or an object (legacy); text fallback SHOULD accompany structured content.
- Compat impact: Additive: existing string/TJSONObject/TJSONArray return paths keep working. TMCPToolBase<T>.ExecuteWithParams (string) remains the documented override; a new virtual with a default implementation is added, so existing subclasses compile unchanged.
- Suggested change: New unit MCPServer.Tool.Result.pas: TMCPToolResult = class with AddText(const Text; Annotations: TMCPContentAnnotations = nil), AddImage(const Data: TBytes; const MimeType: string), AddAudio(...), AddResourceLink(const Uri, Name: string; const Description, MimeType, Title: string = ''), AddEmbeddedText(const Uri, Text, MimeType), AddEmbeddedBlob(const Uri; const Data: TBytes; const MimeType), SetStructuredContent(Value: TJSONValue {takes ownership}; AddTextFallback: Boolean = True), property IsError: Boolean, property Meta: TJSONObject, function ToJSON(Era: TMCPProtocolEra): TJSONObject (for legacy 2025-06-18/11-25 refuse non-object structuredContent → drop it, keep text; for 2025-03-26 also omit structuredContent). TMCPToolBase<T> gets 'function ExecuteWithParamsEx(const Params: T; const Ctx: IMCPRequestContext): TMCPToolResult; virtual;' whose default wraps ExecuteWithParams(Params) into a text block (preserving the 'Error:' heuristic); Execute returns TValue.From(TMCPToolResult); BuildToolCallResponse handles TMCPToolResult first. Base64 via System.NetEncoding.TNetEncoding.Base64.EncodeBytesToString (strip line breaks). Document all return forms in README.

### GAP-TL-16 [minor, effort S] isError is inferred from an 'Error:' string prefix or an 'error' key, not declared explicitly

- Requirements: TL-21
- Current: BuildToolCallResponse sets isError when the text StartsWith('Error:') or 'Error executing tool:' (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:151) and, for structured results, when an 'error' member is non-empty (lines 173-174). ExecuteTool turns every exception into 'Error executing tool: ' + Message (lines 122-127). Bundled tools depend on the prefix (src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Calculate.pas:68,74; src/Tools/MCPServer.Tool.ListFiles.pas:67,92). A legitimate output beginning with 'Error:' is mis-flagged; an error phrased differently is not flagged.
- Required: Errors originating from the tool SHOULD be reported with isError:true; the server needs an unambiguous way for tools to signal this.
- Compat impact: Heuristic is retained (documented as legacy convention) so existing consumer tools behave identically; explicit mechanisms are additive.
- Suggested change: Add EMCPToolError = class(Exception) (MCPServer.Errors.pas); ExecuteTool catches EMCPToolError → isError:true with E.Message (no 'Error executing tool:' prefix), EArgumentException/other Exception → isError:true with 'Error executing tool: ' prefix as today, EMCPProtocolError → re-raise (protocol error). TMCPToolResult.IsError (GAP-TL-15) is the explicit result-level flag. Keep the string heuristic behind TMCPToolsManager.LegacyErrorPrefixDetection: Boolean (default True).

### GAP-TL-17 [minor, effort S] Non-string/non-JSON TValue results yield an empty result object without 'content'

- Requirements: TL-17
- Current: BuildToolCallResponse has branches only for TJSONArray, string and TJSONObject (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:141-181); a custom TMCPToolBase.Execute returning TValue.Empty, an Integer, a Boolean or another object produces {} — invalid CallToolResult (content required).
- Required: CallToolResult.content is required in every revision.
- Compat impact: Only affects consumers' hand-written tools returning unusual TValues; they now get a valid text result.
- Suggested change: Add a final else branch: if ResultValue.IsEmpty then content := [] else content := [{type:'text', text: ResultValue.ToString}]; for TValue holding an arbitrary TObject, serialize via TMCPSerializer to structuredContent + text.

### GAP-TL-21 [minor, effort S] tools capability is declared with non-standard keys and without listChanged

- Requirements: TL-01, TL-26
- Current: TMCPCoreManager.Initialize emits capabilities.tools = {supportsProgress:false, supportsCancellation:false} (src/Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas:106-114) mirroring TMCPToolsCapability (src/Protocol/MCPServer.Types.pas:63-71), which is not actually used to build the JSON. Neither key exists in any revision's ServerCapabilities.tools ({listChanged?: boolean}); schema.json leaves the object open so it is tolerated but meaningless.
- Required: "Servers that support tools **MUST** declare the `tools` capability" as {listChanged?: boolean} — legacy in initialize, modern in server/discover (lifecycle area owns the discover result).
- Compat impact: Legacy clients ignore the unknown keys today and will not miss them. TMCPToolsCapability keeps SupportsProgress/SupportsCancellation (deprecated, unused) and gains ListChanged for source compatibility.
- Suggested change: Emit tools: {"listChanged": false} (or {}) in both the initialize result and the server/discover result; add ListChanged: Boolean to TMCPToolsCapability and have both code paths build the JSON from it. Set listChanged:true only once a dynamic registry + subscriptions/listen exist.

### GAP-TL-23 [minor, effort S] No era-specific shaping of tool definitions/results for legacy sessions

- Requirements: TL-09, TL-18, TL-17
- Current: CreateToolJSON and BuildToolCallResponse are version-agnostic (src/Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas:130-211); nothing prevents a hand-written TMCPToolBase (BuildSchema/Execute) from advertising an array/primitive outputSchema or returning non-object structuredContent to a 2025-06-18/2025-11-25 client, or resource_link blocks / title / outputSchema / icons to a 2025-03-26 client.
- Required: Legacy 2025-06-18/2025-11-25: outputSchema root MUST be type:object and structuredContent MUST be an object; 2025-03-26 has no structuredContent/outputSchema/title/icons and no resource_link content type. Modern: any JSON value/schema.
- Compat impact: Only matters once consumers use the new modern-only flexibility; the generic bases always produce object schemas. Requires the negotiated version to be available to the manager (thread-local request context or IMCPRequestContext).
