- **TSK-04** (MUST, both): Cross-era: under 2025-11-25 "Servers MUST NOT treat this capability as enabling tasks under that protocol version"; "Servers MUST treat clients declaring only the legacy [tasks.*] capability as non-declaring with respect to this extension"; "tasks/result is removed; clients calling it MUST receive -32601"; "The task parameter on CallToolRequest is removed; servers MUST ignore it"; servers "MUST NOT continue to advertise the legacy capabilities under any protocol version that includes this extension." Reserved: tasks/ prefix, notifications/tasks/ prefix, resultType "task".  
  _Source: seps/2663-tasks-extension.md (Backward Compatibility table; Reservations)_

## Authorization (OAuth 2.1 resource-server role), transport security best practices (Origin/DNS rebinding, localhost binding, token passthrough, state/requestState integrity, x-mcp-header injection, logging hygiene), SDK/client ecosystem status for 2026-07-28, and conformance/acceptance tooling (conformance CLI, MCP Inspector)

Requirements extracted: 30. Gaps: 19.


### GAP-SEC-07 [blocker, effort M] Mirrored HTTP headers (MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name, Mcp-Param-*) are neither validated against the body nor rejected on invalid characters; no x-mcp-header support/validation in the schema generator (overlaps transport area, listed for the injection aspect)

- Requirements: SEC-09, SEC-10, SEC-11
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas reads no `Mcp-Method`/`Mcp-Name`/`MCP-Protocol-Version`/`Mcp-Param-*` header (only lines 224, 277, 289, 333, 337 read headers); JsonRpcProcessor dispatches purely on the body `method` (168-171). Protocol/MCPServer.Schema.Generator.pas has no `x-mcp-header` emission and no constraint validation; ToolsManager.CreateToolJSON (185-211) serialises whatever schema the tool returns.
- Required: SEC-09: servers that process the body MUST reject header/body mismatches and invalid `Mcp-Param-*` characters with 400 + `-32020`, MUST decode `=?base64?...?=` sentinels first, MUST NOT expect a header for null/absent values, MUST compare header names case-insensitively; SEC-10: invalid `x-mcp-header` annotations make the tool definition invalid (clients drop the tool), so the server must validate at registration.
- Compat impact: Modern-era only: validation is applied solely when the request carries modern `_meta`/`MCP-Protocol-Version: 2026-07-28`; legacy requests (no header or 2025-xx versions) are not subject to header validation, so legacy clients are unaffected. Library consumers: new optional `[HeaderParam('Region')]` attribute; tools without it unchanged; registration now raises a clear exception for invalid annotations (previously silently accepted).
- Suggested change: New src/Server/MCPServer.HeaderValidation.pas: `TMCPMirroredHeaders.Validate(RequestInfo.RawHeaders, JSONRequest, ToolSchemaLookup): TMCPHeaderValidationResult` — case-insensitive header lookup (Indy `RawHeaders.Values` is already case-insensitive), decode sentinel (reject bad base64 with 400/-32020), compare `Mcp-Method` = body.method (case-sensitive), `Mcp-Name` = params.name|params.uri, each declared `Mcp-Param-<name>` = arguments at the property path (numeric compare for integers, `true`/`false` for booleans), reject `Mcp-Param-*` values with chars outside 0x20-0x7E/0x09; return HTTP 400 + `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":<id>,"error":{"code":-32020,"message":"Header mismatch: ..."}}`. Schema generator: `HeaderParamAttribute` -> `"x-mcp-header": "<name>"`; validate token syntax (RFC 9110 tchar), no CR/LF, case-insensitive uniqueness, primitive type (string/integer/boolean; reject Double), top-level `properties` chain only; raise EMCPSchema at TMCPRegistry.RegisterTool time. README warning: never annotate secrets/PII (SEC-10).

### GAP-AUTH-01 [major, effort M] No authorization hook at all: bearer tokens are never read or validated; no extension point for library consumers

- Requirements: AUTH-01, AUTH-06, AUTH-07, AUTH-08, AUTH-12, SEC-03, SEC-07, SEC-05
- Current: /home/user/Delphi-MCP-Server/src/Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas HandleHTTPRequest (lines 172-208) goes CORS check -> endpoint match -> OPTIONS/GET/POST dispatch; the `Authorization` header is never read anywhere in src (only `Origin`, `Accept`, `Mcp-Session-Id` are read: lines 224, 277, 289, 333, 337). MCPServer.dpr (74-112) wires only Settings/ManagerRegistry/CoreManager. README.md lines 350-353 advertise `claude mcp add ... --header "Authorization: Bearer your-token"` "(if configured)" but nothing can be configured. IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod (Protocol/MCPServer.Types.pas:43) and IMCPTool.Execute (Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Base.pas:18) receive no principal/context.
- Required: AUTH-01/SEC-03: HTTP servers SHOULD implement proper authentication; once implemented, tokens MUST be validated per OAuth 2.1 §5.2 with audience binding, invalid/expired -> 401, insufficient scope -> 403, malformed -> 400 (AUTH-06/08/12). SEC-07: locally-run HTTP servers SHOULD require an authorization token.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients: none (hook is opt-in; default remains an open server). Library consumers: purely additive — new `MCPServer.Authorization` unit and a new `Authorizer` property on TMCPIdHTTPServer defaulting to nil; existing TMCPToolBase<T>, TMCPResourceBase<T>, IMCPCapabilityManager and TMCPRegistry code compiles unchanged. TMCPStdioTransport MUST NOT get the hook (AUTH-02).
- Suggested change: New unit src/Core/MCPServer.Authorization.pas: `TMCPAuthDecision = (adAllow, adUnauthorized, adForbidden, adBadRequest)`; `TMCPPrincipal` (Subject, Scopes: TArray<string>, Claims: TJSONObject, Raw token never stored); `TMCPAuthChallenge` record (Scheme='Bearer', ResourceMetadataUrl, Scope, Error, ErrorDescription); `IMCPAuthorizer = interface ['{...}'] function Authorize(const ABearerToken: string; const AHttpMethod, APath: string; out APrincipal: TMCPPrincipal; out AChallenge: TMCPAuthChallenge): TMCPAuthDecision; end;`. Ship `TMCPStaticBearerAuthorizer` (list of tokens from settings, constant-time comparison loop, no RTL helper exists) and an abstract `TMCPOAuthResourceServerAuthorizer` with `ExpectedAudience: string` (required; raise EMCPConfiguration if empty), `ResourceMetadataUrl`, `RequiredScopes`, and a virtual `ValidateToken(const AToken: string; out AClaims: TJSONObject): Boolean` for consumers (introspection RFC 7662 via System.Net.HttpClient can be provided in-core; JWT signature verification left to a sample unit because the RTL has no JOSE). In TMCPIdHTTPServer add `property Authorizer: IMCPAuthorizer` and call it in HandleHTTPRequest after Origin validation and endpoint match, for GET/POST/DELETE but NOT for OPTIONS (preflight must stay unauthenticated) and NOT for the `.well-known` metadata path (GAP-AUTH-03). Read the token only from `RequestInfo.RawHeaders.Values['Authorization']` with case-insensitive `Bearer ` prefix; never from `RequestInfo.Params`. Re-run the authorizer on every request (no caching by session id).

### GAP-AUTH-02 [major, effort S] No 401/403 challenge with WWW-Authenticate (resource_metadata, scope, error=insufficient_scope)

- Requirements: AUTH-04, AUTH-05, AUTH-08, AUTH-09, AUTH-11
- Current: No code path sets `WWW-Authenticate`; the only non-2xx responses are 404 (IdHTTPServer.pas 189-190), 405 (202-203), 403 for Origin (247-248) and JSON-RPC errors carried in HTTP 200 (509, 553). HTTP status constants at lines 82-89 lack 400 and 401.
- Required: AUTH-04 (legacy MUST / modern one-of MUST): 401 responses carry `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="<url>"`; AUTH-05: SHOULD add `scope="..."`; AUTH-09: 403 with `error="insufficient_scope", scope="...", resource_metadata="..."`; AUTH-11: never advertise `offline_access`.
- Compat impact: Only affects deployments that enable an Authorizer. Legacy (2025-06-18/2025-11-25) clients such as the Inspector parse the same header, so one implementation serves both eras. No API change for library consumers.
- Suggested change: Add `HTTP_BAD_REQUEST = 400`, `HTTP_UNAUTHORIZED = 401` constants. Add `procedure TMCPIdHTTPServer.WriteAuthChallenge(ResponseInfo: TIdHTTPResponseInfo; const AChallenge: TMCPAuthChallenge; AStatus: Integer)` that builds `Bearer resource_metadata="..."[, scope="..."][, error="..."][, error_description="..."]` (quote/escape per RFC 9110; reject CR/LF) and assigns `ResponseInfo.WWWAuthenticate.Text` (Indy emits it as the WWW-Authenticate header) or `CustomHeaders.Values['WWW-Authenticate']`; set `ResponseNo`, `ContentType := 'application/json'` with an optional id-less JSON-RPC error body. Filter `offline_access` out of any scope list at configuration time and log a warning. Expose `WWW-Authenticate` via `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` (see GAP-SEC-02).

### GAP-AUTH-03 [major, effort S] No RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata document at /.well-known/oauth-protected-resource[/<endpoint>]

- Requirements: AUTH-03, AUTH-04, AUTH-14
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas lines 184-192: any path other than `FSettings.Endpoint` returns 404, so both `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` and `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/mcp` are 404. Settings (Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas 83-95, 143-155) have no authorization-server or resource-URI fields.
- Required: AUTH-03: when auth is supported the server MUST serve a PRM document whose `authorization_servers` has at least one entry; AUTH-04: modern clients fall back to probing the path-form then the root well-known URI when no header is present; the `resource` value must equal the canonical server URI (RFC 8707 §2 form, no trailing slash) that the AS binds into the token audience.
- Compat impact: Endpoint only exists when auth is enabled; unauthenticated GET, served with CORS headers. No impact on legacy clients or library consumers; new optional settings only.
- Suggested change: In HandleHTTPRequest, before the endpoint-404 check: `if Assigned(FAuthorizer) and (RequestInfo.CommandType = hcGET) and ((RequestPath = WELL_KNOWN_PRM) or (RequestPath = WELL_KNOWN_PRM + FSettings.Endpoint)) then HandleProtectedResourceMetadata(...)`. Build with TJSONObject: `resource` (Settings.ResourceUri, default `Protocol://Host[:Port]Endpoint` lower-cased scheme/host, no trailing slash), `authorization_servers` (TJSONArray from new `[Auth] AuthorizationServers=` comma list; raise at Start if empty when auth enabled; require https unless loopback), `scopes_supported` (optional, from `[Auth] ScopesSupported=`), `bearer_methods_supported: ["header"]`, `resource_name` (Settings.ServerName). `Content-Type: application/json`, `Cache-Control: max-age=3600`. Add `ResourceMetadataUrl` helper used by GAP-AUTH-02 so the header and the document agree.

### GAP-AUTH-04 [major, effort S] No audience-binding contract and no token-passthrough guard for consumers who forward calls upstream

- Requirements: AUTH-06, AUTH-12, SEC-04
- Current: Nothing validates `aud`/resource binding (no auth code exists). src/Http/MCPServer.Http.Executor.pas provides a generic HTTP client (Get/Post with `TArray<TNetHeader>`) that a custom tool could use to forward the inbound `Authorization` header to an upstream API; nothing prevents or documents against it. README has no security guidance section.
- Required: AUTH-06/AUTH-12: servers MUST validate the token was issued for them (audience), MUST reject others, MUST NOT pass the inbound token to upstream APIs; SEC-04: MUST NOT accept tokens not explicitly issued for the server.
- Compat impact: Design-level: makes audience validation mandatory in the shipped OAuth authorizer base class (consumers overriding `ValidateToken` cannot skip it). No change to tool/resource APIs; documentation for consumers.
- Suggested change: In `TMCPOAuthResourceServerAuthorizer.Authorize`: after `ValidateToken` returns claims, require `aud` (string or array) to contain `ExpectedAudience` (compare after lower-casing scheme/host, no trailing-slash normalisation beyond that) and `exp` > now, else adUnauthorized with `error="invalid_token"`. Never expose the raw token on `TMCPPrincipal` (store only claims). Add a README 'Security' section: 'the inbound bearer token is bound to this server; tools calling upstream APIs must obtain their own credentials (client_credentials or a stored user grant) and must never forward `Authorization`'. Do not ship any 'passthrough' authorizer.

### GAP-SEC-01 [major, effort S] Origin validation is coupled to CORS settings and is skipped entirely when CORS is disabled or AllowedOrigins='*'; exact-match list rejects legitimate loopback origins with ports

- Requirements: SEC-01, SEC-13
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas VerifyAndSetCORSHeaders 210-263: line 221-222 `if not Assigned(FSettings) or not FSettings.CorsEnabled then Exit;` — with `[CORS] Enabled=0` no Origin check happens; line 227 `if (FSettings.CorsAllowedOrigins <> '*') and (Origin <> '')` — `*` accepts every Origin and line 257 emits `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`; line 236 `SameText(Trim(CurrentOrigin), Origin)` is exact-string only, so with the code default `http://localhost,http://127.0.0.1,https://localhost,https://127.0.0.1` (Settings.pas:91) an origin such as `http://localhost:6274` or `http://[::1]:5173` is rejected with 403 (settings.ini.example adds only `:3000`). No `Vary: Origin`. 403 body is empty text (line 248).
- Required: SEC-01: Origin MUST be validated on all incoming connections regardless of CORS configuration; invalid Origin MUST get 403 (body MAY be an id-less JSON-RPC error). SEC-13 optional body.
- Compat impact: Default config already returns 403 for foreign origins (conformant); the gap is that two documented settings (`Enabled=0`, `AllowedOrigins=*`) silently violate a MUST. Fix must keep `*` meaning 'allow any' but log a warning, and must keep the ini keys. Library consumers: behaviour change only for CorsEnabled=False deployments that receive browser traffic — document in CHANGELOG.
- Suggested change: Split into `function ValidateOrigin(RequestInfo, ResponseInfo): Boolean` (always runs; new `[Security] AllowedOrigins=` defaulting to the CORS list for compatibility; match scheme+host with optional `:*` port wildcard; built-in loopback set `http(s)://localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` any port; absent Origin -> allow; `null` -> deny; mismatch -> 403 with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32600,"message":"Origin not allowed"}}` and `Content-Type: application/json`) and `procedure ApplyCorsHeaders` (reflect the validated Origin instead of `*` when an Authorization header is present, add `Vary: Origin`). Keep TLogger.Info on block. Acceptance: conformance scenario `dns-rebinding-protection` under both `--requirements 2025-11-25` and `--requirements 2026-07-28`.

### GAP-SEC-02 [major, effort S] CORS allow/expose lists only cover legacy headers; browser-based modern clients cannot preflight Mcp-Method/Mcp-Name/Mcp-Param-*/Authorization or read WWW-Authenticate

- Requirements: SEC-01, SEC-09, AUTH-04
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas 258-261: `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: POST, GET, OPTIONS`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Accept, Content-Type, Last-Event-ID, Mcp-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Session-Id`, `Access-Control-Expose-Headers: Mcp-Session-Id`. HandleOptionsRequest (265-269) ignores `Access-Control-Request-Headers`.
- Required: Modern requests MUST carry `MCP-Protocol-Version`, `Mcp-Method`, `Mcp-Name` and optionally dynamic `Mcp-Param-*` headers (SEC-09); authenticated requests carry `Authorization`; 401 challenges are read from `WWW-Authenticate` (AUTH-04), which is not a CORS-safelisted response header.
- Compat impact: Purely additive header lists; legacy names retained. No library API change. (The MCP Inspector web UI proxies through its Node backend, so it is not affected; pure-browser clients are.)
- Suggested change: In HandleOptionsRequest reflect `RequestInfo.RawHeaders.Values['Access-Control-Request-Headers']` into `Access-Control-Allow-Headers` (covers dynamic `Mcp-Param-*`), otherwise use a static list `Accept, Content-Type, Authorization, Last-Event-ID, MCP-Protocol-Version, Mcp-Session-Id, Mcp-Method, Mcp-Name`. Set `Access-Control-Expose-Headers: Mcp-Session-Id, WWW-Authenticate`. Keep `DELETE` out unless legacy session termination is implemented (transport area). Add `Vary: Origin, Access-Control-Request-Headers`.

### GAP-SEC-03 [major, effort S] HTTP server binds to all interfaces; Settings.Host is only used for logging

- Requirements: SEC-02, SEC-07
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas Start (136-160) sets `FHTTPServer.DefaultPort := FPort` (155) and `Active := True` without adding a `Bindings` entry, so Indy creates a default binding on 0.0.0.0 (and :: when IPv6 is default). `FSettings.Host` ('localhost' by default, Settings.pas:86) is used only in the log line (159) and in the GET info JSON (309-310). README line 104 says the server 'will listen on http://localhost:3000/mcp', which is misleading.
- Required: SEC-02: when running locally servers SHOULD bind only to 127.0.0.1; SEC-07: restrict access for locally-run HTTP servers.
- Compat impact: Behaviour change: deployments that reach the server remotely while `Host=localhost` would stop working. Mitigate with explicit `[Server] BindAddress=` (default = Host; `0.0.0.0`/`*` = all interfaces with a logged warning) and a CHANGELOG note. Library consumers using TMCPIdHTTPServer programmatically get a new `BindAddress` property (default '127.0.0.1').
- Suggested change: Add `property BindAddress: string` to TMCPIdHTTPServer and `[Server] BindAddress` to TMCPSettings. In Start: `FHTTPServer.Bindings.Clear; with FHTTPServer.Bindings.Add do begin IP := ResolvedIP; Port := FPort; IPVersion := Id_IPv4; end;` mapping 'localhost' -> '127.0.0.1' and adding a second binding `::1` (Id_IPv6) when available; `0.0.0.0`/`*` -> `IP := ''` (all) plus TLogger.Warning('binding to all interfaces; enable [Auth]'). Update README Transport Modes and settings.ini.example.

### GAP-SEC-04 [major, effort S] Full request/response bodies and session ids are logged at Info level and re-exposed through the logs://recent resource; once Authorization, requestState and elicitation inputResponses exist they will leak

- Requirements: AUTH-13, SEC-08, SEC-12
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas 331 `TLogger.Info('Request: ' + RequestBody)`, 335 `'Session ID from header: ' + SessionID`, 555 `'Response: ' + ResponseBody`; StdioTransport.pas 62 `'Received: ' + InputLine`, 70 `'Sent: ' + Response`; MCPServer.dpr 167 sets MinLogLevel := Info; Resources/MCPServer.Resource.Logs.pas serves recent log entries to any client (README 451 `logs://recent`). Headers are not logged today, but bodies will contain `_meta` (client identity), `requestState`, `inputResponses` (user answers / PII) and, for consumers who put tokens in tool args, secrets.
- Required: AUTH-13: servers MUST implement secure token handling; tokens 'cached or logged on the server' are an explicit threat. SEC-08 requestState is attacker-facing state that should not be disclosed. SEC-12: nothing but MCP messages on stdout (already true; keep it so).
- Compat impact: Logging verbosity change only; `TLogger` API unchanged (add a `Redact` hook). Consumers who registered `logs://recent` keep it, but the default registration should require an authenticated principal or be removed from the default tool set.
- Suggested change: Log bodies only at TLogLevel.Debug through a new `TLogger.RedactJson(const Body: string)` that blanks `params._meta`, `params.requestState`, `params.inputResponses`, any key matching (?i)token|secret|password|authorization; never log header values (add `TLogger.Warning` guard in code review checklist). Keep TLogger.UseStdErr for stdio. Make TServerStatusResource/TLogsResource registration opt-in (`[Server] ExposeLogsResource=0`) or check `TMCPRequestContext.Current.Principal` before returning entries.

### GAP-SEC-06 [major, effort M] No integrity-protected requestState codec for the MRTR pattern (required once InputRequiredResult is implemented)

- Requirements: SEC-08, SEC-05, SEC-11
- Current: MRTR/InputRequiredResult does not exist yet (another area), but no HMAC/AEAD helper, key management or replay checks exist anywhere in src; System.Hash (THashSHA2.GetHMAC) is available in the RTL and unused.
- Required: SEC-08: requestState MUST be treated as attacker-controlled; MUST be integrity-protected when it influences authorization/resource access/business logic; MUST be rejected on verification failure; SHOULD embed principal, TTL and originating-request identifier; single-use MUST be enforced server-side where required. Scored by required 2026-07-28 server scenario `input-required-result-tampered-state` (and `input-required-result-request-state`).
- Compat impact: Modern era only; new unit, no change to existing APIs. Consumers implementing multi-round tools get `TMCPRequestState.Pack/Unpack` instead of rolling their own.
- Suggested change: New unit src/Core/MCPServer.RequestState.pas: `TMCPRequestStateCodec` with `Key: TBytes` (from `[Security] StateKey=` base64 or generated per process; document that generated keys do not survive restarts/load-balancing), `function Pack(const APayload: TJSONObject; const AContext: TMCPRequestContext; ATtlSeconds: Integer): string` producing base64url(`json`) + '.' + base64url(HMAC-SHA256) where json includes `sub` (principal subject or ''), `exp` (Unix time), `m` (method), `h` (SHA-256 of canonical tool name + arguments), `d` (opaque data); `function Unpack(const AState: string; const AContext: TMCPRequestContext; out APayload: TJSONObject): Boolean` with constant-time MAC compare, expiry, principal and request-digest checks; on failure the tools manager returns JSON-RPC error -32602 (Invalid params), never -32000..-32019 (SEC-11). Provide `IMCPRequestStateStore` hook for single-use enforcement.

### GAP-SEC-09 [major, effort S] No channel from protocol/manager code to an HTTP status + headers; every failure is HTTP 200 with -32603, which also mislabels errors under the new error-code policy

- Requirements: AUTH-08, SEC-01, SEC-09, SEC-11
- Current: JsonRpcProcessor.pas 204-218 maps any exception to -32603 unless the message contains 'not found' (-32601) or parsing failed (-32700); IdHTTPServer always answers 200 for JSON (553) and SSE (509). HTTP status constants (82-89) lack 400/401.
- Required: AUTH-08 needs 401/403/400 at HTTP level; SEC-09 needs 400 + -32020; SEC-01 needs 403; SEC-11 forbids inventing codes in -32000..-32019 and requires defined codes with their meanings.
- Compat impact: Keep `ProcessRequest(const RequestBody, SessionID): string` for library consumers who call the processor directly (TMCPStdioTransport does); add a new overload. Custom managers can raise the new exception types but are not required to.
- Suggested change: Types.pas: `EMCPJsonRpcError = class(Exception) Code: Integer; Data: TJSONValue` and `EMCPHttpError = class(EMCPJsonRpcError) StatusCode: Integer; Headers: TStrings`. JsonRpcProcessor: new `function ProcessRequestEx(const RequestBody: string; const Context: TMCPRequestContext): TMCPProcessResult` (record: HttpStatus, ContentType, Headers, Body); map EMCPJsonRpcError to its code, EMCPHttpError to its status; keep the string overload delegating to it. IdHTTPServer copies HttpStatus/Headers to ResponseInfo. Stdio ignores HttpStatus. Use only spec-defined codes (-32020/-32021/-32022, -32600..-32603, -32700).

### GAP-ECO-01 [major, effort M] No automated tests, no CI, and no conformance/Inspector acceptance script; build is Windows-only with a hard-coded Studio path

- Requirements: SEC-01, SEC-08, SEC-09, AUTH-04, AUTH-08
- Current: Repository root contains only LICENSE, README.md, build.bat, generate-ssl-cert.bat, settings.ini.example, src/ (no tests/ or .github/; `find . -iname '*test*'` returns nothing). build.bat line 17 pins `DELPHI_PATH=C:\Program Files (x86)\Embarcadero\Studio\37.0`; Linux64 is built from Windows via MSBuild/PAServer (78-94). README 560-563 says 'Test with npx @modelcontextprotocol/inspector or Claude Code'.
- Required: Not a spec MUST, but SEP-2484/SEP-1730 make the conformance suite the yardstick ('Tier 1 requires 100% pass rate'; requirement sets are frozen per revision), and the security MUSTs above (Origin 403, tampered requestState, header mismatch) are directly exercised by scored scenarios `dns-rebinding-protection`, `input-required-result-tampered-state`, `input-required-result-request-state`.
- Compat impact: No runtime impact. Adds a `tests/` DUnitX project, a `conformance-baseline.yml`, and PowerShell/bash acceptance scripts; consumers can ignore them. CI on GitHub-hosted runners needs a licensed Delphi compiler (not available), so plan for a self-hosted Windows runner or a documented manual release checklist.
- Suggested change: (1) tests/MCPServer.Tests.dproj (DUnitX) covering OriginValidator, WWW-Authenticate builder, PRM document, static bearer authorizer (constant-time), header validator (SEP-2243 edge tables), RequestState codec (tamper/expiry/principal), schema x-mcp-header constraints. (2) scripts/run-conformance.ps1: start `Win64\Release\MCPServer.exe`, wait for `http://127.0.0.1:3000/mcp`, then `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance server --url http://localhost:3000/mcp --requirements 2026-07-28 --expected-failures ./conformance-baseline.yml` and again with `--requirements 2025-11-25` (both wires against the same endpoint — the README states a non-listed implementation's single endpoint 'must serve every claimed revision at its own wire'); optionally `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance tier-check --repo GDKsoftware/delphi-mcp-server --conformance-server-url http://localhost:3000/mcp --requirements 2025-11-25,2026-07-28`. (3) Inspector smoke: `npx @modelcontextprotocol/inspector --cli --config ./ci-servers.json --server delphi-modern --method tools/list --format json` with `"protocolEra": "modern"` and a second entry with `"protocolEra": "legacy"`; for auth, `--header "Authorization: Bearer <token>"` and assert exit code 3 (`auth_required`) without it. (4) Pin tool versions in package.json devDependencies (`@modelcontextprotocol/conformance`, `@modelcontextprotocol/inspector`, Node >= 22.19.0). (5) build.bat: read DELPHI_PATH from env/rsvars, add `build.sh` stub calling MSBuild via PAServer docs.

### GAP-AUTH-05 [minor, effort S] settings.ini has no [Auth] section; auth cannot be turned on without code

- Requirements: AUTH-01, AUTH-03, AUTH-05, SEC-07
- Current: Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas: only [Server], [CORS], [SSL] keys (LoadDefaults 83-95, CreateDefaultSettingsFile 106-132, LoadFromFile 134-171, SaveToFile 173-195). settings.ini.example mirrors this.
- Required: Operational prerequisite for AUTH-03/04/05 and SEC-07 (require an authorization token for locally-run HTTP servers).
- Compat impact: Additive properties with safe defaults (`Enabled=0`); existing ini files keep working; consumers that construct TMCPSettings programmatically are unaffected.
- Suggested change: Add to TMCPSettings: `AuthEnabled: Boolean` (default False), `AuthMode: string` ('bearer'|'oauth'), `AuthBearerTokens: string` (comma list; document that a file/env var is preferable), `AuthResourceUri`, `AuthAuthorizationServers`, `AuthScopesSupported`, `AuthRequiredScopes`, `AuthResourceMetadataUrl` (optional override). Read/write in the three ini methods under `[Auth]`. In MCPServer.dpr RunHTTPServer, build `TMCPStaticBearerAuthorizer` or `TMCPOAuthResourceServerAuthorizer` from settings and assign `Server.Authorizer`. Update settings.ini.example and README Configuration.

### GAP-AUTH-06 [minor, effort M] Tools, resources and managers cannot see the authenticated principal or request context (needed for per-user state handles, requestState binding, per-operation scope checks)

- Requirements: SEC-05, SEC-08, AUTH-09, AUTH-12
- Current: IMCPTool.Execute(const Arguments: TJSONObject) (Tools/MCPServer.Tool.Base.pas:18) and TMCPToolBase<T>.ExecuteWithParams(const Params: T) (line 49) get only arguments; IMCPCapabilityManager.ExecuteMethod(const Method; const Params) (Protocol/MCPServer.Types.pas:43) gets only params; TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest(const RequestBody, SessionID) (Protocol/MCPServer.JsonRpcProcessor.pas:146) has no context object. Indy handles each connection on its own thread (TIdContext), stdio is single-threaded.
- Required: SEC-05: state handles SHOULD be bound server-side to the authenticated user derived from the verified token; SEC-08: requestState SHOULD embed the authenticated principal; AUTH-09: per-operation scope decisions need the token's scopes at tool level.
- Compat impact: Zero source change for existing custom tools/resources/managers if the context is ambient (thread-local) and the new interface is opt-in. Signatures of IMCPTool/IMCPCapabilityManager/ProcessRequest stay intact.
- Suggested change: Add `TMCPRequestContext` (Protocol/MCPServer.Types.pas or new MCPServer.RequestContext.pas) with Principal: TMCPPrincipal, Era: (mcpEraLegacy, mcpEraModern), ProtocolVersion, TransportKind, Headers: TStrings (read-only copy), plus `class threadvar`/`threadvar FCurrent` and `class property Current`. Set it in TMCPIdHTTPServer.HandleHTTPRequest (try/finally around dispatch) and TMCPStdioTransport.Run (principal = environment identity or nil). Add optional `IMCPContextAwareTool = interface function ExecuteWithContext(const Arguments: TJSONObject; const Context: TMCPRequestContext): TValue; end;` and in TMCPToolsManager.ExecuteTool prefer it via `Supports(Tool, IMCPContextAwareTool, CtxTool)`. Document `TMCPRequestContext.Current.Principal.Subject` as the key for state handles.

### GAP-AUTH-07 [minor, effort M] No per-operation scope enforcement, no scope hierarchy, and no way for manager/tool code to produce an HTTP 401/403/400 (JSON-RPC layer swallows everything into HTTP 200)

- Requirements: AUTH-08, AUTH-09, AUTH-10
- Current: TMCPJsonRpcProcessor.ProcessRequest catches all exceptions (JsonRpcProcessor.pas 204-218) and maps them to -32603/-32601/-32700 inside an HTTP 200 (IdHTTPServer.pas 509, 553); TMCPToolsManager.ExecuteTool (ToolsManager.pas 120-128) converts tool exceptions to an `isError` text result. There is no scope concept in TMCPToolBase or the schema attributes (Types.pas 14-35).
- Required: AUTH-08: 403 for insufficient permissions; AUTH-09: SHOULD emit `error="insufficient_scope"` with all required scopes in one challenge; AUTH-10: MUST honour scope hierarchies when deciding sufficiency.
- Compat impact: Additive: new attribute and exception types; ProcessRequest keeps its string-returning signature (add an overload returning a record). Existing tools without `[RequiresScope]` behave as before. Enables the Inspector's mid-session step-up (`403 insufficient_scope`) flow.
- Suggested change: Types.pas: `RequiresScopeAttribute = class(TCustomAttribute) constructor Create(const AScopes: string)`; `EMCPHttpError = class(Exception) StatusCode: Integer; Challenge: TMCPAuthChallenge; JsonRpcCode: Integer` and `EMCPInsufficientScope = class(EMCPHttpError)`. TMCPToolsManager.CallTool: read `[RequiresScope]` on the tool class via TRttiContext and call `IMCPAuthorizer.HasScopes(Principal, Required)` where `TMCPScopeHierarchy` (dictionary broader -> narrower, configured from `[Auth] ScopeHierarchy=files:admin>files:write>files:read`) resolves implication; on failure raise EMCPInsufficientScope with the full required set. JsonRpcProcessor: let EMCPHttpError propagate (re-raise before the catch-all) or return `TMCPProcessResult` (HttpStatus, Body, Headers) from a new overload; IdHTTPServer maps it to 403 + WWW-Authenticate via GAP-AUTH-02.

### GAP-SEC-05 [minor, effort S] Legacy session id is generated with CreateGUID (RNG quality unverified on Linux), placed inside the initialize result body as non-standard `sessionId`, and any client-supplied Mcp-Session-Id is echoed back unvalidated

- Requirements: SEC-06
- Current: Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas 97 `FSessionID := TGuid.NewGuid.ToString` and 126 `ResultJSON.AddPair('sessionId', FSessionID)` (not a spec field); IdHTTPServer.pas 533-543 scrapes `result.sessionId` from the body to set the `Mcp-Session-Id` header; 348-349, 485-486, 548-549 echo whatever `Mcp-Session-Id` the client sent without charset validation; no server-side session table exists (FSessionID overwritten per initialize, never checked).
- Required: SEC-06 (legacy era): session IDs MUST be secure/non-deterministic (SHOULD use a CSPRNG), MUST only contain 0x21-0x7E, MUST NOT be used for authentication (already true), SHOULD be bound to user info if state is stored.
- Compat impact: Legacy clients ignore the extra `sessionId` body field today; removing it is safe (Mcp-Session-Id header is the contract). Consumers reading TMCPCoreManager.SessionID keep working. Overlaps with the lifecycle/transport work that decides whether to keep sessions at all.
- Suggested change: Generate the id with an explicit CSPRNG helper (`MCPServer.Crypto.pas`: BCryptGenRandom on Windows, /dev/urandom on POSIX, or OpenSSL RAND_bytes when TaurusTLS is loaded) encoded base64url (visible ASCII, no braces); stop emitting `sessionId` in the JSON result and have the transport set the header directly from the core manager; validate inbound `Mcp-Session-Id` against `[\x21-\x7E]+` and respond 400 otherwise; never key any authorization decision on it. If per-session state is ever stored, key it as `<principal.subject>:<sessionId>`.

### GAP-SEC-08 [minor, effort S] Non-TaurusTLS TLS path enables TLS 1.0/1.1; TLS implementation choice is a hard-coded $DEFINE inside the library unit

- Requirements: AUTH-14
- Current: IdHTTPServer.pas 395-396: `SSLOptions.Method := sslvTLSv1_2; SSLOptions.SSLVersions := [sslvTLSv1, sslvTLSv1_1, sslvTLSv1_2]` (legacy Indy/OpenSSL 1.0.2 path). Line 7 `{$DEFINE USE_TAURUS_TLS}` must be edited in the library source to switch (README 482-492 instructs consumers to edit `src\Server\MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas`). TaurusTLS path (380-384) sets no minimum protocol version explicitly.
- Required: AUTH-14 / OAuth 2.1 §1.5 communication security (TLS per current BCP: TLS 1.2+ only); README itself recommends TaurusTLS for modern ciphers.
- Compat impact: Dropping TLS 1.0/1.1 could break only obsolete clients; all MCP clients are modern. Moving the define to an include file (`MCPServer.inc`) keeps the default (TaurusTLS on) and lets consumers override from their project without editing library sources.
- Suggested change: `SSLVersions := [sslvTLSv1_2]` (+ `sslvTLSv1_3` where the installed Indy supports it) in the OpenSSL-1.0.2 branch; for TaurusTLS set the minimum TLS version option to 1.2. Add `src/MCPServer.inc` containing the `USE_TAURUS_TLS` define and `{$I MCPServer.inc}` in the unit; document `-DMCP_NO_TAURUS` override. Keep `generate-ssl-cert.bat` but note self-signed certs are development-only (already in README).

### GAP-ECO-02 [minor, effort S] README security/auth/Inspector documentation is stale or misleading

- Requirements: AUTH-01, SEC-02, SEC-07
- Current: README.md line 6 badge `MCP-2025--06--18`, line 30 'Implements MCP specification 2025-06-18'; lines 350-353 imply an Authorization header can be 'configured'; line 104 claims listening on localhost (actually all interfaces, GAP-SEC-03); 'Security: Built-in security features including CORS configuration' (line 35) is the only security statement; Inspector section (408-436) predates the web/CLI/TUI split and the per-server `protocolEra` setting (default `legacy`), and does not mention Node 22.19+.
- Required: Documentation prerequisite for AUTH-01 (how to enable auth), SEC-02/SEC-07 (loopback binding, tokens for local HTTP), and for testing modern era with the Inspector.
- Compat impact: None.
- Suggested change: Update badge/feature list to 2026-07-28 dual-era; add 'Security' section (Origin validation, BindAddress, [Auth] modes, no token passthrough, requestState integrity, x-mcp-header PII warning); rewrite 'Testing with MCP Inspector' to show `protocolEra: modern|legacy|auto`, `--cli --method tools/list`, exit code 3 for auth, and add a 'Conformance testing' section with the exact `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance server --url ... --requirements <rev>` commands; state explicitly that 2025-03-26 clients are served unauthenticated only (AUTH-17).

### GAP-SEC-10 [optional, effort S] Optional hardening: no Host-header allow-list (defence in depth against DNS rebinding when Origin is absent)

- Requirements: SEC-01
- Current: Only Origin is inspected (IdHTTPServer.pas 224); `RequestInfo.Host` is never compared with the configured host.
- Required: Not required by the spec (Origin validation is the MUST); the security tutorial lists 'attacker accesses an insecure local server that's left running on localhost via DNS rebinding' as the threat.
- Compat impact: Opt-in setting; default off to avoid breaking reverse-proxy deployments.
- Suggested change: `[Security] AllowedHosts=localhost,127.0.0.1,[::1]` (empty = disabled); when set, reject requests whose `Host` (minus port) is not listed with 421 Misdirected Request or 403.

### Already conformant

- AUTH-02 (stdio SHOULD NOT do OAuth): Server/MCPServer.StdioTransport.pas (whole unit, 43-99) performs no credential handling; MCPServer.dpr RunStdioServer (114-141) passes no settings-derived credentials. Keep it that way: do not wire the new IMCPAuthorizer into TMCPStdioTransport.
- SEC-12 (stdout hygiene in stdio mode): MCPServer.dpr 161-163 sets TLogger.UseStdErr := True before any logging when --stdio is present; StdioTransport.pas 68-69 and 89-93 write only JSON-RPC messages to Output.
- SEC-01 in the default configuration: Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas 227-251 rejects a present Origin that is not in the allow-list with HTTP 403 'Forbidden - Origin not allowed' and Core/MCPServer.Settings.pas 90-91 defaults CORS to enabled with a loopback-only list — i.e. the shipped defaults satisfy the Origin MUST (the gap is only for Enabled=0 / AllowedOrigins=*).
- SEC-06 'MUST NOT use sessions for authentication' (legacy): no server-side session table exists; Managers/MCPServer.CoreManager.pas 97/126 only mints an id and Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas 333-349 merely echoes the header — no authorization decision is ever keyed on Mcp-Session-Id.
- AUTH-07 (no tokens from query string): nothing in src reads `RequestInfo.Params`/`QueryParams` for credentials; the future authorizer reads only the Authorization header.
- SEC-04 / AUTH-12 (no token passthrough today): the core server makes no upstream HTTP calls; src/Http/MCPServer.Http.Executor.pas is a standalone helper not referenced by MCPServer.dpr (uses list 14-35) and takes explicit headers, so no inbound token is forwarded anywhere.
- AUTH-14 transport security available: Server/MCPServer.IdHTTPServer.pas ConfigureSSL (364-408) supports HTTPS with TaurusTLS/OpenSSL 3.x-4.x by default (line 7, 380-384) and validates certificate/key file presence (367-377); README 383-406 and 458-539 document CA-issued certs for production.
- Unknown paths are 404 (IdHTTPServer.pas 187-192) and unknown methods 405 (202-203), limiting the attack surface to the single MCP endpoint (plus the well-known path to be added).
- SEC-13 flexibility: the CORS 403 path already sets `ResponseNo`/`ResponseText` (247-248), so adding the optional id-less JSON-RPC error body is a one-line change.
- Locality defaults: Settings.pas 85-86 default `Port=3000`, `Host=localhost` and settings.ini.example advertise loopback; only the binding (GAP-SEC-03) does not honour it.
- Exception hygiene for tools: Managers/MCPServer.ToolsManager.pas 120-128 converts tool exceptions into `isError` results instead of leaking stack details as protocol errors (JsonRpcProcessor.pas 204-218 still returns E.Message in error.message — acceptable, but redact once auth errors exist).

### Notes

- Ecosystem (server SDKs), from blog/content/posts/2026-07-28-spec-ga/index.md # SDKs: 'All four Tier 1 SDKs speak 2026-07-28 as of today: TypeScript, Python, Go, C#'; 'the Rust SDK supports the new spec in beta'. blog/content/posts/2026-07-27-ruby-sdk-1-0.md: Ruby SDK 1.0 implements 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25 with 100% conformance (Tier 2); 2026-07-28 work under way.
- Ecosystem (dual-era behaviour of official SDKs), from blog/content/posts/2026-06-29-sdk-betas-for-2026-07-28.md: 'clients that speak 2026-07-28 fall back to the initialize handshake when they reach a server on 2025-11-25 or earlier'; Python v2 'answers the legacy initialize handshake alongside server/discover' from one endpoint; TypeScript `createMcpHandler` 'serves 2026-07-28 per request and handles 2025-11-25 traffic from the same endpoint'; Go serves 2026-07-28 only when `StreamableHTTPOptions.Stateless = true` (otherwise negotiates down to 2025-11-25); C# preview defaults its HTTP transport to stateless (conformance known-sdks note: C# serves it at `/stateless`). 'for existing clients and servers nothing breaks today, and nothing breaks on July 28 either'. The Delphi plan's 'one endpoint, both eras' design matches Python/TypeScript behaviour and is what the conformance README expects for non-listed implementations ('the one endpoint you give must serve every claimed revision at its own wire').
- Ecosystem (end-user clients): the spec repo contains NO statement of which end-user clients (Claude Code, Claude Desktop, Cursor, VS Code Copilot, Codex, ChatGPT) ship 2026-07-28 support; docs/extensions/client-matrix.mdx only tracks extensions (MCP Apps, OAuth Client Credentials, EMA). Assume legacy-era clients dominate for the 12-month deprecation window; the README's stated targets (Claude Code over HTTP, Codex over stdio) must keep working via the legacy path. Verify current client behaviour empirically with the Inspector in `legacy`, `auto` and `modern` eras.
- Dual-era normative guidance (docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/versioning.mdx # Backward Compatibility with Initialization-Based Versions): a server 'MAY implement both behaviors'; 'A dual-era server selects its behavior from how the client opens: A request carrying modern per-request _meta is served statelessly ... An initialize request selects legacy semantics, scoped to ... the session (HTTP)'; 'A dual-era server MAY serve both eras concurrently on the same endpoint'. Modern-only servers 'SHOULD name the protocol versions it supports in any error it returns to an initialize request'. HTTP: a request without MCP-Protocol-Version 'MAY' be treated as 2025-03-26 if the server supports pre-2025-06-18 clients. Legacy transport behaviours (GET stream, Mcp-Session-Id, DELETE, Last-Event-ID) 'are not part of this revision' but dual-era servers 'implement the behavior described in the corresponding revision'.
- Authorization in the legacy eras: 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25 already use the RFC 9728 resource-server model (same MUSTs as 2026-07-28; 2025-06-18 makes the WWW-Authenticate header mandatory on 401), so a single auth implementation serves 2025-06-18+ clients. 2025-03-26 used the MCP-server-as-authorization-server model (AUTH-17); recommend documenting it as unsupported for authenticated deployments.
- Authorization changes in 2026-07-28 (changelog.mdx Minor 7-9, Deprecated 4) are all client/AS-side: RFC 9207 `iss` validation (client), `application_type` in DCR (client), issuer-bound credentials (client), DCR deprecated in favour of CIMD (AS/client). Server-side additions: scope hierarchy MUST (AUTH-10), single-challenge scope SHOULD (AUTH-09), offline_access SHOULD NOT (AUTH-11). Nothing in the server role requires CIMD support.
- Official conformance tooling: repository https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/conformance (owned by the Conformance Testing project; docs/community/sdk-tiers.mdx # Conformance Testing). Commands (from its README, fetched 2026-09-03): `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance server --url http://localhost:3000/mcp` (all active scenarios), `--scenario <name>`, `--suite active|all|pending|draft`, `--requirements 2026-07-28` (frozen per-revision set; also `2025-11-25`), `--expected-failures ./conformance-baseline.yml` (YAML with `server:` list; supports `scenario:check-id` granularity; exit 1 on regression or stale baseline), `--verbose`; `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance list [--server] [--requirements 2026-07-28]`; `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance tier-check --repo <owner/repo> --conformance-server-url <url> --requirements 2025-11-25,2026-07-28`; scaffolding `npx @modelcontextprotocol/conformance new-scenario --sep <n>` (seps/2484). GitHub Action: `uses: modelcontextprotocol/conformance@v0.1.11` with `mode: server`, `url:`, `expected-failures:`. Anchor release at 2026-07-28 ship: `@modelcontextprotocol/conformance@0.2.0-alpha.10`. Results land in `results/server-<scenario>-<timestamp>/checks.json`; every scenario also runs `wire-schema-valid` against the JSON schema of the negotiated version.
- Frozen required SERVER scenarios for 2026-07-28 (conformance requirements/2026-07-28.yaml): server-stateless, completion-complete, tools-list, tools-call-simple-text, tools-call-image, tools-call-audio, tools-call-embedded-resource, tools-call-mixed-content, tools-call-error, tools-call-with-progress, server-sse-multiple-streams, resources-list, resources-read-text, resources-read-binary, resources-templates-read, sep-2164-resource-not-found, prompts-list, prompts-get-simple, prompts-get-with-args, prompts-get-embedded-resource, prompts-get-with-image, dns-rebinding-protection, caching, input-required-result-{basic-elicitation, basic-sampling, basic-list-roots, request-state, multiple-input-requests, multi-round, missing-input-response, non-tool-request, result-type, unsupported-methods, tampered-state, capability-check, ignore-extra-params, validate-input}. Not scored: tasks-* (extension). For 2025-11-25 (requirements/2025-11-25.yaml): server-initialize, logging-set-level, ping, completion-complete, tools-list, tools-call-* (simple-text, image, audio, embedded-resource, mixed-content, with-logging, error, with-progress, sampling, elicitation), elicitation-sep1034-defaults, elicitation-sep1330-enums, server-sse-multiple-streams, resources-* (list, read-text, read-binary, templates-read, subscribe, unsubscribe), prompts-* (list, get-simple, get-with-args, get-embedded-resource, get-with-image), dns-rebinding-protection. Note this repo has no prompts or completion support today, so 100% on either set implies implementing those (other areas).
- Conformance coverage of the SERVER auth role: the requirements file says it covers 'an MCP server acting as an OAuth resource server', but neither revision's `server:` list contains any auth/* scenario — all `auth/*` scenarios are client-leg (metadata discovery, CIMD, scope handling, iss validation, etc.). Therefore server-side OAuth behaviour (401 challenge, PRM document, audience validation) is NOT machine-scored today; acceptance for GAP-AUTH-01..07 must rely on DUnitX tests plus a manual/Inspector OAuth run against a test authorization server (e.g. the Inspector CLI: exit code 3 = 'Server requires authentication (401/403, WWW-Authenticate, OAuth)').
- MCP Inspector status (docs/docs/2026-07-28/tools/inspector.mdx, inspector/protocol-eras.mdx, inspector/cli.mdx, inspector/authorization.mdx, inspector/configuration.mdx): single package `@modelcontextprotocol/inspector` with web (`npx @modelcontextprotocol/inspector`), CLI (`--cli`) and TUI (`--tui`); requires Node 22.19.0+; per-server `protocolEra` = `legacy` (default; plain initialize), `auto` (probe server/discover then fall back), `modern` (pin 2026-07-28, no fallback). Modern-era features it exercises: server/discover, per-request `io.modelcontextprotocol/logLevel`, `subscriptions/listen`, MRTR (`input_required`, `requestState` round-trips, `MRTR_MAX_ROUNDS`), x-mcp-header mirroring (CLI/TUI only — the browser SDK skips `Mcp-Param-*`, so a strict server returns -32020 from the web client), the modern error taxonomy (-32020/-32021/-32022, 404/-32601). Full OAuth flow in all three clients (DCR, static client `--client-id/--client-secret`, CIMD `--client-metadata-url`, EMA); handles mid-session 401 re-auth and 403 `insufficient_scope` step-up (scope union); CI flags `--stored-auth-only`, `--use-stored-auth`, `--wait-for-auth`; callback `http://127.0.0.1:6276/oauth/callback` for CLI/TUI. Config file format: `{"mcpServers":{"name":{"type":"http","url":"...","protocolEra":"modern","headers":{...}}}}`. The web backend proxies connections from Node, so the Delphi server sees no browser Origin from the Inspector.
- Delphi implementation facts for the auth work: Indy exposes headers via `TIdHTTPRequestInfo.RawHeaders.Values[...]` (case-insensitive) and the response challenge via `TIdHTTPResponseInfo.WWWAuthenticate` (TStrings) or `CustomHeaders`; Indy parses only Basic credentials into AuthUsername/AuthPassword, so Bearer must be read raw. Each connection runs on its own Indy thread, so a `threadvar` request context is viable; TMCPJsonRpcProcessor is created per server (IdHTTPServer.pas 144) and shared across threads — keep it stateless. RTL provides HMAC-SHA256 (System.Hash.THashSHA2.GetHMAC) and an HTTP client (System.Net.HttpClient) for RFC 7662 introspection; there is no JOSE/JWT in the RTL (JWT signature verification needs a third-party unit or OpenSSL via TaurusTLS) — recommend keeping JWT verification out of the core and shipping it as a sample authorizer. There is no RTL constant-time compare; write one. Verify the RNG behind `CreateGUID`/`TGuid.NewGuid` on Linux before relying on it for session ids; prefer an explicit CSPRNG helper.
- Scope of 'realistically in core' vs 'out of scope' for a Delphi library: IN — pluggable IMCPAuthorizer, static bearer authorizer, WWW-Authenticate challenge builder, RFC 9728 PRM endpoint, audience/expiry checks in an abstract OAuth resource-server base, per-tool `[RequiresScope]` + hierarchy, Origin/Host/bind hardening, requestState HMAC codec, header/body validation, log redaction. OUT — acting as an authorization server (spec: 'implementation details of the authorization server are beyond the scope'), DCR/CIMD (AS/client side), consent pages for confused-deputy proxies (document only), auth extensions (client-credentials/EMA are client+AS features; the server just advertises `extensions` if it adopts them), DPoP.
- Both `dns-rebinding-protection` and the requestState scenarios are scored, so GAP-SEC-01 and GAP-SEC-06 are the two security items with hard conformance acceptance criteria; GAP-SEC-07 (header validation) is tested from the client leg (`http-standard-headers`, `http-custom-headers`, `http-invalid-tool-headers`) and SEP-2243 # Conformance Test Cases lists the server-side edge tables (case-insensitive names, mismatch -> 400, whitespace trimming, base64 sentinel decoding, invalid padding -> 400) to turn into DUnitX cases. Note SEP-2243's tables still cite `-32001`; the published spec renumbered HeaderMismatch to `-32020` (changelog Minor 12) — implement -32020.
- Open questions for the orchestrator: (1) whether to keep `[CORS] AllowedOrigins=*` semantics or deprecate it; (2) whether the default binary should bind 127.0.0.1 only (spec SHOULD) given Linux/container users who expect 0.0.0.0 — proposal: default loopback + explicit `BindAddress=0.0.0.0` opt-in; (3) whether `logs://recent` and `server://status` example resources stay registered by default; (4) StateKey persistence strategy for load-balanced deployments (config-provided key vs per-process); (5) CI strategy given no Delphi on GitHub-hosted runners (self-hosted Windows runner running build.bat + tests + conformance vs. manual release checklist).
- Repository facts relevant to planning: last spec-repo commit 2026-09-02 (1dc2c72); Delphi repo has no tests, no .github, Windows-only build.bat pinned to Studio 37.0 with TaurusTLS auto-discovery; src/Http/* client helpers exist but are not part of the dpr; README badge still says 2025-06-18.

### Requirements

- **AUTH-01** (SHOULD, both): "Authorization is **OPTIONAL** for MCP implementations. When supported: Implementations using an HTTP-based transport **SHOULD** conform to this specification." Consequence: a server may ship without OAuth, but every auth feature it does ship must follow the MUSTs below.  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Protocol Requirements (identical wording in 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18 and 2025-03-26 basic/authorization.mdx)_
- **AUTH-02** (SHOULD NOT, both): "Implementations using an STDIO transport **SHOULD NOT** follow this specification, and instead retrieve credentials from the environment." The stdio transport must not require/validate bearer tokens or emit OAuth challenges.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Protocol Requirements_
- **AUTH-03** (MUST, both): "MCP servers **MUST** implement OAuth 2.0 Protected Resource Metadata (RFC9728)." and "The Protected Resource Metadata document returned by the MCP server **MUST** include the `authorization_servers` field containing at least one authorization server." (Applies only when the server implements authorization, per AUTH-01.)  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Overview (item 4) and authorization-server-discovery.mdx # Authorization Server Location; same MUST in 2025-11-25 and 2025-06-18 basic/authorization.mdx_
- **AUTH-04** (MUST, both): Modern: "MCP servers **MUST** implement one of the following discovery mechanisms ... 1. **WWW-Authenticate Header**: Include the resource metadata URL in the `WWW-Authenticate` HTTP header under `resource_metadata` when returning `401 Unauthorized` responses ... 2. **Well-Known URI**: Serve metadata at a well-known URI ... `https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/public/mcp` ... or at the root: `https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`". Legacy 2025-06-18: "MCP servers **MUST** use the HTTP header `WWW-Authenticate` when returning a _401 Unauthorized_ to indicate the location of the resource server metadata URL" (header is mandatory, not optional). A dual-era server should therefore always emit the WWW-Authenticate header AND serve the well-known URI.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/authorization-server-discovery.mdx # Protected Resource Metadata Discovery Requirements; legacy stricter form in docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/authorization.mdx line 87_
- **AUTH-05** (SHOULD, both): "MCP servers **SHOULD** include a `scope` parameter in the `WWW-Authenticate` header as defined in RFC 6750 Section 3 to indicate the scopes required for accessing the resource." Example: `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="https://mcp.example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource", scope="files:read"`. Also: "Servers **SHOULD** strive for consistency in how they construct scope sets".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Scope Selection Strategy_
- **AUTH-06** (MUST, both): "MCP servers, acting in their role as an OAuth 2.1 resource server, **MUST** validate access tokens as described in OAuth 2.1 Section 5.2. MCP servers **MUST** validate that access tokens were issued specifically for them as the intended audience, according to RFC 8707 Section 2. If validation fails, servers **MUST** respond according to OAuth 2.1 Section 5.3 error handling requirements. Invalid or expired tokens **MUST** receive a HTTP 401 response." ... "MCP servers **MUST** only accept tokens that are valid for use with their own resources. MCP servers **MUST NOT** accept or transit any other tokens."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Access Token Usage > Token Handling (identical in 2025-11-25 and 2025-06-18 # Token Handling)_
- **AUTH-07** (MUST NOT, both): Client-side rule with a server-side consequence: "MCP client **MUST** use the Authorization request header field ... `Authorization: Bearer <access-token>` ... authorization **MUST** be included in every HTTP request from client to server. 2. Access tokens **MUST NOT** be included in the URI query string." A server must read bearer tokens only from the `Authorization` header (never from query parameters) and must re-validate on every request (no per-session caching of the auth decision).  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Token Requirements_
- **AUTH-08** (MUST, both): "Servers **MUST** return appropriate HTTP status codes for authorization errors: 401 Unauthorized - Authorization required or token invalid; 403 Forbidden - Invalid scopes or insufficient permissions; 400 Bad Request - Malformed authorization request".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Error Handling (same table in 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18, 2025-03-26)_
- **AUTH-09** (SHOULD, both): "When a client makes a request with an access token with insufficient scope during runtime operations, the server **SHOULD** respond with: `HTTP 403 Forbidden` ... `WWW-Authenticate` header with the `Bearer` scheme and additional parameters: `error="insufficient_scope"` ... `scope="required_scope1 required_scope2"` ... `resource_metadata` ... `error_description` (optional)". Also: "servers **SHOULD** include all scopes required for the current operation in a single challenge" and "Servers **SHOULD** be consistent in their scope inclusion strategy".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Scope Challenge Handling > Runtime Insufficient Scope Errors_
- **AUTH-10** (MUST, modern): "Servers **MUST** account for scope hierarchies, where a broader scope implies narrower ones, when deciding whether a token is sufficient for an operation."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Step-Up Authorization Flow (SEP-2350 scope accumulation, listed in the 2026-06-29 SDK-betas post as a 2026-07-28 hardening change)_
- **AUTH-11** (SHOULD NOT, modern): "**MCP Servers** (Protected Resources) **SHOULD NOT** include `offline_access` in `WWW-Authenticate` scope or Protected Resource Metadata `scopes_supported`, as refresh tokens are not a resource requirement."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # Refresh Tokens (SEP-2207)_
- **AUTH-12** (MUST, both): "MCP servers **MUST** validate that tokens presented to them were specifically issued for their use" ... "MCP servers **MUST** validate access tokens before processing the request, ensuring the access token is issued specifically for the MCP server, and take all necessary steps to ensure no data is returned to unauthorized parties. A MCP server **MUST** follow the guidelines in OAuth 2.1 - Section 5.2 to validate inbound tokens. MCP servers **MUST** only accept tokens specifically intended for themselves and **MUST** reject tokens that do not include them in the audience claim or otherwise verify that they are the intended recipient of the token." ... "If the MCP server makes requests to upstream APIs, it may act as an OAuth client to them. The access token used at the upstream API is a separate token, issued by the upstream authorization server. The MCP server **MUST NOT** pass through the token it received from the MCP client."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx # Token Audience Binding and Validation; # Access Token Privilege Restriction_
- **AUTH-13** (MUST, both): "Attackers who obtain tokens stored by the client, or tokens cached or logged on the server can access protected resources ... Clients and servers **MUST** implement secure token storage and follow OAuth best practices, as outlined in OAuth 2.1, Section 7.1." Plus: "implementors **MUST** follow OAuth 2.1 security best practices as outlined in OAuth 2.1 Section 7". Server-side consequence: never log or persist bearer tokens; never expose them via resources.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx # Token Theft; intro paragraph_
- **AUTH-14** (MUST, both): "Implementations **MUST** follow OAuth 2.1 Section 1.5 'Communication Security'. Specifically: 1. All authorization server endpoints **MUST** be served over HTTPS. 1. All redirect URIs **MUST** be either `localhost` or use HTTPS." Server-side consequence: `authorization_servers` entries in the PRM document and the `resource_metadata` URL must be https (or loopback for development); TLS on the MCP endpoint itself is expected for any non-loopback deployment.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx # Communication Security_
- **AUTH-15** (MUST, both): "MCP proxy servers using static client IDs **MUST** obtain user consent for each dynamically registered client before forwarding to third-party authorization servers" and (tutorial) "To prevent confused deputy attacks, MCP proxy servers **MUST** implement ..." a consent flow with signed/session-stored `state`. Applies only to servers that proxy to a third-party API using a static OAuth client id (not the case for this repo's core; relevant to library consumers who build such proxies).  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/security-considerations.mdx # Confused Deputy Problem; docs/docs/2026-07-28/tutorials/security/security_best_practices.mdx # Confused Deputy Problem > Mitigation_
- **AUTH-16** (MAY, modern): Authorization extensions (`io.modelcontextprotocol/oauth-client-credentials`, `io.modelcontextprotocol/enterprise-managed-authorization`) are "**Optional** ... **Additive** ... **Composable** ... **Versioned independently**"; servers advertise them in the `extensions` field of the `server/discover` response. Not required for conformance (SEP-2133: "Extension support is not required for 100% protocol conformance").  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization/index.mdx # MCP Authorization Extensions; docs/extensions/overview.mdx # Negotiation; docs/extensions/client-matrix.mdx_
- **AUTH-17** (SHOULD, legacy): 2025-03-26 only (pre-RFC 9728 model where the MCP server itself hosts the authorization server): "MCP servers **SHOULD** and MCP clients **MUST** implement OAuth 2.0 Authorization Server Metadata ... MCP servers that do not support Authorization Server Metadata **MUST** follow the default URI schema" (`/authorize`, `/token`, `/register` relative to the MCP server origin), and "When authorization is required and not yet proven by the client, servers **MUST** respond with HTTP 401". A dual-era server that enables auth cannot satisfy 2025-03-26 clients without becoming an authorization server; the plan should declare 2025-03-26 authorization out of scope (2025-03-26 clients supported unauthenticated only).  
  _Source: /home/user/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/docs/specification/2025-03-26/basic/authorization.mdx lines 45-47, 76, 111-113, 143-160_
- **SEC-01** (MUST, both): "Servers **MUST** validate the `Origin` header on all incoming connections to prevent DNS rebinding attacks. - If the `Origin` header is present and invalid, servers **MUST** respond with HTTP 403 Forbidden. The HTTP response body **MAY** comprise a JSON-RPC _error response_ that has no `id`." (2025-06-18 has the first sentence only; 403 clause added in 2025-11-25.) Must hold regardless of whether CORS response headers are enabled. Scored by conformance scenario `dns-rebinding-protection` in both the 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28 required server sets.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx # Security & Endpoint; docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx # Security Warning; docs/specification/2025-06-18/basic/transports.mdx # Security Warning_
- **SEC-02** (SHOULD, both): "When running locally, servers **SHOULD** bind only to localhost (127.0.0.1) rather than all network interfaces (0.0.0.0)."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx # Security & Endpoint (same in 2025-11-25 / 2025-06-18 Security Warning)_
- **SEC-03** (SHOULD, both): "Servers **SHOULD** implement proper authentication for all connections. Without these protections, attackers could use DNS rebinding to interact with local MCP servers from remote websites."  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx # Security & Endpoint (same in legacy revisions)_
- **SEC-04** (MUST NOT, both): "MCP servers **MUST NOT** accept any tokens that were not explicitly issued for the MCP server." ("Token passthrough is explicitly forbidden in the authorization specification".)  
  _Source: docs/docs/2026-07-28/tutorials/security/security_best_practices.mdx # Token Passthrough > Mitigation_
- **SEC-05** (MUST, modern): "MCP servers that implement authorization **MUST** verify all inbound requests. MCP servers **MUST NOT** treat possession of a state handle as authentication. MCP servers **SHOULD** use secure, non-deterministic handles generated with secure random number generators ... MCP servers **SHOULD** bind handles server-side to the authenticated user, for example by keying stored state as `<user_id>:<handle>` where the user ID is derived from the verified token rather than supplied by the client, and reject a handle presented by any other principal." (Modern era replaces protocol sessions with server-minted handles passed as tool arguments.)  
  _Source: docs/docs/2026-07-28/tutorials/security/security_best_practices.mdx # State Handle Hijacking > Mitigation_
- **SEC-06** (MUST, legacy): "MCP servers that implement authorization **MUST** verify all inbound requests. MCP Servers **MUST NOT** use sessions for authentication. MCP servers **MUST** use secure, non-deterministic session IDs. Generated session IDs (e.g., UUIDs) **SHOULD** use secure random number generators ... MCP servers **SHOULD** bind session IDs to user-specific information." Transport: "The session ID **SHOULD** be globally unique and cryptographically secure (e.g., a securely generated UUID, a JWT, or a cryptographic hash) ... The session ID **MUST** only contain visible ASCII characters (ranging from 0x21 to 0x7E)". Applies while the server serves legacy (`initialize`-based) clients with `Mcp-Session-Id`.  
  _Source: docs/docs/2025-11-25/tutorials/security/security_best_practices.mdx # Session Hijacking (lines 97-109); docs/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports.mdx # Session Management (lines 201-214)_
- **SEC-07** (SHOULD, both): "MCP servers intending for their servers to be run locally **SHOULD** implement measures to prevent unauthorized usage from malicious processes: - Use the `stdio` transport to limit access to just the MCP client - Restrict access if using an HTTP transport, such as: - Require an authorization token - Use unix domain sockets or other Interprocess Communication (IPC) mechanisms with restricted access". Attack listed: "An attacker accesses an insecure local server that's left running on localhost via DNS rebinding".  
  _Source: docs/docs/2026-07-28/tutorials/security/security_best_practices.mdx # Local MCP Server Compromise > Mitigation (final list)_
- **SEC-08** (MUST, modern): "If a client request contains a `requestState` field, servers **MUST** treat `requestState` as an attacker-controlled input. If `requestState` influences authorization, resource access, or business logic, servers **MUST** protect its integrity (e.g. HMAC or AEAD) and **MUST** reject state that fails verification. Integrity protection **MAY** be omitted only when tampering can cause nothing worse than request failure." "To prevent replay, servers **SHOULD** include the following inside the integrity-protected `requestState` payload and verify each on receipt: the authenticated principal ...; a short expiry (TTL) ...; an identifier for the originating request". "Servers for which a given `requestState` must be consumed at most once ... **MUST** enforce that invariant server-side." "Servers **MUST** validate request state as described". Scored by required 2026-07-28 server scenario `input-required-result-tampered-state`.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/mrtr.mdx # Server Requirements (Basic Workflow) items on requestState; # Security Considerations_
- **SEC-09** (MUST, modern): "Servers **MUST** reject requests with a recognized `Mcp-Param-{Name}` header that contains invalid characters". "Any server that processes the message body **MUST** validate that encoded header values, after decoding if Base64-encoded, match the corresponding values in the request body. Servers **MUST** reject requests with a `400 Bad Request` HTTP status and JSON-RPC error code `-32020` (`HeaderMismatch`) if any validation fails." "Servers that process the request body **MUST** reject requests where the values specified in the headers do not match the corresponding values in the request body. This prevents potential security vulnerabilities when different components in the network rely on different sources of truth". Table: value `null` or absent -> "Server MUST NOT expect the header"; header omitted but value in body -> "Server MUST reject". "servers **MUST** decode an encoded `Mcp-Name` or `Mcp-Param-{Name}` value before comparing". "Clients and servers **MUST** use case-insensitive comparisons for header names. Header _values_ ... are case-sensitive." Failure conditions include a missing required standard header (`MCP-Protocol-Version`, `Mcp-Method`, `Mcp-Name`).  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx # Server Behavior for Custom Headers; # Server Validation; # Value Encoding; # Case Sensitivity (overlaps the transport area; listed here because the spec frames it as an injection/source-of-truth defence)_
- **SEC-10** (SHOULD NOT, modern): "Server developers **SHOULD NOT** mark sensitive parameters (passwords, API keys, tokens, PII) with `x-mcp-header`, as header values are visible to network intermediaries." Plus the constraint list for `x-mcp-header` values (MUST NOT be empty; MUST match RFC 9110 token syntax; MUST NOT contain CR/LF; MUST be case-insensitively unique; MUST only be on integer/string/boolean; MUST be statically reachable via `properties` only) which a server must enforce at tool-registration time because "An `x-mcp-header` annotation anywhere else makes the annotation — and thus the tool definition — invalid".  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools.mdx # x-mcp-header (Warning)_
- **SEC-11** (MUST NOT, modern): "New codes **MUST NOT** be allocated in this sub-range [-32000..-32019], and new implementations **SHOULD NOT** use codes from this sub-range at all" ... "[-32020 to -32099] Implementations **MUST NOT** emit any code from this sub-range that is not defined by this specification and **MUST** use defined codes only with their specified meanings" (`-32020` HeaderMismatch, `-32021` MissingRequiredClientCapability, `-32022` UnsupportedProtocolVersion). "Implementations of this protocol version **MUST NOT** emit these codes: `-32002` ... `-32042`". Relevant when adding auth/header/state error paths.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/index.mdx # Error Codes_
- **SEC-12** (MUST, both): "The server **MUST NOT** write anything to its `stdout` that is not a valid MCP message." and "The server **MAY** write UTF-8 strings to `stderr` for any logging purposes". Security-relevant because credential/diagnostic logging must never reach stdout in stdio mode.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/stdio.mdx (top list); 2025-11-25/2025-06-18 basic/transports.mdx # stdio_
- **SEC-13** (MAY, both): "The HTTP response body **MAY** comprise a JSON-RPC _error response_ that has no `id`." Optional: return `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","error":{"code":-32600,"message":"Origin not allowed"}}` with the 403 so clients can surface a diagnostic.  
  _Source: docs/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http.mdx # Security & Endpoint (the 403 body clause)_
